If that proves hard, the engine may instead mark the shaft's jumps as route-limited and cover them with handwritten replays (then record it).
- **Fallback** (engine's call, record it if taken): a per-level dead zone (`camera_deadzone = [112, 136]`) with the existing follow, plus the death line and the activation rule. It bobs, but every design rule still holds.

## D-055 Climbing without ladders: jump-through planks and the shaft rule (game-designer, M3 planning)
- **No ladders.** Climbing is jumping: stairs and ledges (solid), and one-way wooden planks (`steig`) that Resi jumps up through from below, the classic SMB/SMW way up. Reasons: ladders need a new player state (grab, climb, UP/DOWN input, leaving the ladder, enemies and projectiles on ladders) with new animations in three power states, and they would make UP an action in one stage only. The real Falkensteinwand path is steep stairs and bolted wooden steps, not ladders, so planks are also the truthful picture.
- A missed jump through a plank lands on the plank or step it started from (missed jumps never fall more than 4 tiles onto safe ground).
- **Shaft rule**: a vertical section is at most **9 columns** wide inside its walls, entered at its bottom left and left at its top right, with nothing reachable outside its columns before the exit. Then the camera, which never scrolls left, can never strand a plank behind the screen's left edge (the rightmost possible centre puts the camera's left edge left of the shaft's first column). Planks overlap their neighbours by one column so walking enemies zigzag down plank by plank.

## D-056 The Hackl power-up (game-designer, M3 planning)
- **Obtained** from an `H`-Oblatenkiste (`contains = "hackl"`, boxes only). The item rises out of the box and stays put (no rolling, like SMB's fire flower). It turns **Normal or Zuckerschock** into **Hackl**; as Hackl it's 1000 points only. One per stage where it appears (Falkenstein: at the Hackelwurfkapelle, where the legend says Wolfgang threw his axe).
- **Hackl Resi is big** (12×30 hitbox, `player_hackl` 16×32, a green loden jacket over the dirndl), breaks bricks, and survives a hit by dropping to **Zuckerschock** (30-frame freeze, 120 i-frames). This is gentler than SMB1 (where fire → small) on purpose for medium difficulty. Every power change uses the same 30-frame freeze with alternating sprites. Death → Normal. **Carries over** into later stages like Zuckerschock (D-042); intro and route map draw `player_hackl`.
- **Throw = a new press of RUN** (SMB: B runs and fires). Holding RUN keeps running and throws only on the press. Max **2** axes alive; a press with 2 alive does nothing. No throws while frozen, crawling, dying or in the goal sequence. JUMP stays jump, START pause, DOWN drop-through / crawl.
- **Arc**: fixed and independent of Resi's speed: vx ±3.0 (faster than her run, so she never runs into it), vy −3.0, gravity 0.1875 px/frame², max fall 4.0. That is 1.5 tiles up and back at hand height after 6 tiles, ~7 tiles on flat ground. It sticks in solid tiles (20 frames, harmless), passes one-way tiles, vanishes in water/hazards and off screen.
- **Targets**: one per axe. Every stompable enemy is defeated for its base value (100/200, outside the stomp chain). The Fiaker is immune (it bounces off). Swarms and projectiles are passed through. A falling rock is shattered (50). Blocks and items are not affected (breaking bricks with the axe and cutting things are backlog).
- **Not taken**: a separate control for throwing (the game has four buttons and RUN is the SMB convention); a homing or straight axe (an arc is the legend's long throw and makes the axe skill-based); the axe also breaking bricks (it would trivialise box puzzles in every later stage).

## D-057 The Durchkriechstein: an optional scripted crawl (game-designer, M3 planning)
- D-011/D-017 "no ducking, DOWN only for drop-through" stays for movement. The one exception is that **DOWN at the mouth of a Durchkriechstein** (grounded on its floor, facing right, within 4 px of the mouth, JUMP not held, stone unused) starts a **scripted crawl**. Input is ignored while she moves right at 1 px/frame through the 1-tile-high tunnel (hitbox 12×12) and stands up at the exit. She can't be hurt, the timer runs, and she can't turn back (the custom: through in silence, without looking back).
- **Outcome**: Normal → +1 life (1000 points at the lives cap); Zuckerschock or Hackl → shed to Normal with the popup „Ballast weg!" (the legend: only the sinless fit, then even a large person does). Once per level attempt (a death resets it like a box), so lives can't be farmed: dying costs the life the stone gives.
- **Never a softlock**: the crawl is optional and always beside a required route over the boulder (≤ 3-tile step-up); walking into the mouth without DOWN just stops Resi at the boulder's face like a wall; the crawl is automatic, so any size fits; the exit has ≥ 2 rows of headroom and no enemy can reach it; `levels lint` checks the geometry (tunnel cells empty with solid above and below, the mouth's floor at the tunnel floor's height, 2 empty rows at the exit).
- **Not taken**: a 1-tile passage only Normal Resi fits through (her hitbox is 22 px, so it would need real ducking, and a big Resi would be blocked, which the custom contradicts); triggering by walking into the mouth (a big Resi would lose her power by accident on the main path); a forced crawl on the required route (it would punish carrying a power-up).
- **Fallback** (engine): if a visible crawl is costly, Resi may vanish at the mouth and reappear at the exit after the same number of frames (she's "inside the stone"); the `crawl` animations are then unused.

## D-058 Rules for the M3 enemies and hazards (game-designer, M3 planning)
- **Pilger**: like the Selfie-Tourist but without selfie stops: walks 0.5 towards the player, turns at walls and enemies on foot, **walks off ledges** (the climb's threat comes down the mountain). Groups are 2–3 pilgrims placed one cell apart; `lead = true` only swaps in the pennant-carrying sprite. 100 points.
- **Gams**: a ledge-bound hopper. Stand 60, crouch 16 (the telegraph), hop 32 frames (vx ±1.0, vy −4.0, gravity 0.25: 2 tiles up, 2 tiles across). At each crouch it faces the player and checks its landing cell: if there is no floor there at its height, or a wall is in the way, it turns; if both sides are blocked it hops in place. It never leaves its ledge. Stompable, 200 (chain start 200, D-046), knocked off by a bumped block.
- **Steinschlag**: a spawner in a cliff niche. Its counter runs only while the player is within `range` tiles (default 6) **and** the niche is fully on screen below the HUD, so every first rock of an approach shows the whole telegraph: 40 frames of wobble plus pebbles trickling down the column. Then a rock falls (gravity 0.25, max 4.0), shatters on the first solid or one-way tile, hurts on any contact (can't be stomped), passes through enemies and items. `period` 90–300 (default 150), `phase`. Level rules: niche bottom 4–6 tiles above the ground it hits; the stage's first rock drops onto a net; no rock column over the landing of a required jump that already has another hazard.
- **Rockfall net** (`netz`): an ordinary one-way tile. Rocks shatter on one-way tiles anyway, so a net 3 tiles above a path is a shelter and a platform with no new collision kind.
- All patterns are frame counters from activation or from entering range, never the RNG (D-017). The thrown Hackl's interactions with every enemy kind are in D-056.

## D-059 Implementing D-054/D-055: climb camera, factor_y, climb lints and solver (engine, M3 task E14)
- **Format**: `[meta] camera = "side|climb"` (`level::CameraMode`, default `side`); background entries take `factor_y` (0..=32, default 0). Tiles: `kalkfels` solid, `steig`, `steig_wall` (the plank over the back wall, pixel-artist addition; like `balcony` over `wall_village_bg`) and `netz` one-way, `kalkfels_bg` decorative air. The climb lints and the solver see only collision kinds, so `steig_wall` counts exactly like `steig`. `PROP_SPRITES` lists the seven M3 props (the catalog test now also reads the M3 "Props" table). Missing art is fine: tiles without a `tile_<name>` sprite are grey-boxed, props without a sprite draw the cell outline.
- **Camera** (`camera.rs`): `Camera::follow_climb(&mut ClimbFocus, ..)` exactly as D-054's table; "grounded" is `Player::on_ground` (tiles, one-way tiles, moving platforms). The fall-follow limit only stops the camera going further down, it never pulls it up. `Game` keeps `ground_feet` (whole px; in the state hash, 0 in side levels). Start, respawn and stage changes use `Camera::new_climb` (feet at `climb_feet_y` at once). Goal sequence: `Camera::settle_y` towards feet − `climb_goal_feet_y` during Slide/Hop/Walk; side levels keep `follow_y` (D-040). The Durchkriechstein crawl (E15) counts as grounded as long as it keeps `on_ground` set.
- **Death line**: checked after the frame's camera follow, only while `Playing`: `player.y > camera.y + 180` in subpixels (so the dying hitbox top's whole px can still read 180 below the camera), `DeathCause::Pit`. Measured on `00_climb_test`: walking into the cleft dies at frame 151 with the hitbox top at 496 px (level bottom 640); the unit test `a_cleft_kills_long_before_the_level_bottom` bounds such a death at ≤ 45 frames after the edge.
- **Activation**: in climb levels a level entity activates only when, in addition to the horizontal rule, its spawn row is within `climb_activate_y` (64) px of the screen (`Camera::row_near`, touching counts); entities without a spawn use their hitbox's top row. Helper for entity code: `game::spawn_row_near(level, entity, camera, margin)`. Once active, an entity stays active (as before).
- **Tuning** `[camera]`: `climb_feet_y` 148, `climb_settle_speed` 2, `climb_top` 40, `climb_fall_follow` 80, `climb_goal_feet_y` 164, `climb_activate_y` 64; `Tuning::parse` checks 0 ≤ top < feet_y ≤ 180, goal 1..=180, speed 1..=16, follow and activation 0..=180. The tuning is part of the static hash, so the testbed test's pinned `FINISH_HASH` changed; no replay and no golden changed (all 174 M1/M2 replays and 53 goldens pass unchanged).
- **Solver** (D-054 as specified, plus one **addendum**): ranking by x + height gained (subpixels, `solver::search_progress`), frame budget 3× (width + height) px in climb levels, recovery also needs the feet ≤ 8 px lower than the original at the checkpoint. Addendum: in climb levels the re-planning deadline is the checkpoint + **60** frames instead of + 30. Reason: on `00_climb_test` the solver's route jumps from the shaft floor through the first plank straight onto the second; a press shifted by 1–2 frames lands on the first plank, which a player fixes with one more jump (~40 frames), but with + 30 the recovery couldn't catch up and the window measured 2 frames. With + 60 the narrowest window is 23 frames (the cleft jump, bounded by a death), while deaths still fail a shift. The D-054 fallback (route-limited shaft jumps + handwritten replays) was not needed.
- **Addendum (engine, M3 task E17): the height goal is measured on the ground.** Bug found by the level designer on `04_falkenstein`: the checkpoint (landing + 120) is often in the air of the route's next jump, and the height check took the feet *there*, so the goal asked for "standing with the feet ≤ 8 px below a point in the air". On flat ground no shifted run reaches that, so every jump followed by another within 2 s measured a 0..0 window. Now (`Trace::goal_feet`): if the route stands on the ground at the checkpoint, its feet; otherwise the **lower** (larger y) of the last ground before the checkpoint and the next ground after it (only one exists: that one; none: the feet as before). The proposed "last grounded feet" alone was rejected: a checkpoint during a fall (walking off a ledge, e.g. the 6-tile drop) would demand the ledge's height from a shifted run that is already further, down on the floor. The lower of the two keeps D-054's intent — a run that fell to the plank below the one the route jumped from still fails — and never asks for more than the route stood on around the checkpoint. Side levels have no height goal and are unaffected. `00_climb_test`: three of its seven checkpoints are in the air (goal feet 464 → 480, 285 → 336, 233 → 288 px), but every window is unchanged (narrowest 23, frame 73), because a higher surface was reachable there anyway. Tests: `a_climb_checkpoint_in_the_air_uses_the_ground_around_it` (flat climb fixture, second jump 110 frames after the first's landing: window 0..0 before, −12..12 now), `goal_feet_is_the_lower_ground_around_an_airborne_frame`.
- **Addendum (engine, M3 task E19): the climb goal camera moves one way only.** QA on `falkenstein_tour` (frames ~1705–1709): the touch was framed at feet 148, the camera settled up 2 px/frame towards feet − 164 while she slid down 2 px/frame, so it went 8 px up and reversed, then followed the 5 px hop at the pole base. Now the target is fixed at the touch: the **final** framing, pole base (`StageClear::base_y`, the walk ground line) at screen y `climb_goal_feet_y` (164). `Camera::settle_y_towards(target, floor, ..)` settles towards it at ≤ `climb_settle_speed`, and while moving down never stays above `feet − 164` (the floor), so a slide faster than the settle can't drop her out of view. Her feet never go below the base, so the floor is ≤ the target: y is monotonic, never overshoots, and the hop (feet higher) is ignored. Final framing unchanged (feet at 164); side levels keep `follow_y` untouched (every side replay and golden bit-identical). Tests: `the_climb_goal_camera_moves_one_way_and_ignores_the_hop` (flat climb fixture, touches at seven heights: one way, ≤ 2 px/frame, feet never below 164; failed before with the up-then-down track), `the_goal_sequence_settles_the_feet_at_164` now asserts down-only, `settle_y_towards_is_monotonic_and_keeps_up_with_the_floor`. Replays and goldens: none changed (no recorded goal sequence in a climb level on this branch; `falkenstein_tour` lives on the level branch and its frames after the touch will shift).
- **Lints** (`xtask/src/climblint.rs`, part of `levels lint`): fall depth in climb levels (a "walkable" surface is a solid/one-way tile with 2 empty cells above; walking off it into a neighbouring column with that headroom must drop ≤ 6 or ≥ 10 tiles, or be bottomless, or end in a lethal tile); `steinschlag` niche 4–6 tiles above its ground (measured to the first solid tile since D-062) and the `durchkriechstein` tunnel/mouth/exit geometry in every level. Both find their spawns by legend name and read `EntitySpawn::{col,row,len}`, so they work as soon as E15/E16 add the kinds (the Durchkriechstein lint assumes E15 stores the tunnel length in `EntitySpawn::len`). Shaft width is a warning only (printed, never failing); a shaft is detected as a run of non-solid cells between the same two solid walls for ≥ 6 rows with a one-way tile inside, so shafts with jagged walls are not checked.
- **Test level** `00_climb_test` (grey box, 64 × 40, `camera = "climb"`, `min_jump_window = 5`): start terrace, a bottomless 3-wide cleft, a 6-tile drop, a 9-column shaft with four planks 3 tiles apart (two `steig`, two `steig_wall`), a plateau with the goal. Replays: `climb_test_solver`, `climb_test_cleft_death` (dies at the death line, top 496), `climb_test_drop_6` (lands on screen), `climb_test_shaft_miss` (a short hop under the next plank lands back on the plank it started from).
- **Art-review add-on** (Falkenstein review): one-way autotile joins (`jnr-render` `tiles::one_way_join`, resolved per level into `TileLooks::joins`): `kalkfels_bg` counts a `steig_wall` neighbour as the same on all four sides (the back wall runs on behind the plank), while `steig_wall` still sees only itself (it keeps its end brackets); `kalkfels` counts `forest_path`, `meadow`, `stone` or `ground` above it as the same for the top bit only (no open-top lip and grass under dirt). The joins are not symmetric, so they are a table of pairs, not families. New decorative tile `netz_post` (`TileKind::Empty`, art `tile_netz_post`). The request said "like `jetty_post`", but `jetty_post` is solid; `netz_post` is Empty as requested (decoration under a net, never collides).

## D-060 Implementing D-056/D-057: Hackl power state, thrown axes, Durchkriechstein (engine, M3 task E15)
- **Power** is `Power::{Normal, Big, Hackl}`; `is_big()` (tall hitbox, breaks bricks), `after_hit()` (Hackl → Big → Normal → death). `Player::set_power` records `prev_power`, and the renderer alternates `prev_power`/`power` looks every 4 frames of the 30-frame freeze for every change, also Big ↔ Hackl (same height). Carry-over into the next stage copies any big state without the freeze. Replays can check `power=normal|big|hackl`.
- **Hackl item** (`contains = "hackl"`, `EntityKind::Hackl`): rises like the other power-ups (`items.rise_*`) and then stays, no gravity, no hop on a bumped block. Normal/Big → Hackl (`Event::PowerUp`, popups „1000" and „Wirf, Resi, wirf!" via `entity::stacked_popups`: stacked without overlap above the item and above her 32 px sprite top); as Hackl only the 1000 points (`score.power_up`).
- **Throw**: `Player::run_held` is tracked at the end of every `step` in every mode (like `jump_held`), so RUN held across screens, the freeze or the crawl is no press. A press while Hackl and not crawling, in play past the freeze, throws if fewer than `hackl.max_alive` axes exist (`Event::HacklThrown`, `Player::throw_pose` = 10). The axe (`EntityKind::ThrownHackl`, 8×8) spawns centred 8 px ahead / 10 px below the hitbox top, then moves the same frame: X then Y with `move_y(.., drop_through = true)` (one-way passes), gravity after the move. Numbers: apex 25.5 px after 16 moves, back at hand height after 33 moves and 99 px. Thrown against a wall it is stuck at once. Off screen = the hitbox no longer overlaps the camera rectangle (checked by the game, which knows the camera).
- **Axe hits** (`Game::axe_hits`, after all entities moved, before the player's contacts): each flying axe takes the first entity in list order it overlaps whose `Entity::axe_hit()` is not `Pass`; the axe is removed, `Event::AxeHit` fires, and **one path** applies the effect: `Game::defeat_by_projectile` → `enemies::hit_by_projectile`, which acts on the `axe_hit()` class (every `Defeat` enemy knocked off, tumbling, for `stomp_chain[chain_start]` outside the chain; the `Shatter` Stein broken for `enemies.stein_points` with debris; `Bounce`, the Fiaker, nothing). The classification (`EnemyKind::axe_hit`, `Entity::axe_hit` in `hackl.rs`) is an exhaustive match on purpose, so every new kind must be classified. A harmless enemy (crashed E-Biker) lets the axe pass.
- **Durchkriechstein**: legend `{ entity = "durchkriechstein", len = 2..=6 }` (default 4) in the tunnel's first (leftmost) cell; `EntityKind::Durchkriechstein` with the tunnel as hitbox, `state` `crawl::stone::{UNUSED, USED}` (rebuilt on every level reset, so a death resets it). Start rule `crawl::starts`; the crawl snaps the 12×12 hitbox flush to the mouth (so every crawl of a `len` takes exactly `len × 16 + 12` frames) and moves it scripted without tile collision (the lint guarantees an empty tunnel); the player's own movement, throws and all contacts with entities are skipped (can't be hurt, can't collect), enemies, timer, checkpoints and camera run. `on_ground` stays true while crawling. Outcome at the exit as specified; the cap case uses `crawl.cap_points`. `crawl::geometry_problems(level, spawn)` is the one lint rule for it; `levels lint` (`xtask/src/climblint.rs`) calls it for every `SpawnKind::Durchkriechstein`. Dying mid-crawl (time-up) keeps the crawl state until the respawn (a normal standing player, the stone unused again); the renderer hides her while she dies in the tunnel.
- **Fallback art** (until the pixel-artist delivers, all automatic): no `player_hackl` → `player_big` with `r` swapped to `G` (`scene::HACKL_FALLBACK_SWAP`); no `throw` anim → the movement anim; no `crawl` anim → Resi hidden while crawling (D-057's fallback); no `hackl`/`hackl_thrown` → grey boxes (`G` / `i`). No placeholder `.sprite` files. The throw pose picks `throw` frame 0 for its first 4 frames (`scene::THROW_WINDUP_FRAMES`), then frame 1.
- **Deviations**: the item's points are `score.power_up`; the rock's 50 is E16's `enemies.stein_points` (no separate Hackl key). Hashes: the player's new fields and the `[hackl]`/`[crawl]` tuning change every state hash (testbed `FINISH_HASH` updated); routes and frames of existing replays are unchanged.
- **Route-map labels vs a big Resi** (art review of the Hackl map shot; a global move was rejected): a 16×32 Resi on a dot covered „Brunnwinkl". A station may have an alternate label slot (`Station::label_alt: Option<LabelSlot>`): Brunnwinkl primary rows 43–51 ending at x 114, alternate rows 20–28 ending at x 114; Falkenstein primary ending at x 172, alternate at x 171 (x 170 since E19, below). `map::screen_label_pos` picks the slot once per map screen from its end station and Resi's form only: the alternate slot iff Resi is big (Zuckerschock or Hackl) and her 16×32 box on the screen's **end** dot (where she waits) intersects the primary slot, outline included; the start dot never counts (art-director: the target label stays by its dot while she sets off, e.g. St. Gilgen → Brunnwinkl); Normal always keeps the primary slot (her 16×24 box touches Brunnwinkl's and Falkenstein's outline by a row/column, approved in M2). Labels never switch during the walk; Resi is still drawn over them, and every M2 golden is unchanged. The map test checks every screen and form: labels apart, big forms clear of Resi on the end dot; the allowed Normal touches and four collisions on the later stations (St. Wolfgang's label vs Wolfgangsee/Strobl, Bad Ischl's and Café Zauner's vs Bad Ischl) are listed for their milestones' map review.
- **Addendum (engine, M3 task E19): Falkenstein's alternate slot ends at x 170.** QA saw Hackl Resi touch the end of „Falkenstein" on the teaser screen (Falkenstein → St. Wolfgang). The end-dot rule did pick the alternate slot there (the primary overlaps her 16×32 box by a column), but the alternate slot's outline (x 171) sat directly against the box (x 172–187), so the two touched as boxes (with the content font and the current `player_hackl` idle frames the nearest drawn pixels were 3 px apart, but the box rule is what the map test guards, for any art). The slot now ends at x 170: a 1 px gap between the outline and the box. Normal Resi keeps the primary slot (every golden unchanged). Test: `falkenstein_keeps_a_gap_to_a_big_resi_on_st_wolfgang` (real font: the box grown by 1 px all round stays clear in Big and Hackl, and the primary overlaps it); `big_resi_moves_brunnwinkl_and_falkenstein_to_their_alternate_slots` updated to 170.

## D-061 Implementing D-058: Pilger, Gams, Steinschlag (engine, M3 task E16)
- **Level syntax**: `{ entity = "pilger", lead = true }` (`lead` optional, default false: only the sprite changes, `pilger_lead`); `{ entity = "gams" }` (no fields); `{ entity = "steinschlag", period = 90..=300, phase = 0 | 41..period, range = 4..=10 }` (defaults 150, 0, 6 tiles). A phase in 1..=40 or ≥ period is a parse error („phase must be 0 or 41..=…, so its first rock shows the whole telegraph"); other fields on them are parse errors as for every entity. `level::STEINSCHLAG_MIN_PHASE` (41) is a level constant; `enemies.steinschlag_telegraph` must stay below it (validated), so the level rule holds for any tuning.
- **Pilger** (`EnemyKind::Pilger`): the Selfie-Tourist's walk without selfies, 0.5 px/frame, facing the player on its first update (like the Nordic-Walker), walks off ledges (player gravity), turns at walls and at enemies on foot and counts as on foot itself. `lead` is read by the renderer from the spawn (`EntitySpawn::lead`); core behaviour is identical (tested). 100 points, chain start 100.
- **Gams** (`EnemyKind::Gams`, states `STAND`/`CROUCH`/`HOP`, `count` = frame in the state): stand `gams_stand` (60), crouch `gams_crouch` (16), hop until it lands. Gravity is added before the move, so with vy −4.0, gravity 0.25 it is level again after 31 frames and lands on the 32nd; apex is **30 px** (the designer's 32 px is the continuous formula), vx 1.0 → 32 px across; period 108 as specified. At the crouch's start it faces the player and plans the hop (stored in `vx`, it doesn't move while crouching). **Landing check, stricter than "the landing cell"**: `enemies::hop_is_clear` needs a solid or one-way top at its feet height under *every* column its hitbox sweeps from where it stands to the landing spot, and no solid tile in its body's rows there. A landing-cell-only check would let it hop a 1-tile gap onto a neighbouring ledge; "never leaves its ledge" wins. Blocked towards the player → other side → else a hop in place (vx 0). It has gravity in every state (falls if its floor goes) and is "on foot" except in a hop (blocks walkers, knocked off a bumped block). Stomp 200, chain start 200.
- `Tuning::parse` rejects a hop that would carry the Gams past the tiles it checked: `gams_hop_speed_x` × airtime (`tuning::gams_airtime`, simulated like the update) must be ≤ `gams_hop_tiles` × 16 px (content: exactly 32 px).
- **Steinschlag** (`EntityKind::Steinschlag`, not an enemy: no contact with the player): its hitbox is the resting rock's (`stein_hitbox` 12×12 centred in the niche cell, bottom 2 px above the cell bottom, the `stein` sprite's origin 8,14). State `WAITING` until the first frame the player is within `range` tiles (horizontal, centre to centre) **and** the niche cell is fully on screen with its top ≥ `steinschlag_screen_top` (24) px below the screen top; then `COUNTING` from `phase`, +1 per such frame (`count` mod `period`), paused otherwise. Telegraph = counter frames 0..39 (`Pose::Wobble`), the rock spawns at counter frame 40 (`steinschlag_telegraph`), the niche is empty (`pose()` = `None`) until `steinschlag_refill` (30) frames later, then `Pose::Rest`. `UpdateCtx` gained `screen_top` (camera y) for the on-screen test; it uses the existing (horizontal) activation, so E14's vertical activation proximity applies to it unchanged.
- **Stein** (`EntityKind::Stein`): vy from 0, `stein_gravity` 0.25, `stein_max_fall` 4.0; shatters (`state::SHATTERED`, removed) when `move_y` lands it on a solid or one-way top; the game then spawns 4 `EntityKind::SteinDebris` pieces (brick-debris speeds). It ignores water, hazards, enemies and items; below the level it is removed. Contact hurts from any side (no stomp). The pebbles (`kiesel`) are renderer-only: `enemies::rock_column_bottom` gives the column's floor.
- **Defeat by projectile** (for the thrown Hackl, E15): `Game::defeat_by_projectile(i) -> bool` (core, `pub(crate)`) applies `enemies::hit_by_projectile`: Pilger / Gams → defeated, tumbling (`defeat(.., knocked = true)`), for `score.stomp_chain[kind.chain_start()]` (100 / 200) outside the stomp chain; a falling Stein → shattered with debris, `enemies.stein_points` (50); score popup at the target. It returns false (no effect) for every other kind, so the Hackl code handles those itself or extends the match. A Steinschlag niche is never a target.
- **Renderer**: sprites `pilger`, `pilger_lead`, `gams`, `stein` (anims `rest`/`wobble`/`fall`), `stein_debris`, `kiesel` (anim `fall`); until the art exists everything falls back to grey boxes (Pilger `g`, Gams `B`, Stein and niche `F`, debris `f`, pebbles 2×2 `m`, a `lead` Pilger gets a 1 px pole with a red flag above its box). No placeholder `.sprite` files. Three pebbles trickle down the column at 3 px/frame during the telegraph.
- New tuning keys change the tuning part of `state_hash` (testbed `FINISH_HASH` updated, route and frame unchanged). Grey-box level `00_m3_enemies` (side camera; solver 533 frames, narrowest window 12) with 6 handwritten replays `m3_enemies_*` and the solver replay.
- **Addendum (engine, M3 task E19): a Pilger file activates together.** QA: activation was per entity, so a group straddling the activation edge (camera x + 320 + `activate_margin`) when the camera stopped split, and the leader walked off alone (78 px ahead in a relaxed run), breaking D-058's "in file at equal speed" that the stomp chain relies on. Now Pilger spawned in **adjacent cells of the same row** form a file (`enemies::pilger_file`: its row and leftmost column; a gap, another row or another entity in between separates files). `Game::update_entities` first collects the files of every pilgrim that wakes by the normal rule (horizontal, plus D-054's vertical proximity in climb levels, which is the same for a whole row), then wakes every inactive member of those files in the same frame, before anything moves. Facing: spawns have no facing; every member faces the player on its first update as before, and since they wake on the same frame with the player well left of the activation edge, a file faces one way (only a player standing inside a file's span when it wakes, possible in climb levels, would split its facing; no level does that). Tests: `a_pilger_file_straddling_the_activation_edge_wakes_together` (columns 39–41 across x 640 with the camera parked, both ends), `only_adjacent_pilger_in_the_same_row_form_a_file`. Replays: all 194 pass unchanged (`00_m3_enemies`' groups never straddle the edge); no replay updated.

## D-062 The rock-niche lint measures to the first solid tile (coordinator, M3 code review of E16)
- `levels lint`'s Steinschlag niche rule (D-058, implemented in D-059) measures the niche bottom's height to the first **solid** tile below the niche, skipping one-way tiles (`netz`, `steig`, `steig_wall`, any one-way). The range stays 4–6 tiles.
- Reason: Falkenstein rock #1's niche is 6 tiles above the path with a rockfall net 3 tiles up (game-design.md, sec. 3). Measured to the first solid-or-one-way tile, that niche was only 3 tiles high and failed the lint, and so did E16's test level. The rule exists so that a player standing on the path sees the whole niche below the HUD; that is the path, not the shelter the rock shatters on.
- Implementation: `climblint::niche_problem`; tests `rock_niches_are_4_to_6_tiles_above_their_solid_ground` and `rock_niches_skip_one_way_shelters_and_planks`. Supersedes D-059's "first solid or one-way tile" for this lint only (the fall-depth lint still lands on one-way tiles).

## D-063 `kalkfels_bg` joins only the drawn tile above it (engine, M3 task E18b, code review of E18)
- Supersedes the join list of D-059's "Art-review add-on" (and E18's "every drawn neighbour on every side"). The one-way autotile joins (`jnr-render` `tiles::one_way_join`) are now:
  - `kalkfels_bg` ↔ `steig_wall`: the back wall counts the plank as the same on all four sides; the plank still sees only itself (art-director approved, double-shadow fix).
  - `kalkfels_bg` counts any other drawn neighbour **above** it (any name except air and `invisible`: rock, plank, box, brick, any solid or one-way) as the same for the N bit only.
  - `kalkfels` counts solid ground above it (`forest_path`, `meadow`, `stone`, `ground`) as the same for N, and `kalkfels_bg` below it for S.
- Reason: the sprite's only transparent pixels are the row-0 notches of frames with the N bit open, so joins on E/S/W fixed nothing and removed designed shading, e.g. the ragged left `d` shadow column of the back wall beside a shaft's side wall. The top shadow and notches now show only against air/sky above.
- Tests: `kalkfels_bg_joins_any_drawn_tile_above_it_only`, `kalkfels_joins_kalkfels_bg_below_it_only` (the back wall beside rock keeps its W shadow), `kalkfels_bg_joins_a_steig_wall_plank_one_way` (unchanged).

## D-064 M3 acceptance: Falkenstein's level deviations accepted (game-designer, M3 acceptance)
- The as-built `04_falkenstein.level` is accepted; game-design.md "Stage 4" now describes it. Supersedes the matching planning numbers in D-053 (checkpoint col 114, provisional `time_limit` 240) and the planning spec's positions; the rules (D-053..D-058, the Climbing rules table) are unchanged and all hold.
- **H-box at col 164, row 8** (planned 176, row 7): on the enemy-free church plateau, 2 rows up (big Resi fits under it, the Hackl on its top is a standing jump), 9 tiles from the pigeon's trigger and ≥ 15 from group C, so 170–194 is a clear axe lane. Still after the checkpoint (a respawn gets it again).
- **Einsiedelei opens to the right** (planned closed): the cave can't trap anyone, and the Zaunerstollen may roll out and must be chased, as SMB's 1-ups do. Accepted because the bonus is optional and the plateau and saddle are open ground.
- **Gams #1 on a free-standing shelf 95–99 with a fallen rock at 93** as the stomp step (planned a shelf hanging from the overhang at 99–104): the D-061 swept landing check keeps it on the shelf; the optional stomp is now readable. Rocks moved to 92 / 105 / 107, cleft to 110–112; one hazard per required jump still holds.
- **Checkpoint at col 116 behind a 1-high curb at 115**, `M`-box at 117 (planned 114 and 123 inside the shaft): holding LEFT after a respawn can't walk into the cleft; the Mozartkugel is caught between the curb and the shaft wall. The shaft's left wall ends at row 20 (entrance 5 high) so the first plank jump is made in the open. Signs moved with their landmarks (bus 119, Hacklwurf 167 on the plateau, Einsiedelei 160).
- **The Hackl and the Durchkriechstein are optional**: the solver route skips both. That matches the design (power-ups are never required, D-057 forbids a forced crawl); without the Hackl group C is a stomp chain. The box is on the walking path and hard to miss.
- **`time_limit = 255`**: 3 × the relaxed run of ~85 s (D-045, within 200–300).
- **Difficulty**: medium, a step above Brunnwinkl through length, height, two lethal clefts, rock timing and three new enemy kinds (23 jumps vs 16, relaxed run 85 s vs ~62 s), while the narrowest window (12 frames, rule ≥ 5) stays as forgiving as Brunnwinkl's (11). Accepted on purpose for the first climb stage; later stages may tighten windows towards the rule.

## D-065 Climb camera: upward slack for the grounded settle (engine, M3 follow-up task E20)
- **Problem** (backlog "Falkenstein H-box framing", QA M3 acceptance): grounded, D-054's climb camera settles the feet at screen y 148, which shows only 32 px below them. Any box she can walk under has its top ≥ 3 tiles above the ground, so standing on it the ground she came from (and whatever walks there) leaves the screen. On the H-box (164, row 8; plateau row 11, axe lane row 12) the camera even rose to the level top and group C / the saddle Gams were thrown at blind.
- **Rule** (`Camera::follow_climb`, `ClimbFocus::settle_feet`): grounded, the camera settles towards the framing of the **settle feet** instead of the feet. They follow the ground down at once (any step down or level ground: settle feet = feet), but up only once the feet are more than `camera.climb_up_slack = 48` px (3 tiles) above them, and then only by the excess (SMB/SMW style). So a box or plank 3 tiles up keeps the framing, a 4-tile step settles 16 px, and in the shaft the first plank keeps the framing and every further one moves it by 3 tiles (grounded, the feet stay at screen y ≥ 100, so even a big hitbox top is ≥ 70 px down, well below `climb_top`).
- **Addition to the task's rule: `camera.climb_up_reach = 48`.** The slack holds only while she stays within 48 px (horizontally, hitbox centre) of where she stepped onto the ground she stands on (`ClimbFocus::slack_x`, reset on every new ground height); walking further, the settle feet catch up with her feet (at the usual 2 px/frame). Reason: without it any climb of small steps leaves the camera up to 3 tiles low **for good** (feet at screen y 100). On Falkenstein that silenced the Steinschlag: its spawners only count while the niche is on screen below the HUD (D-061), and after the Holzstiege or the trough the niches over path row 28 stayed above the screen, so rock #2 never fell in `qa_m3_hackl_vs_rock` (and 10 more QA routes broke through later Pilger wake-ups). With the reach every replay passes unchanged; the box tops, planks and the H-box (1 tile wide) are well inside it.
- **Unchanged**: in the air everything else is D-054 (the up-follow line while no slack holds, fall-follow limit, death line). The fall-follow limit is still measured from the **last ground's own** framing (`ground_feet`), not from the settle feet, so the death line and the fall-depth lint's 7–9-tile rule mean what they meant (a cleft beside a box kills exactly where it did). Start, respawn and stage changes frame the feet at 148 at once (settle feet = feet). The goal camera (D-059 addendum, E19) is unchanged. Side levels are bit-identical in play; their state hashes change only because the tuning (hashed via `Debug`) gained four keys (`climb_up_slack`, `climb_up_reach`, `climb_top_min`, `climb_catch_up_speed`) and `Game` hashes `settle_feet` and `slack_x` (both 0 there).
- **No bob on a held box (E20 review, option b; Normal still, big/Hackl a bounded ~8 px follow that settles back in ~4 frames).** With the slack holding, a jump from the box passed `climb_top`, the camera followed up and then settled back down: a bob on exactly the H-box spot. Now, in the air, the up-follow line is measured from the held framing: `climb_top − lag`, where `lag = settle_feet − ground_feet` (≥ 0, the framing's lag below the last ground's own), floored at `camera.climb_top_min = 11`. A Normal walk jump from a held 3-tile box peaks with the top at screen y 11 exactly, so it leaves `camera.y` unchanged; a run jump follows only as far as keeping the top at 11 needs (≥ the 8 px on screen the review asked for). The review asked for a floor of 8; **11 is the highest floor that still keeps the walk jump still**, and with 8 (or 9, 10) the re-solved Falkenstein route measured a narrowest window of **3** at the shaft's first plank jump (below `min_jump_window = 5`), with 11 it measures 8. Both values meet the review's "top at least 8 px on screen".
- **Landing catch-up.** In the shaft (planks 3 tiles apart) every plank after the first is held at the plank below, so a jump to the next plank no longer follows up and the landing leaves her top above `climb_top` (down to screen y ~13). The excess settle alone (2 px/frame) would leave her under the HUD for ~14 frames, a snap would pop the camera ~27 px: instead, grounded with the top above `climb_top`, the camera moves up `camera.climb_catch_up_speed = 6` px per frame more than the settle (8 in all) until the top is at `climb_top` (≤ 4 frames in the shaft). So grounded the top is above `climb_top` only during that catch-up, and never above `climb_top_min`.
- **Moving platforms**: on a vertically moving platform the feet change every frame, so every frame is "new ground" and `slack_x` resets every frame; the lag is still capped at `climb_up_slack` (riding up holds the framing until the excess, then follows). No climb level has moving platforms yet.
- **Tuning validation**: `0 ≤ climb_up_slack ≤ climb_feet_y − climb_top`, `climb_up_slack ≤ climb_fall_follow` (so a held framing is never below the fall-follow limit and the limit's "never pulls it up" can't hide a lower death line), `0 ≤ climb_up_reach ≤ 320`, `0 ≤ climb_top_min ≤ climb_top`, `0 ≤ climb_catch_up_speed ≤ 16`.
- **Falkenstein**: the solver route changed (vertical activation and the camera state steer the search): complete at **1540** (was 1542), 22 jumps, narrowest jump window **8** frames (frame 130, x 324; was 12). An intermediate cut of this task measured 6, and 3 with a floor of 8 (see above). D-064's "narrowest window 12" now reads 8. The tour re-copies the route; the other Falkenstein replays keep their copy of the M3 route (marked "before the E20 re-solve"). `falkenstein_rock_hit`: rock #2's niche comes on screen later on that route, the death moved 623 → 636 (shots re-picked 622/632).
- Tests (E20 review additions: `climb_up_follow_line_moves_up_by_the_slack_lag`, `climb_grounded_top_above_climb_top_catches_up_at_8_px_per_frame`, `a_walk_jump_from_a_held_box_leaves_the_camera_still`, `a_run_jump_from_a_held_box_follows_only_to_climb_top_min`, `falkenstein_h_box_top_keeps_the_axe_lane_on_screen` (the original bug: row 12 on screen for every frame she stands on the H-box in `falkenstein_hackl_axes`), the shaft test now checks the top ≥ `climb_top_min` in the air and ≥ `climb_top` from 4 frames after each landing): `camera.rs` (`climb_keeps_the_framing_on_a_step_up_of_up_to_3_tiles`, `climb_settles_up_only_by_the_excess_over_the_slack`, `climb_slack_holds_only_within_the_reach_of_the_step_up`, `climb_settles_down_at_once_below_the_settle_feet`, `climb_fall_follow_limit_is_measured_from_the_last_ground`), `tests/climb.rs` (`a_3_tile_step_up_keeps_the_ground_below_on_screen`, `a_4_tile_step_up_settles_by_16_px_at_2_px_per_frame`, `walking_on_along_higher_ground_settles_on_it`, `a_shaft_of_planks_3_tiles_apart_keeps_resi_on_screen`, `a_cleft_beside_a_box_still_kills_at_the_death_line`), tuning validation (see above). New golden shot `falkenstein_hackl_axes_box_lane`.

## D-066 M4 scope: Stage 5 St. Wolfgang, its shape and difficulty (game-designer, M4 planning)
- M4 is **one stage**, `05_st_wolfgang` (300 × 17 tiles, `camera = "side"`). It introduces the **Schafbergbahn** (D-067), the **Operettensänger** with the high-C column (D-068), the **Souvenirstand** with rolling **Schneekugeln** (D-069) and the **Reisebus** (D-070), and a second `H`-box (D-071). Reused: Selfie-Tourist, Pilger, `taube_sitting`, Mozartkugel, Zaunerstollen, balconies, jetty and Wolfgangsee water, checkpoint, Maibaum. As before, each new thing extends an existing system, so M4 stays one milestone: Festungsbahn → Schafbergbahn (same state machine, own numbers and drawn track), Blasmusiker range counter → singer (vertical column instead of horizontal notes), Steinschlag spawner → stand, Mozartkugel power-up roll → Schneekugel, Fiaker → Reisebus (plus a scripted stop).
- **Shape: side-scrolling with one long rising ride**, not a climb level. The climb camera (D-054) earns its cost on a tall stage; St. Wolfgang is a lakeside village with one mountain railway. A ride 6 tiles up a 1:4 track fits the standard 17-row layout and side camera, and keeps the goal framing (D-029/D-041) and all side-level tooling unchanged. **Diagonal movement is not new**: the Festungsbahn and the Gondel already move along any straight `to = [dx, dy]` line with exact carrying, so the cog railway needs no new motion code (D-067).
- **Stylised order** (Falkenstein precedent): Ried landing → Schafbergbahn valley station → over the gorge to the Alm → bus park at the bypass tunnel → Markt (Pilgerbrunnen, church) → Weißes Rössl → promenade → boat landing → Maibaum. In reality the bus park sits at the eastern tunnel portal, past the Markt. The stage puts the buses first so the stage reads "first the buses, then the crowds". Ending at the boat landing hands over to Stage 6 on the lake.
- **Difficulty**: medium, a step above Falkenstein. The step comes through combinations on safe ground and tighter windows, not height: `min_jump_window = 5` (the verify gate), with a **design target** of narrowest 5–7 frames and at least three required jumps ≤ 8 (backlog M3 note 2; Falkenstein measures 8). Required gaps ≤ 4, step-ups ≤ 3, drops ≤ 3; one moving hazard per required jump over water or a lethal gap, up to two over solid ground; landings ≥ 2 wide over water/gaps. The full table is in game-design.md ("Difficulty and reach rules (stage 5)"). The gate stays at 5 rather than 4: the stage already adds four new kinds, and the backlog asks to tighten towards the rule, not to move it. Stage 6+ may use 4.
- **Numbers**: `time_limit` per D-045 (provisional 260: relaxed ~85 s expected, the train ride ~10 s); Oblaten 75 ± 10; `M`-box at the start (col 14) and within 10 columns after the checkpoint (col 158); `H`-box on the church terrace (col 194, after the checkpoint); `Z`-box on the Kaiserterrasse (optional upper route); checkpoint at col 150 between the bus park and the Markt.
- **Running gags**: Bus 150 on two adjacent signs (D-039): „Bus 150 / kommt nicht" at the start (line 150 doesn't serve St. Wolfgang; you change in Strobl to 546) → „Bus 546 / auch voll" beside the checkpoint (the bus that does come is full too). The tour buses that do come are the stage's charger. The pigeon on the monument sits on the Pilgerbrunnen's canopy. Everything is full: „26 % steil / 0 % Platz". Faith is never the joke: the church, the altar and the fountain are respectful scenery, and the H-box legend is told as a fact („Hier landete / das Hackl"). The jokes target souvenirs („Advent / ganzjährig", snow globes in July), coach tourism („Fotostopp / 5 Minuten"), the operetta cliché (singing waiters, „Glück steht / vor der Tür"), staff shortage („Zahlkellner / gesucht"), and slowness as a selling point („Zum Gipfel / 35 Minuten").
- **Route**: shipping `05_st_wolfgang.level` (+ its `options::LEVELS` line) makes St. Wolfgang playable (D-047). The teaser moves to St. Wolfgang → Wolfgangsee. The M3 replays and goldens that end in the Falkenstein teaser now end on the route map in Wait or on the St. Wolfgang intro, and have to be re-cut and re-approved (as in D-053).
- **Not in M4** (backlog): a bus that unloads a tour group; the high C shattering snow globes; the full ride to the Schafberg summit as a bonus stage; the floating jacuzzi as a moving platform; the Pacher altar interior as a bonus room; the devil-and-wolf legend; the descent from the Falkenstein to Ried.
- **Addendum (level-designer, M4): Stage 5 as built** (`content/levels/05_st_wolfgang.level`; its header lists every column). Sections, enemies, signs and props as specified, with these deviations:
  - **Gorge and the D-067 shortcut**: rock steps under the lower track at 52–59 (street level), 60–63 (row 14) and 64–67 (row 13), the gorge at **68–77** (10 wide as specified, moved 8 columns uphill), and only a 2-wide shelf at 78–79 (row 10, 3 below the halt) instead of stepped rock at 70–79. The pit-recovery lint skips the gorge but needs a stair up to the halt beyond it, and any floor under the track is a landing for a running jump off the roof's front. With the spec's steps at 70–79 the jump works from a quarter of the way up the track; with the shelf alone it works only once the front is over column ~68. A gorge that forces the whole ride can't pass the lint, so the shortcut is **accepted** as the expert line. Riding back down, Resi can't step off at the lower platform: the train arrives with her on it, which counts as a waiting rider, so it leaves again at once. This is accepted, because there is no softlock: she can walk off onto the steps under the track while it is low, or ride up again. The solver rides a little, runs ahead along the steps, catches the train again at the gorge and leaps to the shelf (window 11). `st_wolfgang_zug` rides the whole way with two tap hops.
  - **Hop Oblaten**: 8 Oblaten in a line rising with the track over the gorge (68–75, rows 7/6/5), 29–45 px above the roof under a rider on the coach (the uphill half). Standing doesn't collect them; a tap hop takes 1–2 (D-067: reward 1–2 hops). The spec's 2–3-tile arc would sit under the HUD while she rides.
  - **Seebad**: jetty 244–252 with gaps 247–248 and 253–256 (the 4-gap), and the landing is the promenade **quay one tile higher** (row 14, 257–279). The higher landing is what brings the leap over both gaps from the first jetty (the solver's line) down from 9 to 5 frames. The relaxed way is two jumps via the 4-wide middle jetty (4 across, 1 up). Stand #3, the „Schifffahrt" sign and the step move up with the quay (step 278–279 is now 2 high, top row 12); the hall (rows 10–15), meadow, Maibaum and Ortsende are as specified.
  - **Bus park and checkpoint (QA fix)**: Selfie-Tourist #3 is penned at the Markt entrance (159, pacing 153–162 between a 1-high stone bench at 152 and the fountain basin) instead of standing on the road at 146. At 146 he was reset 4 columns left of the checkpoint and walked into a respawned Resi who held LEFT, and he stood right behind bus #2 during its photo stop. Now a respawned Resi holding LEFT reaches the screen's left edge (x 2264) on open road. Bus #2 may trigger, but it stays parked or stopped left of that edge (right edge 2208, or 2142 at its stop) and drives away from her (`qa_m4_checkpoint_hold_left`). The tourist comes out only as the Markt starts, so he is met right after the checkpoint (stomp him over the bench), not in the bus lane.
  - **Alm**: a 1-high meadow hump at 98 pens Selfie-Tourist #2 on its terrace (92–97). Without it the tourist walked down onto the last terrace, right where bus #1 triggers (a 4-frame window).
  - **Markt**: the `M`-box moves from 158 to **156** (bricks 155/157, 6 columns after the checkpoint). A running leap over the Pilgerbrunnen canopy (4 up) was squeezed under the bricks (3 frames).
  - **Rössl**: B1 at 217–220 (art-director note: it starts at the hotel), with Operettensänger #1 at **219** (QA fix: at 218 a walking landing on B1 slid into him; now 2 tiles of floor lie left of him and col 220 right of him for the jump to B2) and its Oblate at 217. B2 is at 223–228 (the art's balcony line). The Kaiserterrasse is at 233–242, so the gap from B2 stays 4. Its stoppers are a stone flower box at 233 and the chimney at 242, both on the terrace (row 7). Its posts at 233/242 (rows 9–14) are the decorative `terrasse_post_l`/`terrasse_post_r` tiles (no collision, so the street under it stays free).
  - **Small moves**: `haltestelle_alm` at 82 and „Zum Gipfel" at 86 (they overlapped at 83/85); „26 % steil" at 48.
  - **Numbers**: 75 Oblaten placed + 1 in a `?`-box at 142; `time_limit` 260 (provisional); the solver finishes at frame 2098. After the QA fixes the solver finishes at frame 2102. The narrowest window is the Seebad leap (5); the next are 11 (the gorge leap) and 12, and bus #2 is 14. The narrowest target (5–7) is met. **Gap**: only one jump is ≤ 8, where the target asks for three. Before the QA fixes the solver's route had the jump over singer #2 at 5 and the Pilgerbrunnen leap at 8. The windows move with every small edit (the solver picks a different line): trials moving singer #1 or the B1 Oblate gave singer-#2 jumps of 2–25 and Seebad leaps of 4–5. Tightening further would put the gate at risk, so the layout keeps the variant whose narrowest is 5. `time_limit` stays 260: QA's relaxed run is 87.4 s, × 3 = 262 (D-045).
  - **Pen walls (QA acceptance bug `qa_m4_bench_tourist_head`)**: walkers are taller than a tile (Selfie-Tourist 20 px, Pilger 22 px), so where one turns at a 1-tile wall his head sticks 4-6 px above its top, and Resi standing on that wall with her hitbox even a fraction of a pixel past its edge was hurt (she isn't falling, so no stomp). Rule from now on: **every wall a walker turns at and Resi can stand on is at least 2 tiles above the walker's floor**. Stage 5 places: tourist #1 (Ried) paces a dip at 33-43 (floor row 16; stand #1's counter at 32 and the step at 44 become 2 high to him); tourist #2's Alm terrace 92-97 is one tile lower (row 11; step 91 is 3 high, the hump at 98 2 high); tourist #3's bench at 152 and the basin's left end at 163 are 2 high (the Pilgerwasser sign moves to 160 so the basin end shows); tourist #4's globe lane 168-177 is sunk one tile (the basin at 167 and stand #2's counter at 178 become 2 high to him); the Pilger pair, if they walk down to the street, are penned in a dip at 181-183 (stand #2's counter at 180, the step at 184); the Advent sign moves from 182 into the lowered globe lane (171) so its board no longer hides the dip (art-director note). Stand #2 gets its own legend entry with `range = 7` (8 gave the solver a 4-frame globe hop out of the sunken lane). No other walker in Stage 5 turns at a wall (the Pilger walk down the church steps; the singers, stands and buses don't walk). Stages 1-4 were not audited for this class (e.g. 22 px Nordic-Walkers). Solver after the fix: frame 2104, narrowest 5 (the Seebad leap), next 7, 7, 8. The relaxed run is now 88.7 s (× 3 = 266 s); `time_limit` stays 260 (inside D-045's 200-300 range, a 2 % rounding). An engine rule (a grounded player whose feet are at or above a walker's top is never hurt by him) would remove the class of bug; it is a backlog suggestion, not M4 work.

## D-067 The Schafbergbahn: the Festungsbahn's state machine on a long 1:4 track (game-designer, M4 planning)
- New platform kind `schafbergbahn` (`to = [dx, dy]` required, 4 ≤ |dx| ≤ 32). It **waits for its rider** at the lower end, travels, waits `schafbergbahn_wait` = 90 frames at the top, travels back and waits again. This is exactly the Festungsbahn (D-030/D-034): landing, carrying, crush, never-despawn, positions recomputed from the spawn every frame. Its own numbers: body `schafbergbahn_size` = 64×32 (a whole number of tiles, so with whole-tile `to` both roofs lie on the tile grid and sit **flush with the station platforms**: walk on, walk off); travel = max(|dx|, |dy|) × `schafbergbahn_frames_per_tile` = 24 (Stage 5: 24 tiles = 576 frames ≈ 9.6 s; 0.67 px/frame across, so a hop on the roof drifts ~20 px back and lands on the 64 px roof).
- Reasons: a waiting train can't be missed, so a long ride over a lethal gorge is still a safe introduction; standing still is always safe. The Festungsbahn's fixed `festungsbahn_travel` (150 frames for any length) would be 2.6 px/frame on this track, faster than Resi runs. A free-running train (Gondel style) over a 10-tile gorge would turn a landmark into a timing test with long waits.
- The **renderer draws the track** (rails, rack, sleepers) from end to end + 1 tile, like the Gondel cable. It is the ride's telegraph and keeps the 1:4 slope out of the tile grid (no slopes, architecture.md). Steam puffs are renderer particles from the entity's counter.
- **Lints**: the 3-tile clearance applies to the train's **swept body**, so no solid or one-way tile may overlap the body anywhere along the track, nor the 3 tiles above its roof. The Festungsbahn lint only looked above the car; a 64×32 box moving up a 1:4 slope sweeps a parallelogram that stepped ground under the track could cut. The pit-recovery check is skipped over lethal pits, as for Gondel B (D-044). Level rule: no enemy, projectile or globe can reach the track or the rider while it can be ridden (D-032).
- **Fallbacks** (engine's call, record it if taken): (1) no drawn track: the level lays the decorative `zahnrad_gleis_0..3` tiles along the line instead; (2) if the new kind is costly, three `festungsbahn` cars of `to = [8, -2]` with 2-tile intermediate platforms (passing loops), drawn with the `schafbergbahn` sprite. This is the stepped fallback; its 150-frame travel is 0.85 px/frame, so it is fine. The gorge then sits under the middle car only.
- **Not taken**: real slopes (the architecture keeps AABB-on-grid collision); a train you ride to the summit (a stage of its own, backlog); jumps that inherit the platform's speed (no platform does; changing it would retune every moving-platform jump in Stages 1–2).
- **Addendum (engine, M4 task E21): as built.** Syntax and numbers as specified (`platforms.schafbergbahn_size = [64, 32]`, `schafbergbahn_frames_per_tile = 24`, `schafbergbahn_wait = 90`; validated: size 1..=64 px and whole tiles, frames per tile 1..=64, wait + 64 × frames per tile ≤ 32767). No fallback taken: the track is drawn. Deviations and findings:
  - **The roof follows the rail pixel for pixel** (pixel-artist request): the train's x is the straight line as for every platform, its height is snapped to whole px by `platform::rail_offset` (−floor(u · (−dy) / dx) from the spawn), so its 4:1 position never sits between two rail rows. Riders are carried by the train's own per-frame delta as before (exact); the end points are unchanged (whole tiles). The Festungsbahn and the Gondel keep the plain line (replays unchanged).
  - **Track look** (pixel-artist layout, art-director ruling): drawn behind the tiles; `d` rails at Y and Y+3, `m` rack at Y+1 (even columns) and Y+2, `O` sleepers 2 px wide every 8 px over Y+1..=Y+4 under the rails, a `d` girder at Y+5 **under the whole track** (one viaduct line, not a bare band like the Gondel cable), no light lip on the top rail (the diagonal must never read as a walkable slope); Y = the feet row at the spawn + `rail_offset`. Steam: a `dampf` puff every 20 travel frames, leaving 2 px below the funnel cap's top (6 px above the roof, 6 px in from the loco's left end) and rising 1 px per frame for 24 frames where it left; computed from the train's counter, no renderer state.
  - **The swept-body lint is tighter than the stage text implies**: on a track rising right the body's lowest point over a column is where its lower right corner enters it, not its left edge: over column c (c ≥ body left column + 4) the bottom is B − 4 (c − c0 − 4) px on a 1:4 track. The Stage 5 steps must sit below that (e.g. row 14 only from column 60 on); the level-authoring skill gives the formula.
  - **Hop drift (risk 2), measured** (`tests/schafbergbahn.rs`): riding up, a tap hop (24 frames in the air) lands 15.3 px further back on the roof, a full standing jump (51 frames) 33.3 px, more than the ~20 px estimated here; riding down, 16.7 px / 36 px forward (26 / 55 frames: the roof sinks away). From the roof's middle a full hop lands with only 4 px of her 12 px hitbox on it; a 10-frame hop drifts 22 px, so two such hops from 44 px in end on the back edge and a third misses (replays `schafbergbahn_hops`, `schafbergbahn_hops_off_the_back`). The Oblaten arc should reward hops from the uphill half and not ask for more than one or two.
  - **A running jump off the moving roof clears a wide gap**: in the test level the solver skips most of the ride by running along the roof and jumping off its front across an 8-column gorge. Stage 5 should check that this shortcut is acceptable or widen the gorge.

## D-068 Operettensänger and the high-C column (game-designer, M4 planning)
- **Operettensänger** (`operettensaenger`, the singing head waiter of the „Weißes Rössl" operetta): stands, faces the player, 16×24 / 12×22. While the player is within 10 tiles (< 161 px) a 180-frame counter runs (pauses out of range, keeping its pose; starts at 0 on first entering range): **inhale 0–39** (telegraph), **high C 40–87**, idle 88–179 (the 92-frame window).
- **High C**: a vertical **sound column**, 12 px wide, centred on him, growing from his hitbox top at 8 px/frame to 80 px. It passes one-way tiles and stops at the first solid tile above. Any contact hurts, also from above; **the column is checked before the stomp**, so falling onto a singing singer hurts. Outside the column he is stompable (during the inhale too; that cancels the C), 200 points with chain start 200 (D-046), and a bumped block knocks him off. Defeated by stomp or axe: popup „Bravo!" (`popup.bravo`) above the score popup.
- Reasons: the Blasmusiker already owns horizontal projectiles; a vertical column is a new reading (time your stomp, not your jump past) built from existing parts (a range counter, a hurt rectangle). It also makes balconies matter: a singer on a balcony is harmless to the street below and blocks the upper route periodically. The safe introduction is exactly that: singer #1 sings into the sky over Resi's head.
- **Level rules**: nothing in a balcony singer's columns invites a jump from below; a street singer has ≥ 6 free tiles either side and nothing solid lower than 6 tiles above his head; his idle window is where the level-designer tightens the stage's narrowest required windows.
- **Fallback** (engine): if clipping the column at solid tiles is costly, a fixed 80 px column plus a `levels lint` rule "no solid tile within 6 tiles above an Operettensänger".
- **Not taken**: an expanding ring shockwave (a growing circle hitbox, and it overlaps the Blasmusiker's horizontal role); notes as projectiles (Blasmusiker); a singer that walks (the counter and stomp-window reading needs him in place).
- **Addendum (engine, M4 task E22): as built.** Level syntax `{ entity = "operettensaenger" }` (no fields; any other field is a parse error). Tuning `[enemies]`: `operettensaenger_hitbox` [12, 22], `operettensaenger_range` 160 (< 161 px), `operettensaenger_cycle` 180, `operettensaenger_inhale` 40, `operettensaenger_sing` 48, `hohes_c_width` 12, `hohes_c_speed` 8, `hohes_c_height` 80, validated in `Tuning::parse` (inhale + sing < cycle, so there is always an idle window). The counter is the Blasmusiker's (`count` = next frame of the cycle; states `STAND`/`INHALE`/`SING`). The column is not an entity: `enemies::high_c_column` derives it every frame from his counter (height 8 px × frames into the C, capped at 80; 12 px centred on him; from his hitbox top up to the bottom of the first solid tile in its columns, one-way tiles ignored, row 0's solid tiles extend above the level as for the player). So the axe passes it by construction (it only meets entities) and it touches no items, globes or enemies. `Game::interact` tests it **before** the singer's own hitbox and every stomp rule: any overlap is a hurt and skips the stomp for him that frame (risk 3 resolved as specified: unit test `falling_onto_a_singing_singer_hurts_instead_of_stomping`, replay `m4_enemies_singer_column_hurt`, where she would have landed on him 2 frames later). Deviations: (1) out of range he pauses **with a sounding column kept** (the pose rule applied to the C; harmless, since she is > 10 tiles away); (2) „Bravo!" also shows when a bumped block knocks him off (D-068 names stomp and axe; a bump is the third defeat, one rule for all three); the popup sits `POPUP_STACK_PX` (11 px) above the score popup. He counts as an enemy on foot (walkers turn at him, a bumped block knocks him off). Renderer: `operettensaenger` anims `idle`/`inhale`/`sing`/`squished`; the column as `hohes_c` (`ring`) stacked every 8 px from his head, alternate rings one anim frame apart; a column clipped at a height that is no multiple of 8 gets one more ring hung from its top, so every hurting pixel lies in a ring slice (code review); grey-box fallbacks: the singer `d`, the column a white box with an `i` outline.

## D-069 Souvenirstand and Schneekugel (game-designer, M4 planning)
- **Souvenirstand** (`souvenirstand` spawner; `period` 90–300, default 150; `phase` 0 or 31..period−1, default 0, so the first globe of an approach always follows the whole telegraph; `range` 4–10 tiles, default 8): placed in the air cell in front of (left of) its counter. The stand itself is tiles: `standl` (solid counter, 1 high) and `standl_dach` (one-way awning, 3 up, 1 tile of air between them). Its counter runs **only while the player's centre is left of it, within `range`, and the spawner cell is on screen** (the Steinschlag rule: the first globe is always seen coming). Telegraph f 0–29 (the vendor holds up a globe), spawn at f 30, at most **2** globes alive (a cycle with 2 alive shows no telegraph and spawns nothing). Harmless itself, can't be defeated, not an axe target.
- **Schneekugel** (`schneekugel`, 12×12 / 10×10): rolls **left at 1.0** with the player's gravity and max fall, off ledges, never turns. It **shatters on a solid wall** (harmless shards), and is removed in water/hazards, below the level or off screen left. It passes through enemies and items. Side contact hurts. **Stompable**: it shatters, Resi bounces, 100 in the chain. Its defeat (stomp or axe) is drawn as a shatter, not a tumble.
- Reasons: the Verkäufer throws arcs and the Blasmusiker straight air trains; a ground roller with gravity is the third readable projectile, and the stand's roof is a safe spot built in. Snow globes are the most "kitsch souvenir" object there is, sold in July (the Wolfgangseer Advent is the real winter market: „Advent / ganzjährig"). Making globes stompable gives the projectile a skill answer and the stand a score loop. Chains can't run away: globes ~150 px apart can't be chained without landing.
- **Safe introduction**: stand #1's globes shatter on a 1-high curb in front of Resi before they reach her. **Level rules**: every stand's globes roll ≥ 3 tiles in the open before a required take-off; a globe lane over solid ground may hold one more hazard (the Markt tourist), never a lane into a required jump over water (stand #3 starts only after the last water gap).
- **Fallback** (engine): if stomping a 10 px roller misbehaves, Schneekugeln hurt and can't be stomped (jump over or axe them); the curb intro and the level rules stay.
- **Not taken**: a vendor that throws (the Verkäufer); globes that bounce back from walls (endless lanes); a stand that is an enemy you stomp (it would make the awning platform a target).
- **Addendum (engine, M4 task E22): as built.** Level syntax `{ entity = "souvenirstand", period = 90..=300, phase = 0 | 31..period, range = 4..=10 }` (defaults 150, 0, 8 tiles; a phase in 1..=30 or ≥ period is a parse error, `level::SOUVENIRSTAND_MIN_PHASE` = 31; `souvenirstand_telegraph` must stay below it, validated). Tuning `[enemies]`: `souvenirstand_telegraph` 30, `souvenirstand_max_alive` 2, `schneekugel_hitbox` [10, 10], `schneekugel_speed` 256 (1.0). The spawner (`EntityKind::Souvenirstand`, hitbox = its cell, no contact, axe `Pass`) runs the Steinschlag pattern (`WAITING` until the first qualifying frame, then from `phase`) with the conditions: the player's centre **strictly left** of the cell's centre, horizontal distance ≤ `range` × 16, and the cell fully inside the screen (no HUD margin: the stands sit on the street, far below it). **Max alive**: decided at each cycle's frame 0 (and on the first counting frame): with 2 of its globes alive the cycle is `SKIPPING` (pose idle, no globe); otherwise `COUNTING` (telegraph pose 0..29, the globe at 30). A stand's globes are found by their spawn point (`base_x`/`base_y`, the stand cell's bottom centre), passed in `UpdateCtx::globes`; nothing new is stored on the globe. Globes are `EnemyKind::Schneekugel` so stomp, chain, hurt and the axe's `Defeat` path are the existing ones (stomp chain start 100; axe 100 outside the chain); the shatter look is core: every shatter (wall, stomp, axe) removes the globe at once and spawns 4 `SchneekugelScherben` pieces (the rock debris' speeds), so no defeated globe is ever drawn. A globe is not „on foot" (walkers walk through it, a bumped block doesn't knock it). Removed in water/hazards, below the level, or once its right edge ≤ the screen's left edge. Risk 4 resolved without the fallback: a hop off the curb onto a rolling 10 px globe stomps over an ~18-frame range of take-off frames in `00_m4_enemies` (replay `m4_enemies_globe_stomp`), and a globe running into a standing Resi is always a side hit (her feet are below its middle). Renderer: `souvenirstand` drawn with its origin on the counter's first tile top (one column right of the cell), anims `idle`/`telegraph`; `schneekugel` (`roll`) never mirrored (it only rolls left: draw it rolling left); `schneekugel_scherben` as debris; grey-box fallbacks: vendor `S` 12×12 box (a white 4×4 globe above it in the telegraph), globe `h`, shards `w`.

## D-070 Reisebus: a stop-and-go charger (game-designer, M4 planning)
- **Reisebus** (`reisebus`, 64×32 / 60×28, optional `stop` 1–16 tiles): parked and visible, facing left. Trigger when the player is within 10 tiles of its centre (< 161 px; a 4-tile bus is mostly on screen at the trigger); **60-frame telegraph** (hazard lights, door closes); then it drives **left at 1.5**, **ignoring tiles** like the Fiaker and passing through enemies. With `stop = N`: at its spawn column − N it halts for the **photo stop**, 120 frames with harmless camera flashes in the windows, then a 30-frame telegraph and on again. Removed once its right edge is left of the screen. **Roof contact** (falling, feet above the hitbox middle on the previous frame) is a stomp bounce **without damage or points** in every state; any other contact hurts, also parked or stopped. **Axe: bounce** (immune, like the Fiaker).
- Reasons: the Fiaker (dodge) and the E-Biker (stomp) are taken; the bus adds **size** (the longest enemy, 60 px) and **rhythm** (go, stop, go), which you read and use: hop on its roof while it stands. It is immune to the Hackl, so the axe never solves the stage. Ignoring tiles keeps it as simple as the Fiaker and avoids a "bus crashes into a wall" second Hoppala.
- **Level rules**: a bus's lane (its hitbox rows) is free of solid and one-way tiles for 24 tiles left of its spawn, or to the level's left edge (`levels lint`: it must never be seen driving through a wall; the Stage 5 tunnel under the Alm gives it somewhere to go). Every bus has a dodge in view: a spot above its lane (the Alm terrace, the shelter roof), its roof, or its photo stop. Intro: bus #1 triggered while Resi stands on the Alm terrace above its lane; it drives beneath her into the tunnel.
- **Fallback** (engine): without `stop` the bus is a plain big charger, and bus #2 then gets the shelter as its only dodge (the level stays fair).
- **Not taken**: a bus that unloads a tour group (spawning walking enemies into the charger's lane breaks the one-hazard rule; backlog); a rideable bus roof (it drives left, backwards for Resi, and D-051 would drag the camera back); arriving from off screen (no unseen hazards).
- **Addendum (engine, M4 task E23): the Reisebus as built.** D-070 as specified, with these engine calls: (1) **placement**: the spawn cell is the leftmost of the bus's 4 cells (the 60×28 hitbox centred under the 64×32 sprite, feet on the cell bottom), matching game-design's "col 112 (cells 112–115)"; `stop = N` halts it exactly at its parked hitbox x − N tiles (the last 1.5 px step is clamped, so every whole-tile `stop` works). (2) **Durations** follow the Fiaker's counting: 60 telegraph frames including the trigger frame, then it moves; the photo stop is 120 frames including the arrival frame, then 30 restart-telegraph frames, then it moves; the window flashes alternate every `reisebus_flash_frames` = 16 frames, off first (the catalog's 2f/16t `fotostopp`, its frame chosen from the core counter like the selfie flash). (3) The **lane lint** also covers the bus's own 4 cells, counts one-way tiles, and is clamped at the level's left edge; one problem per bus. A bus whose photo stop would lie left of the level's left edge is a lint problem too. (4) Walkers turn at a standing bus (parked, telegraphing, stopped) like at a parked Fiaker, not at a driving one. (5) A **roof bounce while parked** can't happen in play: the trigger is horizontal (< 161 px centre to centre), and landing on the roof needs < 36 px, so the bus is telegraphing by then; it is covered by a unit test only, the replays cover telegraph, drive, photo stop and restart. (6) Until the `reisebus` art arrives the renderer draws a grey box (white, a dark window band, blinking `Y` hazard lights while it telegraphs, white flashes on the photo stop's flash frames). Test level `00_m4_bus` (id `m4_bus`) with `m4_bus_*` replays, separate from the milestone's planned `00_m4_enemies`.

## D-071 The Hackl in Stage 5 (game-designer, M4 planning)
- Stage 5 is the first stage a player can enter **as Hackl** (carried over from Falkenstein, D-056). It gets a purpose without becoming required:
  - **Targets that suit the axe**: Schneekugeln (ground rollers: the descending arc meets them ~6–7 tiles ahead, a skill shot), street singers (hit at hand height; no need to wait out the high C), the pilgrim pair approaching along a clear lane on the church terrace.
  - **What it can't do**: buses are immune (bounce), the singer on balcony B1 is out of the arc from the street, the train needs no help. So the Hackl makes the stage faster and safer but never removes its timing.
  - **Its own `H`-box** on the church terrace (col 194, row 8, after the checkpoint) with „Hier landete / das Hackl": the legend says the saint's axe landed where the church was built. Every player gets the Hackl for the stage's second half; a Hackl arrival scores 1000 there (D-056).
- **Fair without it**: every threat is passable by jumping, stomping, waiting or a safe spot; there is no Hackl-only passage and no Hackl-only bonus.
- **Axe classification** for the new kinds (D-060's exhaustive `axe_hit` match must name them): Operettensänger **Defeat** (200, „Bravo!"), his column **Pass**, Schneekugel **Defeat** (100, drawn as a shatter), Souvenirstand **Pass**, Reisebus **Bounce**, Schafbergbahn **Pass** (a platform).

## D-072 Route-map labels around St. Wolfgang (game-designer, M4 planning)
- St. Wolfgang becomes playable, so three known label collisions of the map test (D-060's `LATER` and `NORMAL_TOUCH` lists) appear on real screens and are fixed in M4:
  1. **„St. Wolfgang" vs Resi on Wolfgangsee** (the new teaser screen St. Wolfgang → Wolfgangsee, every form) and 2. **vs Resi on Strobl** (screen Wolfgangsee → Strobl, from M5). St. Wolfgang gets an **alternate slot**: the same x (right of its dot), top edge raised from y 80 to **y 69**. It is used on screens whose end dot is Wolfgangsee or Strobl, **for every form** (Normal Resi on the Wolfgangsee dot overlaps the primary too). Checked against the map test's box rule: big Resi on Wolfgangsee (top y 78) clears the label outline (rows 68–78, exclusive end), and so does Strobl; the label stays clear of Falkenstein's (x ≤ 173 vs ≥ 188) and of Resi on the St. Wolfgang dot.
  3. **„Falkenstein" vs Normal Resi on St. Wolfgang** (the `NORMAL_TOUCH` pair, approved in M2 while that screen was only a teaser): the screen Falkenstein → St. Wolfgang now ends in Wait with Resi standing there for up to 5 s, so Falkenstein's existing alternate slot (ending at x 170) is used on that screen **for every form**, not only for a big Resi.
- How the slot choice is expressed (e.g. a per-station "any form" flag next to `label_alt`) is the engine's call. Screens that end elsewhere keep their primary slots, so every existing route-map golden that doesn't end on St. Wolfgang, Wolfgangsee or Strobl stays bit-identical. The screens that change are re-cut in M4 anyway (the Falkenstein teaser becomes the Wait screen; the St. Wolfgang teaser is new). The two pairs go out of `LATER` and the Falkenstein pair out of `NORMAL_TOUCH`. Bad Ischl's and Café Zauner's pairs stay for M6/M7. Exact pixels may move by 1–2 px in the art review (art-director), as long as the map test holds.
- **Addendum (engine, M4 task E21): St. Wolfgang's alternate slot is at top y 59, not 69.** At y 69 the raised label (from x 189) overlaps „Café Zauner" (fixed at x 232, top 69) on every map screen, which the map test catches. y 59 is the top closest to the dot that clears it (outline rows 58–67 vs Café Zauner's from 68) and stays clear of Resi on Wolfgangsee and Strobl in every form and of Falkenstein's label. The slot choice is a per-station list `Station::label_alt_ends` (end station ids whose screens use `label_alt` for every form): Falkenstein `["st_wolfgang"]`, St. Wolfgang `["wolfgangsee", "strobl"]`; the big-Resi rule is unchanged otherwise. `LATER` keeps only Bad Ischl's and Café Zauner's pairs, `NORMAL_TOUCH` only Brunnwinkl's. The art-director should review the raised label when the St. Wolfgang → Wolfgangsee teaser is cut.

## D-073 M4 acceptance: Stage 5 deviations and the window target (game-designer, M4 acceptance)
- **The "≥ 3 required jumps ≤ 8" target is dropped** (D-066, game-design.md "Difficulty and reach rules (stage 5)"). As built, the narrowest window is 5 (the Seebad leap), which meets the 5–7 target and sits on the gate. The next windows are 11 (the gorge leap), 12 and 14 (bus #2). Reasons: (1) the window list belongs to the solver's route, not to the layout, and it moves with every small edit (trials gave singer-#2 jumps from 2 to 25 and Seebad leaps of 4–5), so a count target can only be met by luck and keeps endangering the min-5 gate; (2) the step above Falkenstein (narrowest 8) is delivered by what the metric does not measure: the singer's 92-frame window under a hurting column, bus #2's go-stop-go, the globe + tourist lane, the lethal gorge under the train, and four new kinds in one stage; (3) the stage stays fair and readable for a relaxed player (87.4 s, the two-jump Seebad line). From Stage 6 on, difficulty targets name the **narrowest window** (with the gate) and the **hazard timings** to be tuned, not a count of tight jumps. Honest note: the 5-frame Seebad leap is an expert line; the relaxed route's tightest jumps are ~11+.
- **Accepted deviations** (as listed in the D-066 addendum):
  1. **Train shortcut**: the running jump off the moving roof to the 2-wide shelf at 78–79 once the front is over col ~68. It is a risk-for-time trade over a lethal gorge, which suits an expert line. Riding the whole way stays the safe, default reading.
  2. **Hop Oblaten as a rising line** (68–75, 29–45 px over the roof): it rewards 1–2 tap hops from the uphill half (D-067's measured drift) and keeps the coins lower than the spec's arc. While she rides the lower half of the track, the far end of the line still shows behind the HUD (LEBEN/ZEIT, shots `st_wolfgang_tour_zug`, `st_wolfgang_zug_gorge_hop`). That is transient and non-blocking (art-director's call).
  3. **Seebad quay one tile higher**: the relaxed line (two jumps via the 4-wide middle jetty) keeps ≥ 2-wide landings and one hazard (the water); only the leap over both gaps is tight.
  4. **Alm hump at 98** and **tourist #3 penned at the Markt entrance**: both keep a walker out of a bus trigger or a respawn lane. They are better than the spec, which had put a walker behind a charger. The lost „they got off somewhere" joke is fine; the bus park reads without it.
  5. **`M`-box at 156** (6 columns after the checkpoint, still within 10).
  6. **Rössl geometry** (B1 217–220 with singer #1 at 219, B2 223–228, Kaiserterrasse 233–242 with decorative posts): every spec rule holds (B1 sings into the sky over the street, the gap B2 → terrace stays 4, the Zaunerstollen stays penned). The name is real: the hotel's restaurant is „Poll's Kaiserterrasse" (game-design.md Stage 5 sources).
  7. **The train leaves again at once when it arrives at the lower end with a rider.** No softlock: she can step off onto the rock steps while it is low, or ride again. Riding down is no goal of the stage (the route goes right). A short hold at the lower end for an arriving rider would be nicer, but it is an engine change that would also touch the Festungsbahn's shared state machine, so it goes to the backlog and is not M4 work.
- No player-visible text changes. All Stage 5 texts were checked for D-052 double readings: „auch voll" stays, as on Salzburg's checkpoint („Bus 150 / auch voll"), because for a bus it reads only as "full". The „100 % voll" variant stays excluded.

## D-074 M5 scope: Stage 6 Wolfgangsee, its shape and difficulty (game-designer, M5 planning)
- M5 is **one stage**, `06_wolfgangsee` (260 × 17 tiles, `camera = "autoscroll"`, zone `[8, 208]`). It brings two systems, the **auto-scroll camera** (D-075) and **swimming** in a new, non-lethal kind of water (D-076). It also brings two boats, the **Traundl** and the **Dampfer** (D-077), and three enemies: the **Schwan** (D-078), the **Gelse** (D-079) and the **Regattaboot** (D-080). Each new kind again extends an existing system: Gondel → Traundl (same machine, new size), Festungsbahn → Dampfer (waiting machine, one way, plus a hurt zone like the high C), Reisebus → Regattaboot (same machine on the water), the Fiaker/E-Biker charge → the swan's short charge, the Taube's free flight → the Gelse's homing.
