The two systems are the milestone's real cost, so the enemy set stays at three kinds.
- **Enemy set**: Schwan, Gelse, Regattaboot. **Not in M5** (backlog): the **water skier** (a fourth charger, towed by an off-screen boat: the Regattaboot already holds the charger slot; water skiing on the lake is real, St. Gilgen offers it); the **sailing boat as a platform** whose boom swings across the deck at each tack („Klar zur Wende!", a platform with a periodic hurt zone; the paddle wheel proves the hurt-zone part first); a multi-deck steamer (sun deck as a second surface: the platform code has one top); a breath meter; an under-water tint for Resi; swans that dabble (a downward peck); seagulls; the St. Wolfgang – Abersee ferry and the Plätte shown at the Strobl Wassbad as props; a sign on the steamer's Ischlerbahn years (1898–1957); the Weißes Rössl's floating jacuzzi (M4 extra 4).
- **Shape**: side view, 17 rows, a lake crossing. The camera follows Resi on the St. Wolfgang quay and from the Strobl quay on (lead-in and lead-out), and scrolls on its own over the water. Decks are on row 11, the water surface on row 12, 4 tiles of water, the lake bed on row 16, and the goal meadow on row 16 as in every stage (D-029/D-041 framing unchanged). **There is no lethal pit in the stage**: every water column is swimmable over a lake bed. The danger is moving things on a moving screen.
- **Stylised route**: St. Wolfgang Markt landing → the steamer to Gschwendt (a real stop) → open water → the floating Advent lantern (checkpoint) → Traundl ferries and the state border → a regatta → the Strobl landing. Ending at Strobl's landing hall hands over to Stage 7.
- **Difficulty**: medium, a step above St. Wolfgang through reading moving hazards while the screen keeps going, not through tighter jumps. **`min_jump_window = 5`** stays (D-066 allowed 4 from Stage 6; with two new systems and five new kinds, the gate isn't moved). Design target in the D-073 style: narrowest required window 5–8, and the named hazard timings (swan telegraph/charge, Gelse wait/speed, regatta starts, Traundl phases). Rules: at most two hazards can reach Resi at once, always with an escape (dive, deck or open water). Gelsen and Regattaboote never share a screen. Every enemy is on screen ≥ 60 frames before it can touch her. Every deck in the zone is a one-way tile or a boat, 1 tile above the water and ≥ 2 wide. The full table is in game-design.md ("Difficulty and reach rules (stage 6)").
- **Exception to D-032**, after both are introduced alone: a regatta lane may cross a Traundl's track (sec. 6). Reason: jumping the sail from a boat is the section's best move, and falling off is a swim, not a death. The first Traundl and the steamer keep D-032 (swan #1 passes under the steamer's deck).
- **Numbers**: `time_limit` per D-045 (provisional 270: relaxed ~90 s, of which ~71 s is the scroll); Oblaten 70 ± 10; `M`-box at the start (col 9) and within 10 columns after the checkpoint (col 129); the Zaunerstollen on an optional perch in the Traundl section; no `H`-box (D-081); checkpoint at col 122 on the lantern pontoon.
- **Running gags**: Bus 150 on two adjacent signs (D-039): „Bus 150 / wasserscheu" at the start (it doesn't swim, so Resi does) → „Bus 150 / im Stau. Ha!" beside the checkpoint (seen on the south-shore road from the lake, where for once Resi is faster). „Linienschiff / auch voll" (St. Gilgen) is answered: the old steamer takes her, max. 100 passengers or not. The pigeon on a monument is skipped: the lake has no monument within reach (the lantern's top is under the HUD, D-041 says "where there is one"). Jokes target the steamer's age and its missing steam, the swans' sense of ownership (they arrived in 1950), Gelsen, regatta etiquette, the moving state border and Strobl's through traffic. Faith and people are not the joke; the sailors and the rower are friendly figures.
- **Route**: shipping `06_wolfgangsee.level` (+ its `options::LEVELS` line) makes the Wolfgangsee playable (D-047). The teaser moves to Wolfgangsee → Strobl. The M4 replays and goldens that end in the St. Wolfgang teaser now end on the route map in Wait or on the Wolfgangsee intro, and must be re-cut and re-approved (as in D-053/D-066). **No route-map label decision is needed**: D-072 already gives St. Wolfgang its alternate slot on screens ending at Wolfgangsee or Strobl, and the map test already checks every screen and form, with only Bad Ischl's and Café Zauner's pairs on its `LATER` list. If the art review finds a clash on the two new screens anyway, the D-072 pattern (an alternate slot per end station) applies.

### D-074 addendum: Stage 6 as built (level-designer, M5)
- `content/levels/06_wolfgangsee.level` follows the seven sections of game-design.md with these deviations (for the game-designer's review): (1) **regatta boats at 198, 204, 210** (spec 196, 202, 208) and **Gelse #3 at 171** (spec 172): measured, Gelse #3's hunt ends at frame ~3713 and the first boat's hitbox shows at ~3740 (the spec's "move the start line 1–2 columns right"). (2) **Gelse #2 at 160** (spec 162, over B's far end instead of the B/C gap) and **swan #3 at 184** (spec 178): with the spec's positions two hunting Gelsen and swan #3 could reach her together for ~150 frames; now swan #3 reaches the clamp line (~3499) after Gelse #2's hunt ends (~3478). The Zaunerstollen rolls right first, turns at the bollard and comes back over Resi standing under the box; if she has left, it falls into the water (optional bonus). ## D-075 The auto-scroll camera (game-designer, M5 planning)
- Levels may choose `[meta] camera = "autoscroll"` with **`autoscroll = [from, to]`** (columns, required with this camera, a parse error otherwise; `0 ≤ from < to ≤ width − 20`). The zone is where the camera's left edge lies in `[from × 16, to × 16)`. Before it (**lead-in**) and after it (**lead-out**) the camera is the side camera, unchanged. Vertically it is always the side camera.
- **Scroll**: when following would put the camera at or past `from × 16`, it is set to exactly that and from then on moves right by `camera.autoscroll_speed = 192` subpx (**0.75 px/frame**) every frame of play (not frozen, paused, dying or in the goal sequence), independent of the player; the camera's x is the whole-px floor of the subpixel scroll position. At `to × 16` the side camera takes over (never left).
- **Push and squeeze** (SMB3 rule): in the scroll, after the camera and the platforms moved, a hitbox left of the camera's x is moved right by the difference with normal tile collision. If a solid tile stops that move, she dies (`DeathCause::Squeezed`, drawn as the normal hit death). **D-051 is off in the scroll**: a platform carrying her left past the edge pushes her across its deck instead of moving the camera. Outside the zone D-051 applies as before.
- **Right clamp**: in the scroll her hitbox's right edge stays ≤ camera x + 320 − `camera.autoscroll_right_margin = 64`; her movement and any carry stop there like at a wall. Without it she could outrun the screen. With 64 px, she always sees ≥ 4 tiles ahead and anything entering the screen is ≥ 64 px from her.
- **Start, respawn, goal**: framed as in side levels. If the framing lies inside the zone, the scroll resumes after `camera.autoscroll_hold = 60` frames of play (a calm respawn on a checkpoint in the zone); the lead-in → scroll transition never holds. The goal column must be ≥ `to + 20` (lint), so the goal sequence always uses the side camera.
- **Visibility rule for enemies in a scroll zone**: triggers that start a threat also need the enemy **fully on screen** (the swan's and the regatta boat's triggers, the Gelse's wait counter), so with the right clamp every threat is in view ≥ 60 frames before it can touch her. Activation is unchanged (one screen ahead). In the scroll, the camera doesn't depend on the player, so activations happen at fixed scroll positions and boat phases can be tuned exactly.
- **Lints** (`levels lint`): goal ≥ `to + 20`; **squeeze faces**: every left-facing solid face in the zone's columns has its top ≤ 3 tiles above the surface in front of it (deck, water surface or lake bed), so the edge can always push her onto it; the checkpoint in a zone stands on a deck tile (solid or one-way), not on water.
- **Solver**: its frame budget covers the scroll (zone length in px / speed, plus the usual budget for the rest); `world_hash` covers the scroll phase, position and hold. Fallback (as D-054's): if the beam can't hold a route through the zone, the zone's jumps are marked route-limited and covered by handwritten replays (record it).
- **Tuning validation**: `0 < autoscroll_speed < swim.speed`, hold 0..=600, margin 0..=160, and the Dampfer at least as fast as the scroll (D-077).
- **Reasons**: a lake crossing has a pace of its own, and a scroll makes the boats' timing a timeline the designer controls, where the side camera would leave it to the player's dawdling. Pushing instead of killing at the edge (SMB3) is forgiving, and the squeeze death only exists where a wall is. Stage 6 designs every wall out (no solid tiles on rows 11–12 over water), so the rule is there for correctness and later stages. 0.75 px/frame is half the walk speed and 0.5 px/frame slower than swimming, so every way of moving can gain on the edge. Keeping the zone off the start and the goal leaves the intro screen, the goal sequence and the D-029 framing untouched.
- **Fallback** (engine's call, record it): ship the stage as `camera = "side"`. The level is built left to right and stays fair; boats and enemies then wake by the player's progress, and push, squeeze, clamp and hold go unused.
- **Not taken**: a scroll that also moves vertically (not needed on a lake); a scroll speed set per level (one tuning value is enough for one stage; a per-level override can come when a second auto-scroll stage needs it); killing at the left edge without a wall (harsher than SMB3 for no gain); a zone covering the whole level (the start would push Resi before she has seen anything, and the goal camera would need new rules).

## D-076 Swimming (game-designer, M5 planning)
- **Swim water is a new tile kind** (`TileKind::Swim`; tiles `see_wasser` = surface look, `see_wasser_tief` = deep, `steg_pfahl` = water with a pier post). The existing water tiles stay lethal (D-050), so Stages 1–5 are unchanged, and future stages can mix both. Swim water is drawn clear, with the lake bed showing through (the real lake's 8 m visibility), and Stage 6 teaches it on its first screen (sign „Baden heute / erlaubt", a bathing notch with Oblaten under water). **Not taken**: a level-wide "water is swimmable" flag (it would silently change what an existing tile means, and a level could never have both).
- **Swimming** = her chest point (hitbox centre x, 8 px below her hitbox top, every form) is in a Swim tile. **At the surface** = within 8 px below a swim surface (a Swim tile with no Swim above). She can't swim her chest above the surface; only a jump-out leaves upwards.
- **Physics** (tuning `[swim]`): floating at the surface (no gravity) unless DOWN is held; otherwise gravity 16 (0.0625 px/frame²), max sink 256 (1.0); a new JUMP press below the surface band strokes (vy −448, ~24 px up, `Event::Stroke`); a new JUMP press at the surface is a **jump-out** with `jump_speed_walk` (`Event::Jumped`, normal airborne rules after it); horizontal accel 16, decel 8, max 320 (1.25 px/frame, RUN no faster); on entering, vy is clamped to [−stroke, max sink] and the jump buffer and coyote are cleared. The lake bed is ground (walk at swim speed, JUMP strokes). No breath meter.
- **DOWN gets a third meaning** (after drop-through, D-011, and the crawl, D-057): held at the surface, it lets Resi sink. Reason: floating at the surface keeps a relaxed swimmer from having to tap, which SMB's always-sinking swim demands; diving then needs one intentional input, and DOWN = down is the obvious one. Under water DOWN does nothing more (no dive boost: one gravity value is easier to read and to test).
- **While swimming**: every enemy contact hurts (no stomps, no roof bounces; a jump-out is airborne from its next frame and can stomp); RUN throws no Hackl; axes and power-up items touching swim water are removed or lost as in water. Walking enemies treat Swim like Water. Entity-on-tile (D-049) is extended to Swim tiles, so Oblaten, swans and regatta boats can be placed in water cells.
- **Same numbers in every form** (only the hitbox height differs). A held jump-out reaches a deck 1 tile above the water in every form (Normal feet ~53 px above the surface, big ~45 px); a tap doesn't. That is the level rule "decks 1 tile up" and a feel test.
- **The solver's window metric** counts `Jumped` (incl. jump-outs) and never `Stroke`.
- **Lint**: every swim column ends on a solid tile inside the level; in a scroll zone, no solid tile sits directly on a swim surface.
- **Reasons**: SMB's water levels are the reference (sink, stroke, slow horizontal movement), with two changes for a lake you swim on more than in: the surface holds her, and a jump-out from the surface is a normal jump, so boats are boarded with the same skill as a jump onto a ledge. The dive is the answer to the stage's surface and air enemies (swans, regatta boats, Gelsen), which gives swimming a purpose beyond "not dying".
- **Fallback** (engine's call, record it): **surface-only swimming**. The chest point is held at the surface (no sinking, no strokes, DOWN unused), and JUMP is the jump-out. The level then gives every swan a deck within its charge range and every regatta lane a Traundl, since diving is gone. Lethal water and all other rules stay.

### D-076 addendum: swimming as built (engine, M5 task E27)
- **Full swimming, the fallback (surface-only) is not taken**: every rule of D-076 is implemented with the designer's numbers (`[swim]`: `chest_px` 8, `surface_band` 8, `gravity` 16, `max_sink` 256, `stroke_speed` 448, `accel` 16, `decel` 8, `speed` 320), measured by the feel tests (stroke rise 23.6 px, max sink 1.0, 12 px of sinking in 20 frames, 1.25 px/frame with and without RUN, a held jump-out lands on a 1-tile deck or quay in every form and a tap doesn't; jump-out feet heights Normal 53/11 px, big 45/3 px within ±4).
- **Engine choices the spec left open**: (1) Swimming is decided once per step from the position before the move (`Player::swimming`); the rise cap then holds the chest at the surface line after the move. (2) A jump-out sets `Player::leaving_water`: until she stops rising she follows the normal airborne rules even while her chest is still in the water (otherwise the cap would catch the jump-out in its first frames); this is what "airborne from its next frame" means in code. (3) Floating keeps the depth at which she stopped (vy 0 anywhere in the band, no snap to the line); falling in, the entry clamp and the float rule stop her on her first swimming step unless DOWN is held. A stroke from below ends exactly on the line. (4) A new press on the very step that enters the water is a real press (a jump-out at the surface); only presses buffered before it are dropped. (5) In swim water the coyote frames stay 0, also on the bed. (6) The surface over the chest is the top of the run of swim tiles it is in, so under a solid tile (a box in the water) that tile's underside counts as the surface; the zone lint keeps solids off swim surfaces where it matters.
- **What is lost in swim water** (D-076 "as in water"): items, thrown axes, E-Bikers and snow globes via `TileMap::swallows` (Hazard, Water, Swim; `touches_lethal`, still Hazard and Water, only guards the roof-bounce placement, since swim water is no danger to Resi). Walking enemies would sink out of the level in lethal water but meet a lake bed here, so they are **removed at the touch** of a swim tile (`EnemyKind::sinks_in_swim_water`, an exhaustive match: the M5 kinds of E29 must classify themselves; the Taube, the Bienenschwarm and the tile-ignoring Fiaker and Reisebus don't sink).
- **Unchanged**: lethal water (D-050 tests pass unchanged), every earlier replay, solver replay and golden. State hashes change only because the tuning gained `[swim]` and the player three hashed fields (`swimming`, `leaving_water`, `swim_frames`); the testbed's pinned finish hash was re-pinned (route and frame unchanged).
- **Solver**: the window metric skips a JUMP hold whose press swims a `Stroke`; jump-outs count like jumps. Its search and recovery need nothing new for the grey box `00_m5_swim`; the scroll zone over swim water is E30's.
- **Integration with E25/E26/E28/E29** (merge of `m5-wolfgangsee`): `Tuning::validate_swim` now also requires `camera.autoscroll_speed < swim.speed`; `levels lint` passes `swim::problems` the columns the scroll shows (`from` to `to − 1 + 20`, the camera's left edge lying in `[from × 16, to × 16)`). The Schwan, the Gelse and the Regattaboot don't sink in swim water. `steg_pfahl`'s art is an `auto` 16-frame autotile on its own name (N: pile above, S: pile below; its frames ignore E/W), so no autotile family rule was needed (render test). Left for E30: the contact rule while swimming goes through E29's `enemies::contact(kind, from_above, swimming)`, fed by the `Game::player_swimming()` stub (still `false`); the two contact tests (`every_enemy_contact_hurts_while_swimming`, `no_roof_bounce_while_swimming`) are `#[ignore]`d until it returns `Player::swimming`, as are the `// E27: | Swim` markers of E28/E29.

## D-077 Boats: Traundl and Dampfer (game-designer, M5 planning)
- **Traundl** (`traundl`, `to = [dx, 0]`, 2 ≤ |dx| ≤ 8, dy 0, optional `phase`): the Gondel's free-running ping-pong (D-044/D-048) with its own size and keys (`traundl_size` 48×16 body, `traundl_wait` 30, `traundl_frames_per_tile` 16; a 48×32 sprite with the standing rower). Body left-aligned in its spawn cell on row 11, bottom on the water line, deck flush with every row-11 deck. No cable: the level marks both track ends with a `dalben` (mooring pile) prop. The real boat is the **Plätte**, also called Traunerl or Fuhre. „Traundl" is the game's established name (the satire skill's glossary), so the entity keeps it, and no sign uses either name.
- **Dampfer** (`dampfer`, `to = [dx, 0]`, 8 ≤ dx ≤ 64, dy 0): the Festungsbahn's waiting machine (D-030/D-034), **one way**. It waits at its spawn for a rider, travels in dx × `dampfer_frames_per_tile` = 20 frames (0.8 px/frame, slightly faster than the scroll, so a standing rider is never pushed), then stays **moored** at the far end until the level resets. Body 160×16 (10 tiles; the real ship is 33 m), deck flush with the quays. The sprite is 160×48 with exactly one walking surface, the deck line. The funnel, the flagstaff and the railings must not read as surfaces (D-038). There is no sun-deck awning: it would be a second surface the platform code can't carry.
- **Paddle wheel**: while the Dampfer travels, a hurt rectangle 32×20 px at the hull's middle (from 4 px above the water line to 16 px below it) hurts the player on contact, never stomped, never an axe target. It is part of the platform, derived from its state each frame like the singer's high C (D-068). A rider never touches it, a surface swimmer does, and a diver passes under it. While the Dampfer waits or is moored, the wheel is still and the zone is off.
- **Both boats**: landing, carrying, crush and never-despawn are the shared platform code; the 3-tile clearance lint applies; the pit-recovery lint is skipped over swim water (a fall is a swim). Swimmers pass under boats and jump up through their one-way decks.
- **Reasons**: two kinds cover the lake's two readings, the calm big ride and the small boats that don't wait. Both reuse machines that have been through three milestones of QA. A waiting steamer can't be missed, so the scroll starts on a deck where standing still is safe. One way, because a returning steamer would carry a lingering rider back against the scroll (pushed across its deck, then into the water) for no gain.
- **Fallbacks** (engine's call, record it): (1) the paddle wheel is decoration only (no hurt zone), and the stage loses one swimmer hazard; (2) if the one-way variant is costly, the Dampfer returns like the Festungsbahn after a long `dampfer_wait` (the level then puts a pier at its far end, and a rider who stays is pushed off by the edge); (3) the Traundl falls back to a `gondel` with the Traundl sprite and size if the per-kind size is costly.
- **Not taken**: boats that bob (a moving deck height would change every landing; the renderer may bob the rower and the ripples, never the deck); sailing boats as platforms (D-074 backlog); boats that inherit speed into jumps (no platform does).
- **Addendum (engine, M5 task E28): as built.** No fallback taken: the wheel hurts, the Dampfer goes one way, the Traundl is its own kind. Syntax and numbers as specified; tuning `platforms.traundl_size = [48, 16]`, `traundl_wait = 30`, `traundl_frames_per_tile = 16`, `dampfer_size = [160, 16]`, `dampfer_frames_per_tile = 20`, `dampfer_wheel_zone = [32, 20]`, `dampfer_wheel_rise = 4` (validated: the Traundl's cycle fits the u16 counter as the Gondel's; the Dampfer's hull whole tiles, ≤ 320 × 64 px; ≤ 64 frames per tile; the Dampfer at least as fast as the scroll, 4096 / frames per tile ≥ 192 subpx/frame, i.e. ≤ 21 frames per tile, checked against `camera.autoscroll_speed` (E26 wired it in place of the former stub, D-083); the wheel zone ≤ the hull's width × 64 px, its rise 0..=its height). Engine calls: (1) **hash tags** `Traundl` 24, `Dampfer` 25. (2) The one-way machine is a flag on the shared timing (`CarTiming::moor`) and a fifth car state `car::MOORED` (`count` 0, height fraction = the far end), so the Festungsbahn and the Schafbergbahn are unchanged; a moored Dampfer's `world_hash` is constant. (3) The **wheel hurts any player overlapping it** while the Dampfer travels (the spec's swimmer case until E27; a rider's feet are on the deck, 12 px above the zone's top (game-design.md says 16), so a rider never touches it). It is checked in `Game::interact` before the stomp rule, like the high C. (4) The **recovery lint skip** is generic: a column whose first non-air tile below the track's top is water (`Water`, E27: `| Swim`) has no floor, and an exit column over water passes. (5) The renderer never mirrors either boat (the catalog: the Traundl rows bow first both ways, the Dampfer only goes right); the Traundl shows `ride` while its position changes. (6) **Grey boxes** until the boats package lands: Traundl `l` hull with a 6×14 `G` rower stub at x 38 of the hull; Dampfer `w` hull with an 8×20 `o` funnel stub at x 70 and the wheel zone outlined `d` while it turns; `gischt` fallback: two 4×3 white boxes just under the water line, ±8 px around the wheel's centre. Tiles `steg`, `kai`, `seegrund`, `poller` have no sprites yet and draw as grey boxes by kind. (7) Test level `00_m5_boats` (lethal `wolfgangsee_water` stands in for swim water; a dry dock under the first part of the Dampfer's way lets a player meet the wheel) with replays `m5_boats_dampfer_ride`, `m5_boats_wheel_hurt`, `m5_boats_moored`, `m5_boats_traundl_chain` and `m5_boats_solver` (553 frames, narrowest window 10: the solver runs along the moving deck and jumps off its bow to the pier, as on the Schafbergbahn). No golden shots.

## D-078 The Schwan (game-designer, M5 planning)
- **Schwan** (`schwan`, 24×24 / 16×12): floats on the surface (spawned in a surface-row swim cell, hitbox bottom 4 px below the water line, so its top is 8 px below the decks). Territorial: trigger when the player is within 96 px (6 tiles) **and** the swan is fully on screen and rested → **telegraph 32** frames (wings arched, neck back: the hiss) → **charge** 1.5 px/frame for 64 frames (96 px) towards the side the player was on → **return** home at 0.5 → **rest 60** frames. Ignores tiles at its float height; levels keep its path free of solid tiles and away from piers and reeds.
- **Contact**: from the side, and any contact while Resi swims, hurts. A deck rider is out of its reach (the safe introduction: swan #1 charges under the steamer's deck). A diver ≥ 1 tile down passes under it. **Stompable** from the air (incl. a jump-out): 200, chain start 200 (D-046). It sulks (`squished`, head under the wing) and the popup „Beleidigt!" (`popup.beleidigt`) stacks above the score popup, the „Bravo!" mechanism (D-068). Axe: Defeat (200, „Beleidigt!").
- **Reasons**: mute swans really defend their stretch with arched wings and a hiss, and the lake's swans are newcomers from 1950 who act like the landlords („Seit 1950 / Hausherr"). A short charge from a home point is a readable pattern built from existing parts (a range trigger as for the Blasmusiker, a charge as for the Fiaker, a return like the swarm's anchor). It makes the dive useful and the deck a refuge.
- **Fallback** (engine's call): the swan paddles back and forth between two points (the Nordic-Walker's pacing on the water: ±3 tiles, 0.5 px/frame) without a charge. Contact rules, stomp and axe stay.
- **Not taken**: a swan that leaves the water and walks on decks (it would hunt riders too, and the deck would stop being a refuge); a swan that can't be defeated (it would block a swim lane for good); a swan that dabbles downwards (a hazard for divers, backlog).
- **Addendum (engine, M5 task E29): the Schwan as built.** As specified, no fallback taken. (1) **Placement**: `{ entity = "schwan", tile = "wolfgangsee_water" }`: the swan is the one entity whose cell may hold a water tile (until E27's swim water lands, then the swim tile; E30 switches it); hitbox 16×12 centred in its cell, bottom `schwan_float_px` = 4 under the cell's top. (2) **Counting** follows the Fiaker: the trigger frame is telegraph frame 1 (32 frames), the charge moves on the next frame (64 moves, 96 px), the return clamps its last step so it arrives exactly at `base_x` (192 moves), the arrival frame is not a rest frame (60 rest frames), so with Resi in range it triggers again exactly 348 frames after the first trigger. It faces Resi at home and while resting; the charge direction is fixed at the trigger. "Rested" = the `HOME` state (it starts rested). (3) **Contact**: the generic rule through `enemies::contact` (stomp from the air, hurt from the side, every contact hurts while swimming); the swimming query is a stub (`Game::player_swimming` = false) until E27, so the deck refuge and the diver pass are proved by geometry tests. (4) „Beleidigt!" stacks above the score popup for every defeat (stomp and axe; a bumped block can't reach it): `enemies::defeat_popup`, the „Bravo!" mechanism. Tuning `enemies.schwan_*` (hitbox, float_px, range 96, telegraph 32, charge_speed 384, charge_frames 64, return_speed 128, rest 60), validated. Grey box until the art lands: white, two white wing posts while it hisses.

## D-079 The Gelse (game-designer, M5 planning)
- **Gelse** (`gelse`, 16×16 / 8×8): **hover** from activation until it has been fully on screen for 60 frames (the telegraph) → **hunt** for 480 frames: per axis its velocity changes by 8 subpx (1/32 px/frame²) towards her target (her hitbox centre x, her top + 4 px), clamped to ±0.75 px/frame. It overshoots and loops, which leaves dodge windows → **leave**: harmless, straight up at 1 px/frame, removed above the screen. It ignores every tile except water: no part of it ever enters a water or swim tile (the move is cut at the tile's top and that velocity zeroed). So it **waits at the water line above a diver** and stings when she surfaces under it. Hurts on contact; stompable (100, a normal chain stomp); axe: Defeat (100).
- **Reasons**: Gelsen are the lake's summer reality and an Austrian word. A slow homing flyer is the first enemy that follows Resi, so it is the stage's reason to dive and to swim away under water. The limited hunt keeps several Gelsen from piling up into an unfair swarm, and the wait in view keeps them fair on a scrolling screen. Its homing is deterministic (it reads only the player's position).
- **Fallback** (engine's call): no homing. The Gelse flies left on the Taube's integer sine (D-026) at its spawn height, never entering water, and the wait, stomp and axe rules stay.
- **Not taken**: a Gelsen swarm spawner from the reeds (a third spawner type, and more on screen than the "two hazards" rule allows); a cloud that can't be stomped (the bee swarm already is one); a Gelse that drains Oblaten instead of hurting (a new damage type for one enemy).
- **Addendum (engine, M5 task E29): the Gelse as built.** Homing as specified, no fallback taken. (1) **Placement**: 8×8 hitbox centred in its cell (no fields). (2) **Wait**: `count` counts only frames it is fully on screen (cumulative, pausing off screen); on the 60th it switches to the hunt, which moves from the next frame. (3) **Hunt**: per axis `v += 8 × sign(target − its centre)`, clamped to ±192, then the move (x first, then y); target = her hitbox centre x and her top + `gelse_target_dy` = 4. A move whose new hitbox overlaps a water tile is cut at that tile's edge (top for a move down) and that velocity set to 0 (`enemies::move_clear_of_water`; `wolfgangsee_water` until E27, marked `// E27: | Swim`). After 480 hunt frames it leaves: straight up at `gelse_leave_speed` = 1 px/frame, harmless (`Entity::harmful` false, so axes pass too), removed once its bottom is above the screen. (4) Stomp 100 (a normal chain stomp; it is squished where it was hit, in mid-air, for `squish_frames`, not tumbling like a Taube), axe Defeat (100, tumbling). (5) Renderer: `wait` while hovering with a ±2 px bob (`entities::gelse_bob_px`), `fly` hunting, `leave`; grey box: a dark `k` box until the art lands. New `UpdateCtx::player_top`.

## D-080 The Regattaboot (game-designer, M5 planning)
- **Regattaboot** (`regattaboot`, 32×64 / 24×56, no fields): **the Reisebus's machine on the water** (D-070). Placed in the left of its 2 cells in the surface row (sprite bottom on that cell's bottom). Its hitbox runs from the masthead (28 px above the decks) to the keel (12 px below the water line). Trigger: the player within 10 tiles (< 161 px to its centre) **and** the boat fully on screen → 60-frame telegraph (start flag, horn) → races **left at 1.5 px/frame**, ignoring tiles, passing enemies and platforms, never stopping; removed once its right edge is left of the screen. Contact from above (falling, feet above its middle on the previous frame, not swimming) bounces **without damage or points**. Any other contact hurts, also while parked, and always while swimming. A diver ≥ 13 px below the water line passes under it. Axe: Bounce. Lane lint as for the bus (no solid or one-way tile in its rows for 24 tiles left of the spawn).
- **Reasons**: the regatta as a charger reuses the bus with one new trigger condition (fully on screen, the D-075 visibility rule). It has three answers that use the stage's new verbs: dive under it, jump the sail from a Traundl, or bounce off the masthead. „Regatta / Vorrang: wir" makes right-of-way the joke and not the sailors.
- **Fallback** (engine's call): the Fiaker's machine (no photo stop is used anyway) with the same size, trigger and contact rules. If the on-screen condition is costly, the level places every regatta boat so that it is fully on screen before Resi can come within 10 tiles of it (checked by a replay at the right clamp).
- **Not taken**: boats that tack back and forth (a platform with a boom, D-074 backlog); a boat crew that can be stomped off (a second hitbox on one entity); boats racing right with the scroll (they would sit on screen for a long time and block the surface).
- **Addendum (engine, M5 task E29): the Regattaboot as built.** The Reisebus's machine, no fallback taken: `enemies::update_reisebus` with the `enemies.regattaboot_*` keys (trigger 160, telegraph 60, speed 384) and the extra trigger condition fully on screen; no `stop` field, so it never halts. Placement: the left of its 2 cells in the surface row; hitbox 24×56 centred under the 32 px sprite, its bottom `regattaboot_keel_px` = 12 under the cell's top (the masthead 28 px above the decks). Not stompable, so the generic contact rule gives the bus's roof bounce (no damage, no points) and hurts from the side, also parked. **Refinement (art-director, approved `regattaboot` sprite):** the drawn masthead is a 6 px cap, so the bounce comes only from a **masthead part** `regattaboot_mast` = 8×20 px centred on the hitbox from its top (`enemies::regatta_parts`); below it the **body** spans the full 24 px and hurts on any contact, also from above. Contact in the two 8×20 corners beside the mast part is ignored, so a player falling beside the cap passes it and meets the sail (hurt) instead of bouncing in mid-air; the from-above test uses the mast part's middle (10 px under the masthead), not the hitbox's. This makes the masthead bounce an 8 px target (game-designer to confirm in the M5 review; widening `regattaboot_mast` is a tuning change). The thrown Hackl still meets the whole 24×56 hitbox (it only bounces off anyway); while swimming every contact hurts (stub, see D-078 addendum). Walkers turn at it unless it races (like the bus). Lane lint: `reisebus_lane_problems` covers both kinds (message names `regattaboot` and D-080; water in the lane is fine). The renderer draws its sprite from the sprite's bottom (= the cell bottom, 4 px below the keel), never mirrored; grey box: cream with a `d` mast, a blinking `r` start flag while it telegraphs, a `Y` bow wave while it races. **Level note** (found in the grey box `00_m5_enemies`): "fully on screen" is also vertical; a perch high above its lane (row 8 over a surface on row 12) lets the side camera frame Resi so high that the keel is below the screen, and the boat then never starts. Stage 6 decks on row 11 keep the camera at rest, so this only matters for raised perches.

## D-081 The Hackl in Stage 6 (game-designer, M5 planning)
- **No `H`-box in Stage 6.** The legend places the Hackl at the Falkenstein and in St. Wolfgang (D-056/D-071), not on the lake, and a lake stage gives the axe few targets. A Resi who arrives as Hackl keeps it, and the `M`-boxes score 1000 for her.
- **What it does here**: from a deck, axes defeat swans (200, „Beleidigt!") and Gelsen (100). Regatta boats are immune (Bounce). The boats are platforms (Pass), and the paddle wheel is no entity. **No throwing while swimming** (a RUN press does nothing), and axes vanish in swim water. So the Hackl makes the decks stronger but never replaces the dive, and the stage is fair without it (every threat has a dive, deck or timing answer).
- **Classification** for the exhaustive `axe_hit` match (D-060): Schwan **Defeat**, Gelse **Defeat**, Regattaboot **Bounce**, Traundl **Pass**, Dampfer **Pass**.

## D-082 Verify runs independent work in parallel; the solver runs over the real levels only in `verify` (engine-engineer, E25)
- **Context**: `cargo xtask verify` had grown to ~7.5–8.5 min against the 10-min budget (test step ~5 min, solver ~2.3 min) and grew ~1 min per stage. Most of the test step was one test, `verify_fails_without_the_committed_solver_replay`, which solved every real level twice inside `cargo test` but accepted any result, so it never checked that a level is solvable.
- **Decision**: (1) that test checks the `solver` step's bookkeeping (missing replay, level order, a stuck level) on synthetic levels; the `solver` verify step alone solves the real levels, as before. (2) Independent items run on scoped threads with results joined in item order (`xtask/src/par.rs`), so reports are identical for any thread count: the replays (`replays` step and its test; both determinism runs of a replay stay on one thread), the golden shots, and the solver's levels. (3) The `solver` step runs in two passes. Pass 1: every level's search, the only part with a wall-clock limit, largest level first, never more busy threads than cores: about 7 threads per level, at most 8 (14 cores: 2 levels at a time × 7). Pass 2, after all searches: route clean-up and jump windows (no clock) for every level at once on all cores, then the reports in level order. (4) Solver test output goes to per-process folders (`tmp/xtask-solver-test/<pid>/`), so a `check` and a `verify` at the same time no longer delete each other's files.
- **Measured** (14 cores, warm build): `verify` 500 s → 93–141 s depending on other agents' load (test 320 s cold / ~5 min warm → 10–15 s; solver 136 s → 60–105 s). The slowest search is 16.5–18.2 s (two runs) at a load average of 20–35, ≥ 3.3× below the 60 s limit; 4 levels × 3 threads with the windows overlapping other levels' searches had given 21–25 s. All 17 solved routes equal the committed solver replays.
- **Not taken**: caching solver results by content hash (the key would have to cover the level, the tuning and all code, e.g. by hashing the xtask binary; not needed at this runtime, kept in `backlog.md`); `opt-level = 3` for jnr-core/xtask (no gain); one thread per level for all levels at once (133 s: the large stages become the critical path).

## D-083 Implementing D-075: the auto-scroll camera (engine, M5 task E26)
- **Format**: `CameraMode::Autoscroll` and `[meta] autoscroll = [from, to]` (`LevelMeta::autoscroll`, `autoscroll_zone()`); `Level::parse` rejects a missing zone, a zone with another camera, and anything but `0 ≤ from < to ≤ width − 20` (`level::SCREEN_COLS`).
- **Camera** (`camera.rs`): `Autoscroll { phase: ScrollPhase, pos, hold }` in `Game` (`Game::autoscroll()`), `Camera::follow_autoscroll` as D-075's table; start, respawn and stage changes frame as in side levels and `Autoscroll::placed` sets the phase from that framing (inside the zone: `hold = autoscroll_hold`). The hold and the scroll advance only in `follow_camera`, which frozen, paused, dying and goal-sequence frames never reach. **Lead-out is final**: "never left" is read as "never back to the scroll"; D-051 still applies in the lead-out (a rider carried left there moves the camera left as in any side level, the phase stays `LeadOut`). Stage 6 has no platform at the zone's end, so this never shows.
- **Order in a frame** (scroll only, `Game::scrolling`, the hold included): the player's own movement and every carry use the clamp from the frame's starting camera (`player::Bounds { min_x, max_x, max_x_speed }`, `platform::carry(.., max_x, ..)`); D-051 (`follow_carried_left`) is skipped; after the checkpoint, goal and camera follow (also on the frame the scroll hands over to the lead-out), `push_from_left_edge` moves a hitbox left of the new camera x right with `TileMap::move_x` (only solid tiles block), and if it ends short of the edge she dies with **`DeathCause::Squeezed`** (state-hash tag 5, rendered like `Hit`).
- **Addition: the right clamp keeps pace.** "Stops there like at a wall" taken literally (speed set to 0) made a runner at the clamp stutter: the clamp moves 0 or 1 px a frame, she lost her speed at every touch and fell up to 3.4 px behind in a 16-frame saw-tooth (measured on the grey box). Stopped at the clamp her speed is now capped at `autoscroll_speed` instead (0 during the hold), so holding right she stays < 1 px behind it (`the_right_clamp_stops_her_own_movement`). The clamp never pulls her left (if she were ever past it, she just can't move further right). The carry's clamp only stops the carry; her speed is untouched. For the level-designer: as at a real wall, a jump from the clamp is therefore a walking jump (`jump_speed_walk`), so gaps and hops a rider at the clamp must take need walking-jump reach.
- **Hashes**: `state_hash` and `world_hash` include the scroll state only in auto-scroll levels, so every other level's search and hashes stay as they were; the three new tuning keys are in the static hash (tuning `Debug`), so the testbed's pinned `FINISH_HASH` changed (frame unchanged). Every earlier replay, solver replay and golden passes unchanged.
- **Tuning** `[camera]`: `autoscroll_speed = 192`, `autoscroll_hold = 60`, `autoscroll_right_margin = 64`; validated `0 < speed < player.walk_speed` (interim stand-in for D-075's `< swim.speed`, which E27 adds with `[swim]`; the Dampfer check is E28's), hold 0..=600, margin 0..=160.
- **"Fully on screen"**: `Camera::fully_on_screen(&Aabb)` (pub) and `UpdateCtx::fully_on_screen(&Aabb)` (pub(crate), for entity updates; E29's swan, Gelse and regatta triggers use it through `enemies::fully_on_screen`): the hitbox inside the camera rectangle, its edges included.
- **Lints** (`xtask/src/scrolllint.rs`): goal ≥ `to + 20`; squeeze faces in the columns seen during the scroll (`from .. to + 20`) with a **pass-under** reading: a face with ≥ 2 empty rows between its bottom and the surface in front (Resi's tallest hitbox is 30 px) is overhead and skipped, otherwise its top must be ≤ 3 tiles above that surface (for water: the water body's top). Without it the stage's own rule "boxes/bricks ≥ 4 tiles above a deck" would fail the lint. Known gaps for E27/E30: a one-way deck over water is "the surface" for a face behind it, so a swimmer under the deck is not checked against that face (Stage 6 keeps rows 11–12 over water free of solids, so it can't happen there); faces in columns `to + 1 .. to + 20` are checked although the edge never reaches them (stricter than needed). "The checkpoint in a zone" = a checkpoint in those columns; it must stand on a solid or one-way tile.
- **Solver**: budget 3 × width px + `scroll_frames` (zone px / speed, rounded up); ranking unchanged (x). The grey box solves in ~8 s with no special handling; **the D-075 fallbacks (route-limited zone, `camera = "side"`) were not taken**.
- **Grey box** `00_m5_autoscroll` (112 × 17, zone [8, 80], `min_jump_window = 5`, narrowest window 19): quay → water gap → pier → a 3-tile island (the squeeze wall) → pier → shallows with a Gondel going left → pier with the checkpoint → gap → lead-out quay, goal at 104. Its water is the lethal `wolfgangsee_water` until E27, so `m5_autoscroll_pushed_into_water` ends in a death (with swim water it should end swimming). Replays: `m5_autoscroll_{solver,pushed_into_water,squeezed,carry,checkpoint_hold}`; no shots, so no goldens.

## D-084 M5 integration: squeeze faces over swim water, the solver over water, the cross-task stubs (engine, M5 task E30)
- **Squeeze faces over swim water: at most 2 tiles** (`levels lint`, code-review finding on E26/E27). D-075's limit of 3 tiles assumed she stands on the surface in front of the face; a swimmer climbs out with a held jump-out instead, and the weakest form's reaches only ~45 px (big and Hackl Resi; Normal ~53). So a left-facing solid face in an auto-scroll zone whose surface in front is a **swim** surface may rise at most `jnr_core::swim::MAX_FACE_TILES` = 2 tiles above it (32 px); over a deck, the ground or lethal water the limit stays 3. The number is not hand-waved: the feel test `jump_out_heights_bound_the_squeeze_face_over_swim` measures every form's held jump-out from the water line and fails if the weakest one doesn't clear 2 tiles, or would clear 3 (then the limit should rise). **Not taken**: deriving the limit at lint time from a simulation (the lint would depend on the player physics at run time; the test pins the same thing).
- **Pit recovery under boats (D-077) skips swim water only.** E28 skipped every column whose first tile below the track is `Water` as a stand-in for swim water. With swim water real, the narrowest rule that keeps every existing level lint-clean: columns over **swim** water are skipped (a fall is a swim) and an exit over swim water passes; **lethal** water is looked through again as before D-077 (a fall into it is a death, not a softlock, and the floor below it counts). Every level in `content/levels` lints clean under it; the unit test `boats_skip_the_pit_recovery_over_water` covers swim, lethal and dry basins.
- **Solver jump windows over water (D-033).** A window's checkpoint is the first landing + 2 s; over water the route is often in the air or swimming there, and "standing at the route's x minus one tile" can then be impossible for any run, the original included (`00_m5_swim`: the bollard hop measured 1 frame because the route was in the air over open water at its checkpoint). Rule: if the route is off the ground at the checkpoint **and swims before it stands again**, the checkpoint moves to that next frame on the ground; routes that never swim keep the D-033 checkpoint, so every earlier level's windows are unchanged. A shifted jump that ends in swim water hands over to the re-planning at its first swimming step (E27), and the recovery still has to stand on the ground: a surface swimmer never counts as recovered (the lake bed is ground), and no extra time is granted (an extra 2 s for swims was tried and made no difference on the grey boxes, so it was dropped as loose). `00_m5_swim`: narrowest 25 (was 1).
- **`00_m5_lake`** (152 × 17, `autoscroll = [8, 114]`, `min_jump_window = 5`): every M5 kind in an auto-scroll zone over swim water (Dampfer with swan #1 under its way, swan #2 and a dive line in open water, rafts, Traundl A under the Gelse, the checkpoint raft inside the zone, Traundl B, a pier, the Regattaboot, the Strobl quay). The solver finishes it at frame 2462 with 2 jumps (it swims most of the way), narrowest window 18 (frame 845); the last jump-out (frame 2349) is route-limited (≥ 4, not counted). Re-solved grey boxes on swim water (narrowest windows): `00_m5_autoscroll` 11 (frame 1316; D-083's "narrowest window 19" was measured over the lethal stand-in and is superseded), `00_m5_boats` 25 (frame 145), `00_m5_enemies` 19 (frame 320), `00_m5_swim` 25. **The D-075 fallbacks (route-limited zone, `camera = "side"`) were not taken.**
- **Cross-task stubs resolved**: `Game::player_swimming()` is `Player::swimming` (no stomps or roof bounces while swimming, via E29's `enemies::contact`); the Gelse avoids lethal and swim water alike (`move_clear_of_water`); the schwan-on-`Water` special case of `entity_tile` is gone (any entity may sit on a swim tile, none on lethal water). The grey boxes `00_m5_autoscroll`, `00_m5_boats` and `00_m5_enemies` are on swim water (the boats' dry dock and the enemies' dry regatta lane and trench became water); `00_m5_autoscroll` keeps one lethal 3-column pit (80–82) as the test pit for its checkpoint respawn. Replays re-cut where the behaviour legitimately changed (listed in the E30 commit); the replay format gained `swimming=<true|false>`.

## D-085 M5 review: Stage 6 as-built deviations, Gelse placement, route balance (game-designer, M5)
- **Accepted**: regatta boats at 198/204/210 with Gelse #3 at 171; swan #3 at 184; the Zaunerstollen perch `steg` 173–177 (row 8) with one bollard at 177 and the `Z`-box at 175 row 4; „Baujahr 1873" at 69; Traundl B at 153.
- **Rejected**: Gelse #2 at 160. It started hunting ~20 frames before Resi boarded Traundl B and met her mid-ride; jumps don't keep a platform's speed, so neither a stomp nor a dodge on the 3-tile deck is fair. It moves to **col 154, row 6**: its hunt starts as Traundl A reaches its far end, it reaches her on the border raft ≥ 50 frames before B docks, and it ends at ~3350. New stage rule: a Gelse first reaches Resi where she stands still or swims, never mid-ride on a Traundl. Boat hops the solver doesn't ride are verified by handwritten replays (`wolfgangsee_traundl`, `wolfgangsee_gelse_dive`) with hand-measured windows ≥ 5 frames.
- **Route balance accepted**: in an auto-scroll zone every route takes the same time. Boats are the comfort and score route, surface swimming is the exposed route, the lake bed is safe but poor. Swimming is part of the premise. No breath meter, no lethal deep water, no fourth enemy kind in M5 (D-074).
- **Flat lake bed**: within the columns the scroll's left edge reaches, the lake bed has no raised steps, because the push has no step-up and would squeeze a resting diver against one. The shallows at 100–106 become a reed belt over the row-16 bed. The squeeze lint also checks under-water faces against the lake bed (E31). *As built (engine, E31):* a left-facing face whose front cell at its top is swim water, in columns `from + 1 ..= to + 1` (the edge never pushes further; the stricter `to + 20` would flag the Strobl beach), is an error unless ≥ 2 rows of swim water lie under it (a block a diver passes under); quays and piers rising above the water line stay under D-084's 2-tile rule, lethal water is not checked. `00_m5_autoscroll`'s dry basin step (col 55) was such a trap: the pier end at col 54 is now a solid pier head from deck to bed (all its replays pass unchanged).
- **`time_limit` 270** stays provisional until QA's measured relaxed run at M5 acceptance (D-045: 86.7–90 s keeps 270, otherwise 3 × the run rounded up to 10 s, clamped to 200–300).
- **Regatta masthead**: the 8 px bounce part (`regattaboot_mast` 8×20, D-080 refinement) is confirmed.

## D-086 The auto-scroll camera's vertical rest and lead-out catch-up, power changes while floating, sign and enemy draw layers (engine, M5 task E32)
- **Problem** (art-director, Stage 6 goldens; two QA M5 engine bugs are folded in below): D-075 made the auto-scroll camera the side camera vertically. `Camera::new` centres Resi in the dead zone (camera y ≈ 72 on a row-11 deck) and the dead zone only follows down once her centre passes screen y 120, which a Resi standing on a row-11 deck never does, so after any jump it stayed up (≈ 75 on the decks, ≈ 44 on the steamer). The lake bed (screen y 164 at the intended rest) was never on screen while she swam at the surface. game-design.md "Stage 6" assumed the rest at y 92.
- **Rule** (`camera = "autoscroll"` levels only, in every phase: lead-in, scroll, lead-out): the camera's vertical **rest** is the level's bottom clamp (`level_h − 180`; 92 for Stage 6's 17 rows: decks at screen y 84, water at 100, bed at 164). Start, respawn and stage changes frame y at the rest (`Camera::new_autoscroll`), then the dead zone. Each frame, while she **rests** (`Game::camera_rests`: `Player::on_ground` — tile, deck, boat, quay or lake bed — or swimming with the chest within the surface band, not on a jump-out's way up), y first settles towards the rest by at most `camera.autoscroll_settle_speed = 2` px (the climb camera's settle speed, D-054); then the side camera's dead zone applies unchanged and wins (on the Zaunerstollen perch, row 8, she stays at the dead zone's top edge, no creep and no jitter). In the air or diving only the dead zone moves it, as before. **No per-level key**: the bottom clamp is the rest of every auto-scroll level so far (a lake has nothing below its bed); a `[meta]` rest can come with a stage that needs another one.
- **Scope**: side and climb levels are untouched (`follow` / `follow_climb`; every one of their replays is frame-for-frame identical); the goal sequence keeps `follow_y` (D-040), which on the Strobl meadow (row 16) is clamped at 92 anyway. The new tuning key is in the static hash (tuning `Debug`), so pinned finish hashes moved; no replay pins one.
- **Title view** (found by the render test `subtitle_band_is_clear_of_tiles_on_every_stage_title`): at the rest, the start bollard (col 12, row 10) reaches screen y 68, into the title subtitle's band (D-035). The title screen of an auto-scroll stage therefore keeps the side camera's framing (`Game::frame_title`, after `Mode::Title` is entered at a start or after the teaser); START re-places the camera at the rest while the navy intro card shows, so play still starts at y 92 and nothing visibly jumps. Other levels unchanged (their title framing already is the side camera's). Test: `the_title_keeps_the_side_framing_and_play_starts_at_the_rest`. Alternative for the game-designer/art-director if this exception is unwanted: move or lower the bollard, or accept a bollard top touching the subtitle shadow.
- **Triggers**: "fully on screen" is vertical too (D-080 note). With the camera between ~20 (a high jump) and 92, a row-6 Gelse (hitbox y 100–108), the swans and the regatta boats (masthead 148, keel 204) are fully on screen whenever they are horizontally, so every Gelse wait, swan trigger and regatta start happens on the frame it did before (re-measured in the D-074 addendum, E32 update). The rest's only gameplay-state difference on any replay: a Gelse leaving after its hunt is removed once its bottom is above the screen, a few frames earlier now (it is harmless then). That changes the solver's de-duplication and hence its routes on `wolfgangsee` and `00_m5_lake` (re-written; see below), not what any route does.
- **Draw layers** (`jnr-render` `entities::Layer`, render only, no gameplay effect): `draw_entities` draws in three passes, each in entity order: **signs** (after the Gondel cables), then the **middle** (items, props, checkpoints, the Steinschlag niches, the Souvenirstand, and every moving platform: Festungsbahn, Gondel, Schafbergbahn, Traundl, Dampfer), then **enemies and projectiles** (`Enemy`, `Defeated`, `Thrown`, `Stein`, `Note`, `ThrownHackl`; the Operettensänger with its high C). Resi and the front pass (debris, popups) follow as before. So no board hides an Oblate or Resi (the notch and Strobl shots), and no hazard disappears behind a hull or car (the Gelse under Traundl B). Side effect across Stages 1–5: enemies now also draw in front of items they overlap (e.g. the Pilger in front of Oblaten in the Falkenstein shaft), which matches "a hazard is never hidden". Items stay behind the platforms.
- **QA M5 bug: a power change while floating sank her** (`qa_m5_shrink_while_floating`, was `qa_m5_bug_shrink_while_floating_sinks`, D-076). A floating Zuckerschock has her chest point on the water line; a hurt kept the feet (`Player::set_power`), so Normal's chest landed at surface + 8, just outside the `< 8` band, and she sank to the bed (and JUMP stroked). **Rule** (`Game::set_player_power`, used by every in-play power change: hurt, Mozartkugel, Hackl, the Durchkriechstein shed): while swimming and not on the ground, a power change keeps the **top**, and so the chest point (`chest_px` below the top in every form), in both directions; a shrink then raises the feet, a grow lowers them. If the grown hitbox would reach into a solid tile (sinking within 8 px of the bed), the feet stay instead. On land and standing on the bed nothing changed (the feet stay). The stage-start carry-over (`flow.rs`) is on land and keeps `Player::set_power`. Not taken: re-snapping the chest to the surface line after the change (it would also lift a diver to the surface). Tests: `a_power_change_while_floating_keeps_the_chest_point` (shrink from Zuckerschock and Hackl, grow to both; she still floats 120 frames later and JUMP jumps out), `a_power_change_on_or_just_above_the_bed_keeps_the_feet`. Effect on existing replays: only `m5_enemies_regatta_side_hit` (a hurt while floating) plays differently after its Hurt, with the same events and expectations.
- **QA M5 bug: the lead-out camera snapped** (`qa_m5_leadout_camera_catch_up`, was `qa_m5_bug_leadout_camera_snap`, D-075/D-083). At the hand-over the side camera re-framed her at the follow line at once; the right clamp lets her be up to 99 px right of it, so the picture jumped (3328 → 3433 in one frame on the solver route). **Rule**: in the lead-out the side camera moves right at most `camera.autoscroll_catch_up_speed = 4` px per frame (validated `player.run_speed` < it ≤ 16, so it always gains on her); following her at her own speed is never bounded by it. On the QA route it catches up 42 frames after the hand-over (she walks on for the first frames). D-051's left follow in the lead-out is unchanged. Tests: `autoscroll_hands_over_to_the_side_camera_at_to` (now 4 px steps), `the_lead_out_camera_catches_up_without_a_snap` (the QA replay plus 60 still frames: every frame moves 0..=4 px, caught up ≤ 45 frames after the hand-over). The replay format got no `camera_x` expectation; the unit test reads the replay instead. Its two shots (`handover_before`, `handover_after`) are new goldens for the art-director.
- **Re-cut**: `wolfgangsee_solver` (new search route, still complete at 4541, 11 jumps, narrowest counted window 11; D-074 addendum), with `wolfgangsee_tour` re-copying it (the tour coupling test; completion 4712 and every shot frame unchanged, the shots show the same scenes) and `m5_lake_solver` (complete at 2460 with 3 jumps, narrowest 25 at frame 2281; D-084's 2462 / 2 jumps / 18 is superseded). No handwritten or QA replay changed: all `wolfgangsee_*`, `qa_m4_flow_st_wolfgang_hackl_to_wolfgangsee` and `qa_m5_*` replays pass unchanged with identical events.
- Tests: `camera.rs` `autoscroll_starts_at_its_rest_the_bottom_clamp`, `autoscroll_settles_back_to_its_rest_at_2_px_per_frame_only_at_rest`, `autoscroll_dead_zone_wins_over_the_rest_on_a_perch`; `tests/lake.rs` `the_autoscroll_camera_starts_at_its_rest_and_the_side_camera_does_not`, `after_a_jump_from_a_deck_the_autoscroll_camera_settles_back_at_2_px_per_frame` (the side camera stays up, the old behaviour), `the_camera_rests_at_the_surface_and_on_the_bed_but_not_diving_or_in_the_air`, `wolfgangsee_rests_on_its_bottom_clamp_wherever_she_stands_or_floats` (the solver route: y 92 on every frame after 30 resting frames unless the dead zone holds her); `entities.rs` `layers_put_signs_first_and_enemies_last`, `an_oblate_is_drawn_in_front_of_a_sign_board_listed_after_it`, `an_enemy_is_drawn_in_front_of_a_boat_listed_after_it` (both fail with the old single pass); tuning validation `autoscroll_settle_speed` 1..=16 and `autoscroll_catch_up_speed`. Both new camera keys are in the static hash: the testbed's pinned `FINISH_HASH` was re-pinned (route and frame unchanged).

## D-087 M5 QA: Stage 6 squeeze fixes, Zaunerstollen box, respawn phase, time limit (game-designer, M5)
- **Zone starts at col 16**: `autoscroll = [16, 208]` (was `[8, 208]`; zone 192 columns, 4096 frames). With `from = 8` the edge swept the bathing notch (13–15) ~107 frames into the scroll and squeezed a diver resting on its bed against the quay 16–17 (QA `qa_m5_bug_notch_diver_squeezed`); D-085's "the notch before the scroll" was untrue. From 16 the scroll begins with the camera's left edge on the notch's right wall, when her centre passes col 25 (~130 frames into the steamer ride); the notch is behind the screen for good. Every camera position comes ~11 frames earlier (the first 128 px are ridden at 0.8 instead of 0.75 px/frame); phases and triggers are camera-fixed, so relations are unchanged — re-measured by the level-designer. Rule: a basin bounded by a solid face from the bed to above the water lies left of `from`. Not taken: rebuilding the notch (loses the safe swim lesson); a warning sign (an unfair death in the tutorial).
- **Deck bollards: the SMB3 squeeze is accepted** (QA `qa_m5_bug_pontoon_bollard_squeeze`). A Resi who stands still on a deck is pushed into a 1-high bollard and squeezed (pontoon 133 ~7.7 s after a respawn; perch 177). On a deck that is fair: one standing jump clears it in every form, the push is visible first, and a hop or a DOWN drop through the `steg` escapes into water. Under water it is not (a resting diver is hiding), so D-085's flat bed stays. The squeeze lint guarantees a face is clearable by one jump, not that it can't pin. **Deck-bollard rule**: a solid on a deck in a scroll zone is 1 high, at the deck's right end with water or air behind it, never on a deck where the stage asks her to wait (steamer, Traundl, rafts, piers), and reached by the edge ≥ 6 s after any respawn. Tests: QA's replay becomes an intended-death replay (second death `Squeezed`); one replay hops 133 at the last moment and survives. Not taken: removing the bollards or making them props (item stoppers at deck ends are pin points too); a step-up in the edge push (engine rule for every auto-scroll level, against the SMB3 reference; backlog candidate).
- **Zaunerstollen box at (173, row 4)** (was 175; QA `qa_m5_bug_zaunerstollen_rolls_off_bollard`). From 175 the item fell onto the bollard's top at 177 and was lost; from 173 it lands on the perch ≥ 10 px short of the bollard face, turns at its side and returns to Resi. Not taken: col 174 (still reaches the top); a 2-high bollard (meets the item from above too, taller squeeze face).
- **Traundl A after the checkpoint respawn: accepted as is.** A is in range at the respawn and runs ~125 frames ahead of its continuous phase; Gelse #2 then first reaches her ~13 frames after A rests at its far end (a standing deck, D-085 holds) with a 15-frame stomp window (≥ 5), and the dive still escapes. General rule: after a respawn in a zone, a free-running boat already in range is out of step by `(respawn camera x − activation camera x) / 0.75 − 60` frames modulo its period; this replaces "the hold shifts by ≤ 60 frames". Verified by `wolfgangsee_respawn_traundl` (through B and C to the perch, each hop's window ≥ 5). Not taken: a new phase; moving the checkpoint; waking in-range entities by the scroll after a respawn.
- **`time_limit = 260`** (D-045): QA's relaxed run 85.7 s (boat route; 85.0 s as Normal) × 3 = 257, rounded up to 260. Supersedes D-085's provisional 270.

## D-088 Oblaten clear of sign boards (engine, M5 task E33; follows D-086's draw layers)
- Since signs draw first (D-086), `levels lint` fails on any placed Oblate sharing a pixel with a sign board's face and frame as drawn (posts left out, overflowing text included); the 12 findings in Stages 2–5 at the time are allowlisted as warnings in `signlint::KNOWN` until the level-designer moves them (validation.md L3, `xtask/src/signlint.rs`).

## D-089 Stage 6 time limit stays 260 (game-designer, M5 acceptance)
- Confirms D-087. The re-cut relaxed run `qa_m5_wolfgangsee_relaxed` completes at 83.2 s; 3 × 83.2 rounded up would be 250, but that run is 8 frames under the 250/260 boundary (83.34 s) and walks along the steamer deck; a relaxed rider who stands on the deck gives ~85.5 s (260). The scroll fixes 68.3 s of every run, so only the lead-in (the notch swim lesson) and the lead-out vary; their spread straddles the boundary, and D-045's "relaxed for explorers" takes the generous end. A fresh QA relaxed run anywhere in 80.0–86.7 s confirms 260; outside that D-045 applies strictly. Not taken: 250 (re-cuts every Stage 6 HUD golden for no gameplay gain).
- As built (M5 acceptance): „Baujahr 1873" stands at col 68 and „Seit 1950" at col 76 (D-085 accepted 69; both moved for the D-088 board clearance in the art-director's golden round 2).

## D-090 Stage 6: high-ground camera rest, top clamp, Zaunerstollen box, time limit 270 (game-designer, M5 acceptance)
- **Zaunerstollen box at (173, row 5)** (was row 4, D-087; QA `qa_m5_bug_zaunerstollen_under_hud`). On the perch the dead zone held the camera at y 53 (Normal; big 49), so the box drew at screen y 11–27 and the 1-up rose to ~1–11, behind „LEBEN". While the dead zone holds the camera, the box's screen y depends only on its height above the perch, so a lower box alone (row 5: item top 15/19) or a lower perch doesn't fix it; rows 9–11 are regatta boat #1's lane (174–197), and C's 3-tile clearance rules out a box over its track. With the high-ground rest below, the box's bottom (y 96) is 32 px above the perch, so every form walks under it and any standing jump bumps it; the item's top stays at screen y ≥ 26 (27 during Normal's bump) and it lands 13 px short of the bollard's face (x ~2819 vs 2832), turns there and returns. Rule for any box: at the camera where she bumps it, box top − camera y − 12 ≥ 22. Not taken: moving the bonus to the Strobl quay; a 1-tile perch at col 173.
- **High-ground rest** (auto-scroll levels; supersedes D-086's "on the perch she stays at the dead zone's top edge"): while she rests on ground (D-086's `camera_rests`), the camera settles towards `clamp(ground top − 84, 24, level_h − 180)` at 2 px/frame; floating at the surface keeps `level_h − 180`. Ground is framed at screen y 84 like the decks: decks, boats, quays and the lake bed unchanged (92); the perch 44; a bollard 76; a box top 28. Her centre is then at 69–73 in every form, inside the dead zone, which still applies afterwards. Side and climb levels untouched.
- **Top clamp** (auto-scroll levels, every phase): camera y ≥ 24. At 24 the water surface is at screen y 168 and a swan's hitbox (bottom 196) and a parked regatta keel (bottom 204) are fully on screen, so a stomp or masthead bounce never hides the lake (QA `qa_m5_bug_perch_bounce_lake_off_screen`: camera y 0, frames 3710–3815) and never delays a trigger (QA: 8 frames for a regatta boat). A higher Resi may leave the top of the screen, as in SMB3.
- **`time_limit = 270`** (D-045 strictly; supersedes D-087/D-089's 260). QA's fresh relaxed run `qa_m5_accept_relaxed_explorer` lies outside D-089's 80.0–86.7 s band; it touches the goal with 171 s left of 260 (89.x s of clock), × 3 < 270 → 270. Its LevelComplete frame 5401 (90.02 s) is one frame past the 270/280 boundary: boundary noise. From now on a relaxed run is measured by what the ZEIT clock consumes up to the goal touch (Playing frames only).
- Tests: unit tests for the clamp (y ≥ 24 in every phase; side and climb bit-identical) and the high-ground rest (perch, bollard, box top vs. deck, boat, bed, surface); the two QA bug replays become regressions (item top ≥ 22 and lives +1; camera y ≥ 24, water on screen, swan #3 fully on screen before its telegraph, regatta trigger frames unchanged).
- As built (engine, E34): `camera::Rest` (`None` / `Ground(top)` / `Surface`) from `Game::camera_rest` (`Ground` = her feet while `on_ground`, `Surface` = floating in the chest band, else `None`) replaces D-086's `bool`; `Camera::follow_autoscroll` settles towards `clamp(top − camera.autoscroll_ground_y, autoscroll_top, level_h − 180)` (`Surface`: the bottom clamp), then the dead zone, then `Camera::clamp_autoscroll_top` (y ≥ `camera.autoscroll_top`, or the bottom clamp if a short level's is higher). The clamp is also applied by `Camera::new_autoscroll` (start, respawn, stage change) and by the goal sequence's `follow_y` in auto-scroll levels, so it holds in every phase; the title view keeps the side framing (D-086). **Tuning**: `camera.autoscroll_ground_y = 84` (validated `deadzone_top + 15 ≤ it ≤ deadzone_bottom + 8`, so standing at the rest her centre, 8–15 px above the feet, is inside the dead zone and the two never fight) and `camera.autoscroll_top = 24` (0..=90; 24 = the lowest y that keeps a parked regatta keel, bottom 204, fully on screen). Both are in the static hash: the testbed's `FINISH_HASH` was re-pinned (route and frame unchanged). Where the clamp wins over the dead zone (she stands on a box top above row 6, or jumps above screen y 64 from the clamp), her top may leave the screen, as D-090 allows. Side and climb levels are bit-identical (every non-Stage-6 replay and golden passes unchanged). QA's bug replays are now `qa_m5_zaunerstollen_clear_of_hud` (lives 4; `tests::lake::the_zaunerstollen_stays_clear_of_the_hud`: the item's drawn top ≥ 22 on every frame, 24 at its highest) and `qa_m5_perch_bounce_lake_on_screen` (`tests::lake::the_perch_bounce_keeps_the_lake_on_screen`: camera y ≥ 24 on every frame and at 24 for > 80 frames, swan #3 telegraphs at 3795, the frame after its hitbox is first horizontally on screen, was 3801; `masthead_bounces_never_delay_a_regatta_start`: on the solver route, whose masthead and stomp bounces take the camera to the clamp, and on the relaxed run each regatta boat starts the frame after it is horizontally on screen, at the same scroll-fixed frames). D-086's `autoscroll_dead_zone_wins_over_the_rest_on_a_perch` became `autoscroll_dead_zone_still_applies_after_the_high_ground_rest`; new unit tests `autoscroll_high_ground_rest_frames_the_ground_at_screen_y_84` and `autoscroll_top_clamp_holds_in_every_phase`; `wolfgangsee_rests_on_its_bottom_clamp_wherever_she_stands_or_floats` became `wolfgangsee_camera_rests_where_she_stands_or_floats` (solver, Traundl and relaxed runs: after 30 resting frames on the same ground y is that ground's rest, the perch's 44 included, and y ≥ 24 throughout). Measurements: D-074 addendum, E34 update.

## D-091 Solver speed-up: exact speed-ups, one shift queue, a result cache, stale replays fail (engine-engineer, E35)
- **Context**: the `solver` verify step had grown from ~85 s to ~270 s with Stage 6 (verify ~320 s of the 10-min budget), and M6 adds Strobl and Bad Ischl. Measured on the 14-core container: pass 1 (searches) 62 s wall but only ~5.6 cores busy, because more than half of every search's time was the serial ranking of each ~1600-node bucket (sorting 1.3 KB nodes, `world_hash` per node on the calling thread); pass 2 (clean-ups and windows) 205 s wall at ~3.9 cores, because one jump's shifts ran one after the other and Wolfgangsee's jump at frame 1325 (its first `Landed` is on the far deck, so the checkpoint lies ~2900 frames on, D-084) needs 24 re-plans of ~7 s each: ~200 s on one thread.
- **Decision: only changes that keep every route and every window bit for bit** (no re-plan cap, no new checkpoint rule; the D-033/D-084 metric and its thresholds mean the same):
  1. **Search**: a node's progress and `StateKey` are computed when it is made (on the expanding thread); a bucket is ranked by sorting indices (stable, same tie order), the nodes are boxed, and the nodes cut from a bucket are freed on the expanding threads. Macros that start with the same inputs share those frames (`MacroTree`, 184 instead of 264 simulated frames per expansion; a test compares every macro against running it alone, incl. deaths and the goal inside a macro).
  2. **`Game::world_hash`** (jnr-core) hashes the entities a word at a time (`hash::WordHash`: multiply + xor-shift per 64-bit word, each step a bijection of the state) instead of byte-wise FNV-1a: 1.65 → 0.32 µs per call. The fields are the same; the value is only ever compared within a run (the solver's de-duplication key), never stored. `state_hash` keeps FNV-1a.
  3. **Jump windows**: every shift of every jump of every level is one item of one work queue on all cores, smallest |d| first; a shift is skipped only when a smaller shift on the same side of the same jump has already failed (the window ends there, so the walk never reads it), and each window is grown from the table by the old `window_range`. Same windows for any thread count (tests compare with measuring one jump at a time, 1/2/5/16 threads).
  4. **`clean_route`** trials start from the cleaned route's state at the hold and stop at the first differing position (the frames before the hold are those of the accepted route, whose positions all match).
- **Solver cache** (backlog item; `xtask/src/solver/cache.rs`): complete solves are stored in `$CARGO_TARGET_DIR/cache/solver/<level>.json` under a key over the parsed level and tuning (`Debug` text), beam width, frame budget, time limit and `XTASK_SOLVER_CODE_HASH`, which `xtask/build.rs` computes at build time over every file of `crates/jnr-core` and `xtask`, `Cargo.lock`, the workspace `Cargo.toml`, `rustc -vV` and the profile. Being a compile-time value, it always describes the code in the binary (sources edited after a build can't pair a new key with an old result); art, render and app crates are left out because they can't change a route or a window. Reports, images and every check still run on a cached result. Failed searches are not stored (they may depend on machine speed). `solve --no-cache` / `verify --no-cache` solve everything; a fresh target dir (CI) has no cache. The cache is a convenience for repeated verifies, not the budget: the cold run alone must fit.
- **Stale solver replays fail** (backlog, E32 review): the step compares the committed `<level>_solver.replay` inputs with the fresh route and fails with both lengths and the first differing input; the fix is `solve <level> --write` with the reason in the commit. All 23 committed solver replays matched, so none was re-written.
- **Measured** (14-core container, other agents' verifies raising the load average to ~10 during most runs, so ±10%): solver step **cold 95–108 s** (before: 270 s at load ~2, 310 s at load 7–14), searches 39–43 s + windows 54 s (before 62 + 205 s); **warm 2.3 s** (all 23 levels cached); `verify` 161 s cold, 50 s warm (before ~320 s). CPU per cold step ~1000 s user (before ~1150 s); slowest search Wolfgangsee 12–18 s (before 28 s). Every report (frames, search deaths, expanded nodes, every window and bound) is identical to the pre-E35 run, and every committed solver replay matches its fresh route. Headroom: two more stages of St. Wolfgang's size add roughly 15–20 s cold.
- **Not taken**: (a) bounding the re-plan horizon: a cap that counts as "not recovered" would collapse the Wolfgangsee lake jumps (their goal itself is ~2900 frames away), and an early exit at the route's own standing frames or on state equality with the route never triggers there (no standing frame on the lake; the shifted swimmer never meets the route's exact state); a swim checkpoint rule would change the metric (backlog). (b) An exact memo across re-plans (same frontier state ⇒ same result): logged beams of different shifts coincide for only ~18% of the frames. (c) More threads per search: once the ranking was cheap the searches were expansion-bound. (d) Ordering the shift queue by expected cost: a schedule simulation on logged item times gave 55.5 vs 59.6 s, not worth a cost model.
- **Risk** (unchanged by E35, more visible now): the searches' 60 s wall-clock limit still counts real time; with other agents loading the machine to a load average of ~50, Wolfgangsee's search hit it once in E35's measurements (a spurious STUCK). At normal load the slowest search (Wolfgangsee) takes 12–18 s.

## D-092 M6 scope: Stage 7 Strobl and Stage 8 Bad Ischl, their shapes and difficulty (game-designer, M6 planning)
- M6 is **two stages** (as M2 was): `07_strobl` (280 × 17) and `08_bad_ischl` (300 × 17), both `camera = "side"` with the Stage 1–5 vertical layout. Each brings **two genuinely new mechanics**, and each new thing again extends a system that has been through QA: Strobl's **Fensterladen** (D-094) is a stationary platform whose one-way top switches on a schedule (the Gondel's cycle without the motion), its **Ampel** with **Autos** (D-093) is the Souvenirstand's spawner pattern firing Reisebus-style chargers; Bad Ischl's **Kurgast** (D-095) is a Nordic-Walker who becomes a one-way platform when stomped, its **Offiziere** (D-096) are a Pilger-style file with the Operettensänger's "hurt zone checked before the stomp". The Postbus is a `reisebus` with its own look (D-097). Everything else is reuse: walkers, the Fiaker, the Blasmusiker, the singers, E-Bikers, a Gelse, pigeons, shelters, balconies, terraces.
- **Shape: side levels, no auto-scroll and no climb.** Both towns are flat. The solver's runtime grew with the auto-scroll stage (M5 report), and side levels keep it and every tool (goal framing, D-029/D-041) unchanged. Strobl is the town everybody drives through, so it is built around traffic and the people who stay. Bad Ischl is the spa town that still lives on its emperor, so it is built around drills, rest and operetta. **Stylised orders** (the Falkenstein/St. Wolfgang precedent): Strobl landing → promenade and guest houses → Dorfplatz bus stop → B158/L116 junction → the Bürglstein walkway → the Klausbrücke at the outflow → the Ischlerbahn trackbed. Bad Ischl: the Kaiser's hunting statue at the Katrin station → Kaiserpark and Kaiservilla → Esplanade on the Traun → Trinkhalle and Kurpark → Kongress & TheaterHaus → Pfarrgasse to Café Zauner. Bad Ischl ends in front of the café with its queue, so M7 can open inside.
- **Difficulty: the ramp continues past the Wolfgangsee.** Strobl: `min_jump_window = 5`, design target narrowest **5–7**, lethal water again (the walkway, the Ache), one hazard per jump over water, up to two over solid ground, plus two timing systems read from a distance. Bad Ischl: **`min_jump_window = 4`**, the global rule's "≥ 4 later" (game-design.md "Difficulty: medium") taken for the first time, for the last full stage; design target narrowest **4–6**; two hazards together over solid ground in every section from the Kaiserpark on; groups (files of officers) instead of single enemies. Both keep the D-073 style: the targets name the narrowest window and the hazard timings to tune, never a count of tight jumps. The full tables are in game-design.md ("Difficulty and reach rules (stage 7)" / "(stage 8)"). The D-085/D-087/D-090 lessons are rules from the first draft: collectibles on row 8 or lower at the street framing and the box rule (box top − camera y − 12 ≥ 22), sign boards ≥ 6 columns apart and never under an Oblate (D-088), nothing important hidden behind a platform (D-086 draw layers), the pen-wall rule (D-066).
- **Numbers**: `time_limit` per D-045, measured by the ZEIT clock (D-090): provisional Strobl 240 (relaxed ~80 s), Bad Ischl 270 (~90 s). Oblaten Strobl 75 ± 10, Bad Ischl 80 ± 10. `M`-box at the start (col 14 in both) and within 10 columns after the checkpoint (Strobl 139, Bad Ischl 158); the Zaunerstollen on an optional balcony in both (Strobl's Pension balcony via a shutter, Bad Ischl's theatre balcony; in Bad Ischl, where the Zaunerstollen was invented, with „Stollen / seit 1905"). Checkpoints: Strobl col 132 (Dorfplatz), Bad Ischl col 152 (Esplanade).
- **Running gags**: Bus 150 on two adjacent signs per stage (D-039), ending the chain: Strobl „fährt durch" (the town everybody drives through) → „hält! Voll." (the one time it stops, it's full, and the Postbus itself finally appears, D-097); Bad Ischl, the terminus: „jetzt leer" (empty at last, now that Resi doesn't need it) → „fährt zurück" (and off it goes, back to Salzburg). The pigeon on the monument: the promenade bust in Strobl (the joke is the pigeon, never the person) and the Kaiser's hat on the hunting statue in Bad Ischl. Callbacks: „Baden heute / verboten" answers Stage 6's „erlaubt"; „Stau / seit 1957" answers St. Gilgen's „Ischlerbahn / bis 1957". **Satire limits**: the jokes target through-traffic, holiday-flat customs (minimum stays, airing schedules), road works, imperial nostalgia, spa promises (the Salzprinzen), spa-town quiet, operetta counts, and queues. Faith is not the joke: Strobl's St. Sigismund church and the Kaisermesse are not used. The 1914 declaration of war signed at the Kaiservilla is not used either. Real people appear only as history (Franz Joseph, Sophie, Elisabeth as "Sisi", Lehár, Nickerl), never mocked; the officers are today's tradition regiments in historic uniform, friendly figures like the pilgrims.
- **Route**: shipping `07_strobl.level` and `08_bad_ischl.level` (+ their `options::LEVELS` lines) makes both playable (D-047). The teaser moves to Bad Ischl → Café Zauner. The M5 replays and goldens that end in the Wolfgangsee teaser now end on the route map in Wait or on the Strobl intro, and are re-cut and re-approved (as in D-053/D-066/D-074). The route-map labels are D-098.
- **Not in M6** (backlog): cars waiting at a red light as platforms (the "traffic jam as platforms" idea; the Kurgast's deck chair, D-095, builds the enemy-as-platform code first); a roundabout; the 546 bus; the Postalm toll road; the Bürglstein's forest path as an upper route; the Katrin cable car ride (a Gondel to a summit bonus); the salt mine as a bonus room („Stollen"); the 1607 brine pipeline (Soleleitung) as SMB-style pipes; a brine pool where Resi floats and can't dive (the D-076 surface-only rule as a tile); the Marmorschlössl; the Kaiserzug's arrival; the Lehár Villa interior; the Zauner Esplanade terrace (the café's summer branch since 1927).
