- **Fallback for the scope** (D-066/D-074 pattern): if the milestone runs long, Bad Ischl ships with the Kurgast's fallback (D-095: a plain walker) and the officers' fallback (D-096: a plain marching file) before anything is cut; no required passage depends on a chair or on the salute.

## D-093 Ampel and Autos: a traffic light that sends out cars (game-designer, M6 planning)
- **Ampel** (`ampel` spawner; `period` 300–600, default 400; `cars` 1–4, default 3; `phase` 0 or `telegraph + cars × gap .. period − 1`; `range` 6–16 tiles, default 12): placed in the road-level air cell at a side street's mouth. Its counter runs only while the player's centre is **left of** the spawner, within `range`, and the spawner cell is **fully on screen** (the Souvenirstand rule, D-069). Cycle: **red + amber** 30 frames (the telegraph, the real Austrian order before green, with headlights in the mouth) → **green**, a car at 30 + k × 40 for each of `cars` → **four green blinks** (64 frames: the Austrian StVO's blink before amber) → **amber** 30 → **red** for the rest. `period ≥ 30 + cars × 40 + 64 + 30 + 120` (validated), so the lane is free for ≥ 2 s of red at the spawner. At most 4 of its cars alive; a car whose spawn box would overlap the player is skipped. The light is harmless, can't be defeated, axe Pass.
- **Auto** (`auto`, 32×16 / 28×14): drives **left at 2.0 px/frame**, ignoring tiles and passing through enemies and platforms, never stops; removed off screen left. **Roof contact from above is a bounce without damage or points** in every state, any other contact hurts (the Reisebus rule, D-070); axe Bounce; walkers ignore it. Three looks by spawn index (renderer only).
- **Level rules**: the lane (the car's hitbox rows) is clear of solid and one-way tiles for 24 tiles left of the spawner (the Reisebus lane lint); a refuge above the lane in view of the spawner; the lane is over solid ground only; the first Ampel's cars are watched from above (a terrace on decorative posts) before a lane must be crossed.
- **Reasons**: Strobl's identity is traffic, and a side view can't show a crossing road, but it can show a junction whose side street pours cars into Resi's road whenever the light turns green. The light is the telegraph, and it's a real one that every Austrian reads: red-amber means "here they come", four green blinks mean "nearly over". The parts exist (a range-gated spawner, a tile-ignoring charger with a bounce-only roof), so the new code is the cycle and the light. Cars give three answers: wait for red, wait above the lane, or bounce across the roofs. Cars can't be stomped or axed, so the Hackl doesn't trivialise them.
- **Fallback** (engine's call, record it): no spawner. The level places single `auto` chargers (the Reisebus machine with the car's size and speed, trigger < 161 px, telegraph 30, no `stop`), and the Ampel becomes a decorative prop that the level sets in a fixed pose. The layout and its refuges stay.
- **Not taken**: cars from off screen (unseen hazards); cars that drive right (they would come from behind Resi); cars that stop at a red light and become platforms (the queue idea, backlog; it needs enemy-as-platform code, which D-095 builds first); a pedestrian light that Resi must press (a new verb for one stage).
- **Addendum (engine, M6 task E36): Ampel and Auto as built.** Spawner and cars as specified; **the fallback (single chargers, a decorative light) was not taken**. (1) **Level syntax** `{ entity = "ampel", period = 300..=600, cars = 1..=4, phase = 0 | 30 + cars × 40..period, range = 6..=16 }` (defaults 400, 3, 0, 12). The cycle numbers of the level rules are `level` constants (`AMPEL_TELEGRAPH` 30, `AMPEL_GAP` 40, `AMPEL_BLINK` 64, `AMPEL_AMBER` 30, `AMPEL_MIN_RED` 120; `level::ampel_min_period(cars)`, `ampel_min_phase(cars)`); a period below the minimum or a phase inside the telegraph or the green is a parse error. `Tuning::parse` keeps `enemies.ampel_telegraph`, `_gap`, `_blink`, `_amber` at 1..=those constants (so the level rules hold for any tuning), `ampel_blink` a multiple of 8, `ampel_max_alive` 1..=8, `auto_hitbox` ≤ 32×16, `auto_spawn_dx` 0..=15, `auto_speed` 0 < v < 12 px, and `ampel_gap × auto_speed ≥` the car's width (the cars of one green never overlap). (2) **Spawner** (`EntityKind::Ampel`, hash tag 26; hitbox = its cell, no contact, axe `Pass`, never drawn by `Entity::pose`): states `WAITING` → `COUNTING` (the Steinschlag/Souvenirstand pattern, `count` = the cycle frame f). Its counter runs only while the player's centre is strictly left of the cell's centre, within `range` × 16 px (centre to centre) and the cell is fully on screen (`UpdateCtx::fully_on_screen`, D-083's helper); it starts at `phase` on the first such frame. At f = telegraph + k × gap (k < cars) it sends car k, unless `ampel_max_alive` of its cars are alive (`UpdateCtx::cars`: the spawn points of every car alive, matched like the Souvenirstand's globes) or the car's hitbox would overlap the player's (new `UpdateCtx::player_box`); a skipped car is simply not sent, the cycle goes on. The light is `enemies::ampel_light(e, level, t) -> AmpelLight { Red, RedAmber, Green, GreenBlink { on }, Amber }` from the counter (red while waiting; each blink is an eighth of `ampel_blink` on, then an eighth off). Like the Souvenirstand it despawns once it is `despawn_margin` behind the camera (she is right of it then, so it would never count again). (3) **Auto** (`EnemyKind::Auto`, id 17): hitbox 28×14 with its left edge `auto_spawn_dx` = 2 px into the spawner cell and its bottom on the cell's bottom; drives left at 2.0 (`auto_speed` 512) moving `x` only (no tiles, enemies or platforms), never stops; removed once its right edge ≤ the screen's left edge. Not stompable, so the generic contact rule gives the Reisebus's roof bounce without damage or points in every form and a side hurt; axe `Bounce`; not „on foot" (walkers don't turn at it), `sinks_in_swim_water` false. Its `count` is k, its index in its green: the renderer's look (`drive_0` … `drive_2` = k mod 3; so a 2-car Ampel shows the hatchback and the estate, a 3-car one all three). (4) **Lane lint**: `enemies::reisebus_lane_problems` covers the Ampel with its car's hitbox at the spawn point: row 14 on a row-15 road, from 24 tiles left of the spawner through the cell the car pokes into (spawner column + 1). (5) `world_hash` lists the Ampel with the enemies (active, state, counter); the cars are enemies. (6) **Renderer**: `ampel` (anims `rot`, `rotgelb`, `gruen`, `gruen_blink` with its frame from the counter, `gelb`) at the cell's bottom centre in the middle layer, so its cars drive in front of it; with the approved art (E36 merge: `art/sprites/props/ampel.sprite`, `enemies/auto.sprite`, `enemies/postbus.sprite`, pinned by `entities::tests::the_real_atlas_has_the_traffic_art`) the grey boxes are only fallbacks; without the sprite: a 2 px `m` pole and a `k` housing with three lamps (`r`, `Y`, `g`; unlit `d`) and two blinking `Y` headlights in the mouth during red and amber. `auto` by look, never mirrored; grey box a 28×14 box in `r` / `w` / `h` by look with a `d` window band and a `Y` headlight. Grey box level `00_m6_traffic` (90 × 17, the Stage 7 framing) with replays `m6_traffic_{watch,red_crossing,shelter_wait,roof_bounces,side_hit,postbus_halt,solver}` (solver: complete at 520, 2 jumps, narrowest window 14).

## D-094 Fensterläden: timed one-way platforms on the stage clock (game-designer, M6 planning)
- **Fensterladen** (`fensterladen`; `period` 120–480, default 240; `open` 60..=period − 30, default 150; `phase` 0..period − 1): a shutter that folds out of a guest-house window. Body 32×8, its top on its spawn cell's top edge, left-aligned. **Open** for f < `open` (a one-way top: landing, standing, jump-through like every platform; the last 30 frames rattle in the renderer, the body never moves), **closed** for the rest (no collision; a rider just starts falling with her coyote frames; nothing is pushed or crushed; the last 20 frames show the landlady at the window as the opening telegraph). f = (stage clock + `phase`) mod `period`.
- **The stage clock** (new, shared with D-096) = frames of play since the stage started or since the last respawn, i.e. the frames the ZEIT timer counts (frozen, paused and dying frames don't). Reason: shutters that work as a staircase need an exact relative phase. A counter from activation gives each shutter its own start frame, which depends on how the player approaches (the Gondel B/C problem, D-052), so a pair could drift out of overlap.
- **Level rules**: on the required route a shutter stands over solid ground (a closing shutter only drops her); two shutters that form a required step are open together for ≥ 60 frames per cycle (Stage 7's pair: 90); 2 tiles wide, ≤ 3 tiles above what it is reached from, ≥ 3 tiles of air above it (the platform clearance lint); nothing hostile reaches a shutter on the required route (D-032 extended); shutters over lethal ground only on optional routes.
- **Reasons**: a platform that appears and disappears on a visible schedule is a classic jump-'n'-run reading that the game doesn't have yet; Strobl's guest houses make it a joke instead of an abstraction (the landlady airs the rooms, „Lüften / 10–12 Uhr"; „Zimmer frei / ab 7 Nächte"). It reuses the stationary platform code (landing, carry, clearance), and only the collision switch is new.
- **Fallback** (engine's call, record it): shutters become static one-way platforms (the level puts `balcony` tiles where the shutters are, and the prop shows them open). Stage 7 then loses the timing but stays complete.
- **Not taken**: shutters that swing horizontally and push Resi (a platform that pushes needs crush rules); shutters that open only when Resi comes close (a proximity trigger makes the schedule unreadable from a distance); awnings that roll out (a growing platform).
- **Addendum (engine, M6 task E37): implemented as specified; the fallback was not taken.** (1) **The stage clock** is `Game::stage_clock() -> u32`, ticked in `Game::tick_timer` (the one place the ZEIT timer counts a frame of play, so frozen, paused, intro, dying and goal-sequence frames are left out by construction) and set to 0 by `reset_level` (stage start, respawn, continue, the next stage of a run); it is hashed in `state_hash` (testbed `FINISH_HASH` re-pinned). (2) **A shutter's state is stored, not recomputed**: right after each clock tick `Game` sets every Fensterladen's `count` = f and `state` = open/closed, active or not (and `from_spawn` at clock 0). So the frame the renderer draws is the state the next frame's landing uses, an inactive shutter is drawn in its state, and the solver's `world_hash` sees it through the entity (levels without shutters hash as before). (3) **Collision** reuses `platform::land` with one filter, `platform::carries` (a Fensterladen only while open): a closing shutter simply stops catching its rider each frame, so she falls with the coyote frames `stand` gave her; an opening one is caught only by feet crossing its top from above, so it never lifts or crushes anyone inside it. Nothing else in the platform code changed. (4) **Note for the Stage 7 level** (from the grey box): with f = (clock + `phase`) mod `period`, Stage 7's #3 (`phase` 60) is open at clock 180–329 mod 240, not at 60–209 as the spec's prose says (that would be `phase` 180); the pair still overlaps 90 frames (clock 0–89) and #3 opens before #2, which is what a staircase needs. And a running jump from a balcony 4 tiles up reaches a roof 3 tiles higher across 8 columns: the grey box `00_m6_shutters` needed 10 columns of street between its balcony and the annex before the solver stopped skipping the shutters. Stage 7's balcony (51–55, flower box at 55) is 6 columns from the annex (62), so the level task must check that bypass with the solver.

## D-095 The Kurgast and his deck chair: an enemy that becomes a platform (game-designer, M6 planning)
- **Kurgast** (`kurgast`, 16×24 / 12×22, no fields): walks **0.375 px/frame** towards the player at activation; turns at walls, enemies on foot and ledges (the Nordic-Walker rule). **Stomp** → he lies down in his **deck chair** where he stood: the hitbox becomes a **16×16 one-way top platform**, bottom on the floor, harmless (no hurt, no stomp, no points; walkers turn at it; chargers, cars and notes pass), popup „Kurruhe!" (`popup.kurruhe`, stacked like „Bravo!"). He rests **300** frames, then the wake telegraph **40** (sits up, stretches); **both counters pause while Resi stands on him**, so he never stands up under her. Then he stands up (feet kept), faces the player and walks, harmful again. **100 points for his first stomp only**; later stomps lay him down again with a bounce, give no points and neither advance nor reset the chain (no farming). A bumped block knocks him off (100 if not yet scored). Axe: walking **Defeat** (100), lying **Pass** (like a crashed E-Biker).
- **Level rules**: no required passage needs a chair; a chair spot is on flat ground with the Kurgast penned by walls ≥ 2 high; optional ledges 4 tiles up are reached by a running held jump or by a walking jump from a chair.
- **Reasons**: an enemy that turns into a tool is the SMB shell idea in a gentler form, and it is the spa guest's whole nature: slow, then lying down, then wanting quiet. It is also the first enemy-as-platform code, which later ideas need (cars waiting at a red light, D-093 not-taken). A one-tile chair turns a near-impossible 4-tile step into a 3-tile one, so the payoff is concrete. The timer that pauses under her is the fairness rule that makes the tool safe.
- **Fallback** (engine's call, record it): no platform. A stomped Kurgast is squished and removed like a Selfie-Tourist (100, „Kurruhe!" kept); the level turns each chair-only bonus ledge into one with a `stone` step. Stage 8 stays complete (D-092).
- **Not taken**: a chair you can kick like a shell (a projectile across the Kurpark would hit the officers and the Kurkapelle, breaking the two-hazard rule); a Kurgast who walks off ledges (he would leave his chair spot and fall into the Traun); a Kurgast who throws towels to reserve deck chairs (a German cliché, not the Ischl one).
- **Addendum (engine, M6 task E38): as built, no fallback taken.** `EnemyKind::Kurgast` (hash id 19, 20 reserved), states `WALK` (0, not yet scored) → `LIE` → `WAKE` → `RESTED` (walking again, scored) → `LIE` … (`enemies::update_kurgast`); tuning `enemies.kurgast_hitbox` 12×22, `kurgast_speed` 96 (0.375), `kurgast_chair` 16×16, `kurgast_rest` 300, `kurgast_wake` 40 (validated: chair ≤ 32 wide and no taller than he stands). Walking he is the Nordic-Walker's walk (`walk_turning_at_ledges`, shared with `update_walker`) and turns at enemies on foot (`turn_at_enemies`). A stomp calls `enemies::lie_down` instead of `defeat`: the hitbox becomes the chair (centre and feet kept), he never moves while in it (no gravity, no step). **The enemy-as-platform hook**: `enemies::is_platform_now(&Entity)` (true for a Kurgast in `LIE`/`WAKE`), checked next to `EntityKind::is_platform` in the shared `platform::land`. Standing on a still top is that landing every frame, so the hook needs no `carry`; it doesn't make the kind a moving platform (`is_platform` stays false: no never-despawn, no track lints). A later platform enemy (D-093's waiting cars) adds its case to `is_platform_now`; one that moves would also need the `carry` call in `Game::update_entities`. In the chair he is harmless (`Entity::harmful` false: no hurt, no stomp, the axe passes), `on_foot` (walkers and pilgrims turn at him), and the rest and wake counters run only on frames the player doesn't stand on the chair (`platform::stands_on` with `UpdateCtx::player_standing`), so 340 frames when nobody does. He stands up in the frame `count` reaches `kurgast_wake` (feet and centre kept), faces the player and takes his first step on the next frame. **Points**: `enemies::scores_on_defeat` is false once he has scored (any state but `WALK`): the first stomp gives `stomp_reward` (100 in the chain); a later stomp bounces her without points and leaves `stomp_chain` as it was. **Clarification**: the bump's "100 if not yet scored" applies to the axe too (D-095 says "walking Defeat (100)"): his points total 100 at most, so an axe on a Kurgast who has scored knocks him off for nothing (no score popup; `Game::defeat_by_projectile` skips 0 points). „Kurruhe!" (`Popup::Kurruhe`, sub-tag 9) shows on every lie-down: stacked `POPUP_STACK_PX` above the score popup on the first stomp, at the score popup's place on later ones (there is none); a bump or an axe shows none (he doesn't rest, he tumbles away). Renderer: `kurgast` (`walk`), the chair as `liegestuhl` (`idle`, `wake`) or else the `kurgast` sprite's `liegen`/`wake` (`Pose::Liegen`, `Pose::Wake`); grey boxes until the art lands: walking a `m` box, the chair a `m` box with an `R` canvas stripe, plus two white arms above it in the wake telegraph. Grey box `00_m6_kurgast`, replays `m6_kurgast_chair_step`, `_rest_on_chair`, `_waking_hurt`, `_second_stomp` and its solver replay. **Level note** (found in the grey box): a fully held walking jump rises 65 px, so it clears a 4-tile one-way ledge (64 px) from the floor with 1 px to spare; Stage 8's "a running held jump or a walking jump from a chair" is a pacing statement, not a hard gate (the replays use a 16-frame hold, 54 px, which reaches the ledge only from the chair).

## D-096 The Offiziere: a marching file with a sabre salute (game-designer, M6 planning)
- **Offizier** (`offizier`, 16×32 / 12×22; optional `lead = true` for the flag bearer, a look only; optional `phase` 0..239): the k.u.k. uniforms of today's tradition regiments at the Kaisertage. A **drill on the stage clock** (D-094): f = (clock + `phase`) mod 240. **March** f 0–167 at 0.5 px/frame (facing the player at activation; turns at walls, enemies on foot and ledges) → **„Habt Acht!"** f 168–191 (halt, stiff, hand on the hilt: the telegraph) → **„Präsentiert!"** f 192–239 (halt, sabre raised: an **8×16 sabre zone** above his hitbox top that hurts on any contact and is **checked before the stomp**, the high-C rule of D-068). Stompable outside the zone in every phase: 200, chain start 200, popup „Abtreten!" (`popup.abtreten`) on every defeat (stomp, axe, bump). Axe: Defeat (200); the sabre zone: Pass.
- **Files** of 2–3 officers one cell apart share a `phase` and so march and salute in perfect step: the stage clock gives every member the same drill frame whatever their activation frame was. A file's first meeting is seen from above (Kaiserpark terrace), a file never paces a landing over water, and the stomp window is ≥ 168 frames per 240-frame cycle.
- **Reasons**: groups are Stage 8's step in difficulty: a stomp chain over a whole file is the showcase, but only in the march. „Präsentiert!" turns the file into a row of spikes for 0.8 s; that's a vertical hazard read from the drill, built from the Pilger's file walking and the singer's zone-before-stomp check. The old drill words are the telegraph text and the joke („Habt Acht! / Kaisertage", „Abtreten!").
- **Fallback** (engine's call, record it): no drill. Officers are a plain marching file (the Nordic-Walker machine at 0.5, 200 points, „Abtreten!" kept), with no halt and no sabre zone. The stage keeps its layout; its narrowest windows are re-measured.
- **Not taken**: officers who charge or fire a salute (a Böller shot; a projectile on top of the singers' and the band's would exceed the two-hazard rule); a drill counter from activation (files would fall out of step); an officer who can't be defeated (it would block the Kurpark lawn).
- **Addendum (engine, M6 task E39): as built, no fallback.** Level syntax `{ entity = "offizier", lead = true|false, phase = 0..=239 }` (defaults false, 0; `level::OFFIZIER_MAX_PHASE`; any other field is a parse error). Tuning `[enemies]`: `offizier_hitbox` [12, 22], `offizier_speed` 128, `offizier_cycle` 240, `offizier_march` 168, `offizier_attention` 24 (Präsentiert = the rest, 48), `offizier_sabre` [8, 16], validated in `Tuning::parse` (march ≥ 1, attention ≥ 1, march + attention < cycle ≤ 240, so there is always a Präsentiert frame and every drill frame has a `phase`). `EnemyKind::Offizier` (hash id 21; 21–22 reserved), `Popup::Abtreten` (sub-tag 10; 10–11 reserved). `enemies::update_offizier`: the drill frame f = (stage clock + `phase`) mod cycle is recomputed every frame from `UpdateCtx::stage_clock` and kept in `count` (so `world_hash` sees it); states `WALK` / `state::ATTENTION` / `state::PRESENT`, poses `March` / `Habtacht` / `Praesentiert`. He faces the player on his first active frame and moves with the Nordic-Walker's code (`enemies::walk_turning_at_ledges`, shared with `update_walker` and the Kurgast: ledge turn while on a floor, gravity, wall turn; speed 0 while halted, so a halted officer neither turns at a ledge nor moves, but falls if his floor goes); he turns at enemies on foot (`turn_at_enemies`) and is on foot himself (walkers turn at him, a bumped block knocks him off). The sabre zone is no entity: `enemies::sabre_zone(&Entity, &Tuning) -> Option<Aabb>` (only in `PRESENT`; 8×16 centred on him, its bottom on his hitbox top), checked in `Game::interact` right after the high C and before the paddle wheel, his hitbox and every stomp rule: any overlap hurts and skips his other contact rules that frame (unit test `falling_onto_a_presenting_officer_hurts_instead_of_stomping`, replay `m6_offiziere_sabre_hurt`). Outside the 8 px blade column his 12 px hitbox stays stompable in every phase, also during „Präsentiert!" (a fall onto his shoulder, 2 px beside the blade, is a stomp: the D-096 numbers as specified). 200 with chain start 200; „Abtreten!" stacked above the score popup on every defeat (stomp, axe, bumped block; `enemies::defeat_popup`). Axe `Defeat`; the zone is passed by construction (axes only meet entities). `sinks_in_swim_water` true. Staggered activation is kept (no Pilger-style file wake): members of a file wake when each reaches the activation edge, so a file may wake spread out by a few px, but every member runs the same drill frame from his first frame on (unit test and replay `m6_offiziere_file_in_step`). **The stage clock** is E37's `Game::stage_clock()` (D-094; E39 built on a stub with the same name and semantics, replaced at the merge: ticked only in `Game::tick_timer`, reset in `reset_level`, hashed in `state_hash`; the tick point is the same, so the drill timing and the replays are unchanged). Renderer: `offizier` / `offizier_lead` (by the spawn's `lead`, like `pilger_lead`), anims by pose (`march`, `habtacht`, `praesentiert`, `squished`); grey boxes until the art lands: a `STONE_GREY` box, a yellow hilt at his front in „Habt Acht!", the sabre drawn from `sabre_zone` (a white blade with a silver outline, exactly the hurting pixels) in „Präsentiert!", and for the flag bearer a 1 px staff with a black-and-yellow flag behind him (dipped in „Habt Acht!", ≥ 2 px clear of the blade). With the sprite the `praesentiert` art draws the blade (sprite catalog). Test level `00_m6_offiziere` with `m6_offiziere_*` replays.
- **Addendum (level-designer, M6): Stage 8 Bad Ischl as built** (`content/levels/08_bad_ischl.level`; for the game-designer's review; placed here because the timings are the drill's). **Solver**: complete at frame 1851 (30.9 s), 17 jumps, `min_jump_window = 4` holds; **narrowest window 8** (frame 1510, x 3774: the running leap off the `Z`-box's top on B2 over Operettensänger #2 onto the bench), then 11 (frame 1602: bench → over file C → the launch step), 15 (notch 2 take-off), 15 (the hop over the Trinkhalle steps into Kurgast #4's pen), 16 (the bandstand). Above the 4–6 design target: every arrangement that put file C next to the singer or the officers right at the bandstand gave 1–3 frames (the solver hugs a timed hazard on one side, so the other side needs slack), so the stage keeps a margin over its gate instead. **Named timings**: notch 1 with Kurgast #3 on the landing 20 (frame 731); notch 2 15; file B 16–25 on the solver route (it jumps the file in its march); file C + singer #2 8 / 11; the Fiaker 25 (the solver passes the steps before the charge). Not counted: a route-bounded ≥ 2 at frame 1447, the solver's optional jump up through B1 beside singer #1 while he sings (the street under B1 needs no jump). All officer phases 0 (drill frame = clock − 1 + phase, D-099). **Deviations from the spec**, all in game-design "Stage 8": (1) Kurgast #1 at col **38** (spec 30) and the Katrin station with its canopy **4 columns left** (prop col 31 dx 8, canopy 30–34, `?`-box 32): he walks from the first frame, so with the spec's columns every Resi met him left of the canopy and his chair never sat under it; now an unhurried Resi stomps him under the canopy's left end and jumps through it from the chair (`bad_ischl_kurgast`). (2) The pigeon sits at col 23, `dy -5`, on the hat's brim beside the crown: the hat seat is on the 2-tile block's centre line, every cell centre is 8 px off it, and `taube_sitting` has no `dx` (engine requirement if the art-director wants it exactly on the crown). (3) **No flower urn** at 186: Kurgast #4 and file B share one pen 174–203 (D-099: they turn at each other), so the file turns at his chair and a held walking jump from the chair clears all three (`bad_ischl_chair_over_file`); with the urn the chair sat 9+ tiles from the file. (4) The bandstand's left plinth is a **lawn wall at 204** (2 high, the pen wall), 2 columns left of the floor 207–214: with the plinth at 206 flush with the floor, hopping it always landed on the floor under the roof next to the musician (window 2). (5) A wall step 216 (2) / 217–218 (3) right of the bandstand: the only way onto its roof Oblaten (3 up from 217). (6) **Singer #2 at 245** and a **2-high bench 249–250** between him and file C (pen 251–257 with the step 258): with the file pacing up to the singer the singer jump had 1–3 frames. The "stomp the file and bounce on" combination stays (`bad_ischl_file_c_singer`: singer stomped in his idle window, bench, a stomp on file C's rear officer onto the step). (7) Fiaker at 93 (spec 94), so its trigger is reached on the steps from col 85.5 (from 94 only at the steps' right end). **Numbers**: Oblaten 81 placed + 1 in the `?`-box; `time_limit = 270` provisional (D-045: QA measures the relaxed run by the ZEIT clock). **Replays**: `bad_ischl_{kurgast, chair_rest, kurgast_waking_hurt, chair_over_file, file_a_watch, file_b_chain, sabre_hurt, file_c_singer, fiaker, checkpoint, goal_walk_off, wurstkranz}` and the Hackl replays `bad_ischl_hackl_axes` (walking Kurgast Defeat 100, an officer Defeat 200) and `bad_ischl_hackl_chair_pass` (an axe through a lying Kurgast; he gets up later and hurts her, so he was never knocked off), on the route `hackl bad_ischl` (their `stage=` is the second station's slot, `st_gilgen`, as in `flow_hackl_carry_over`).

## D-097 Strobl and Bad Ischl: the Postbus, water, the Hackl and the ending (game-designer, M6 planning)
- **The Postbus** is a `reisebus` with the optional field **`look = "postbus"`**: the same machine (D-070), the same hash and behaviour; only the renderer differs (`postbus` sprite, and a `halt` anim with the doors shut and faces at the windows instead of the photo flashes). Reason: the running gag pays off ("hält! Voll."), and a new kind would add nothing to play. Fallback: a plain Reisebus, the sign carries the joke alone. Not taken: a rideable Postbus (D-070: a bus driving left drags the camera back, D-051).
  - **As built (engine, M6 task E36)**: `look = "reisebus" | "postbus"` on a `reisebus` only (`level::BusLook`, `EntitySpawn::look`, default `Reisebus`; an unknown look or the field on any other entity is a parse error). Not in the static hash (it hashes kind, column and row) and never read by core: a Postbus and a plain bus run hash-identical (`tests::traffic::a_postbus_behaves_and_hashes_like_a_plain_bus`). The renderer draws a Postbus with `postbus` (the Reisebus's anims; at its stop `halt` instead of `fotostopp`) and, without that sprite, a `Y` grey box with the bus's window band and hazard lights and five `e` faces in the windows at its halt. The fallback (a plain Reisebus) was not needed. Auto `Bounce` and Ampel `Pass` are in the exhaustive `axe_hit` matches; Auto is in `sinks_in_swim_water` (false).
- **Water**: both stages use **lethal** water only. The Bürglstein walkway is over `wolfgangsee_water` (the lake's outflow has a current: „Baden heute / verboten", answering Stage 6's „erlaubt"), and the Ischler Ache and the Traun are river water (`salzach_water`, the Salzach's look). The swimmable `see_wasser` stays Stage 6's open lake (D-076: a level can mix both, these choose lethal). Reason: lethal gaps are part of the step in difficulty after a stage without pits.
- **The Hackl**: **no `H`-box** in Stages 7 and 8 (the D-081 pattern: the legend places the Hackl at the Falkenstein and in St. Wolfgang). A Resi who arrives as Hackl keeps it, and the `M`-boxes score 1000. Classification for the exhaustive `axe_hit` match: Auto **Bounce**, Ampel **Pass**, Fensterladen **Pass**, Postbus **Bounce** (a Reisebus), Kurgast walking **Defeat** (100) / lying **Pass**, Offizier **Defeat** (200, „Abtreten!"), sabre zone **Pass**. The Hackl helps with walkers, Kurgäste and officers, never with cars and buses, and the salute still has to be read.
- **Swim water vs the new kinds** (D-076's exhaustive `sinks_in_swim_water`): Kurgast and Offizier sink like walkers; the Auto ignores tiles and doesn't. Stages 7 and 8 have no swim water; the classification only keeps the match exhaustive.
- **Bad Ischl has no Ortsende.** The journey's destination is not a town Resi leaves: the last board is „Café Zauner / gleich da" (style `street`), the goal area shows the café's façade with a queue (a back prop, decoration only: the M7 boss foreshadowed), and after the tally Resi walks past the queue and off screen. Every other part of the goal sequence (D-029/D-040) is unchanged.
- **Addendum (level-designer, M6): Stage 7 as built** (`content/levels/07_strobl.level`, 280 × 17, side camera; full layout in its header). Deviations from game-design.md "Stage 7", for the game-designer:
  1. **Pension Alpenrose moved 4 columns right and the annex is 9 tiles high (roof on row 6), not 7.** As specified (balcony 51–55, annex from 62, top row 8) the solver skipped the pair three ways: a running jump off the balcony or its right flower box onto the annex roof (the D-094 addendum's risk), a running jump off #2 straight onto the roof (64 px up: a running jump rises 81), and the Z-box top (128) as a perch. Now: a 4-column garden (57–60), the façade 61–71 (`wall_village_bg` rows 9–14 under a hipped `roof_bg` roof, rows 6–8), #2 in (67, 12), #3 in (69, 9), the annex 72–77 with its roof 96 px above #2 (out of reach) and 48 px above #3 (a walking hop), steps 78 (row 9) and 79 (row 12), the promenade from 80 (?-box 84, Selfie-Tourist #2 at 88). From the Z-box top a long running jump still reaches #2: that enters the staircase from above, it doesn't bypass it (accepted). The solver now climbs #2 → #3 → annex.
  2. **The balcony's right stopper is a 3-high pillar** (stone, rows 8–10, col 55), not a 1-high flower box. The Zaunerstollen rises onto the box top and rolls right: it fell onto the 1-high box, rolled off the balcony and away. It now turns at the pillar and rolls back to her (replay `strobl_fensterlaeden`). The left flower box (51) stays 1 high.
  3. **Shutter phases**: #2 `phase` 90, #3 `phase` 150 (the spec's 0/60 shifted by 90, the same 60 apart): the pair is open together at clock 150–239 of every 240 (90 frames). With 0/60 the solver reached #2 at clock ~430, just as it opened, and its jumps there had windows 2 and 5; with any phase from 70 the route meets the overlap and both windows are ≥ 8. #1 (0), #4 (0), #5 (120) as specified.
  4. **Postbus parked at 119, shelter B at 111–114** (spec: 124 and 113–116). Parked at 124 it was 104 px from the checkpoint (132): it triggered at the respawn and a Resi holding LEFT ran into its back. At 119 it is 184 px away and its hitbox ends 10 px left of the respawn screen's edge; walking left to that edge triggers it and it drives away (replay `strobl_checkpoint_respawn`). It halts at 115–118, right of shelter B.
  5. **Terrace 147–154, „Grün blinkt" at 157** (spec 146–153 and 156). Ampel #1 counts only while its cell is fully on screen, i.e. with her centre right of col 152; one column more terrace gives a standing spot of two columns there. Ampel #1 as specified (2 cars, 400, range 12, phase 0).
  6. **Ampel #2: `phase` 250, `range` 15** (spec: phase unset, range 12). Phase 250 starts it in red (150 frames) when her centre passes col 176, so a first approach can cross (the grey box's choice). With range 12 the left half of shelter C (172–175) was out of range: standing there paused the light in red, and stepping off restarted it into the green. Range 15 covers the whole shelter.
  7. **Gelse #1 at (218, row 8)** (spec 215, row 9): at row 9 over the deck's middle it hung in every running arc from deck B to deck C, and the solver stomped it in mid-air (window 3). Above deck C's right end it is clear of the arcs and still starts hunting while she stands on deck C (2400 in `strobl_gelse`; it reaches her there, any hop from ~2455–2490 stomps it).
  8. **E-Biker #1 parked at 244** (spec 248), one column right of the buffer (241–242, 2 wide for the 32 px prellbock): parked at 248 a running Resi always met it riding (the solver stomped it in a two-stomp chain with #2, window 3). At 244 it crashes into the buffer before she can land in its path; a walking Resi watches the crash from the bridge (2808 in `strobl_ebikers`). „Radweg / war Bahn" moved to 239 (the bridge end) so the parked bike doesn't cover the board.
  9. Smaller: the cliff backdrop is a hump inside rows 3–14 (not a box); no house walls beside the junction mouths (the `einfahrt_bg` tiles draw the corners; 162–163 have nothing above them); „Baden heute / verboten" at 190 and „Steg wird / saniert" at 196 (spec 191/195: 4 columns apart); the bust has no collision (a 24 px figure on its 2-high plinth; the plinth is the pen wall), the pigeon sits in (36, 11) with `dy` −8 on its head; 78 Oblaten + 1 in the ?-box.
  - **Solver** (`strobl_solver`): complete at frame 1729, 17 jumps, **narrowest window 8** (the jump from #2 to #3 at frame 423; one above the 5–7 target, kept: the pair is the stage's new mechanic). Named timings: the shutter pair 9 (street → #2, 397) and 8 (#2 → #3, 423), the annex hop ≥ 22; Ampel #2's lane 15 and 21 (1020, 1096; the route crosses in red); the walkway 18 (A → B), 13 (B → C), ≥ 11 (C → D, Gelse #1 hovering; the solver passes before its hunt starts); the bridge and buffer 25, the Ache with E-Biker #2 22 (the route stomps it after the gap). Every counted window ≥ 8; `min_jump_window = 5` holds.
  - **Replays**: `strobl_fensterlaeden` (#1 → balcony → Zaunerstollen, then #2 → #3 → annex), `strobl_shutter_closes`, `strobl_postbus_shutters` (the Gasthof shutters as refuge), `strobl_postbus_shelter`, `strobl_postbus_bounce`, `strobl_ampel_watch`, `strobl_ampel_red`, `strobl_ampel_shelter`, `strobl_car_roofs` (three roof bounces), `strobl_car_side_hit` (Zuckerschock → Normal), `strobl_walkway_fall`, `strobl_gelse`, `strobl_ebikers` (crash, Ache, walk-off 100), `strobl_wurstkranz` (5000), `strobl_checkpoint_respawn` (death in Ampel #2's lane), `strobl_hackl_axes` (title flow from St. Wolfgang: the Hackl carried into Strobl, axes bounce off the halted Postbus and off a car). No shots (goldens are the flow/tour task's).
  - **`time_limit = 240`** provisional (D-045/D-092). The handwritten relaxed-ish run `strobl_ebikers` (waits at the shutters, the Postbus, both Ampeln and both E-Bikers, walking speed) touches the goal at 3419 frames (57 s of clock); QA's relaxed run decides.

## D-098 Route-map labels at Bad Ischl and Café Zauner (game-designer, M6 planning)
- Bad Ischl becomes playable, so the two `LATER` pairs of the map test appear on a real screen, **Strobl → Bad Ischl**, where Resi waits up to 5 s on the Bad Ischl dot (276, 98): her box (x 268–283, y 76–99 Normal, 68–99 big) covers (1) **„Bad Ischl"** in its primary slot above the dot (rows 84–92) and (2) the bottom rows of **„Café Zauner"** (top 69, right edge 290, so rows 69–77) in every form.
- **Fix** (the D-072 pattern, one alternate slot per station and screen, chosen by `label_alt_ends`, no new engine feature):
  1. **Bad Ischl**: `label_alt = { top: 94, right: 266 }`, `label_alt_ends = ["bad_ischl"]`. The side stays `Above`; the right limit pulls the label **left of the dot** (x = 266 − width), level with it (rows 94–102). Its outline ends at x 267, 1 px clear of Resi's box (from 268); it stays clear of the Strobl label (rows 114–122), the Wolfgangsee label (from row 114) and St. Wolfgang's primary slot (rows 80–88). It also takes the label off the map's church spire, which pokes between "Bad" and "Ischl" in the primary slot (M5 art note).
  2. **Café Zauner**: `label_alt = { top: 58, right: 290 }`, `label_alt_ends = ["bad_ischl"]`: raised by 11 px, so its outline ends at row 67, 1 px above big Resi's top (68). St. Wolfgang's raised slot (top 59) is only used on screens ending at Wolfgangsee or Strobl, so the two never meet; on the Strobl → Bad Ischl screen St. Wolfgang is in its primary slot (rows 80–88).
- `LATER` becomes empty (delete it with its use); `NORMAL_TOUCH` keeps only Brunnwinkl's pair. Every other map screen keeps its primary slots, so every existing route-map golden stays bit-identical; the new screens are Strobl → Bad Ischl (Wait) and Bad Ischl → Café Zauner (teaser), re-cut in M6 anyway. On the teaser screen Resi stands on the Café Zauner dot (x 292–307, from y 54 / 46): Café Zauner's primary slot ends at x 290 (outline 291) and Bad Ischl's primary slot ends at row 92, so neither needs an alternate there. The engine's map test checks all of this; exact pixels may move by 1–2 px in the art review if the test still holds.
- **Fallback**: if the left slot clashes with the map art (the Ischl river or the cake), Bad Ischl's alternate goes **below** its dot with a right limit that keeps it clear of the Strobl label (right-aligned to x 266, top 104 only if the test passes; otherwise the art-director moves the dot by a few px, D-043's "dots and labels may move a few px to fit the art").
- **Addendum (engine, M6 task E40): as built.** No fallback taken: `bad_ischl` `label_alt = { top: 94, right: 266 }` and `cafe_zauner` `label_alt = { top: 58, right: 290 }`, both `label_alt_ends = ["bad_ischl"]`, exactly as specified; with the real font both clear Resi on the Bad Ischl dot in every form while the primaries do not (unit test `bad_ischl_and_cafe_zauner_move_on_the_screen_ending_on_bad_ischl`), and the big-Resi rule picks no alternate on any other screen. `LATER` is gone from the map test. No existing route-map golden changed (`verify` goldens pass unchanged); the two new screens get their goldens in the M6 flow work. Also in E40: tiles `felssteg` (one-way), `kies` (solid), `einfahrt_bg` (`Empty`) and the 14 props of the catalog's "Props (group `props`, M6)" table in `PROP_SPRITES` (the catalog test reads it). Until the art lands they draw as before: tiles as grey boxes by kind (`einfahrt_bg`, being `Empty`, draws nothing), props as their cell's grey-box outline. The `options::LEVELS` lines for `07_strobl` and `08_bad_ischl` come with the level files (level-designer).

## D-099 M6 integration: the stage clock in `world_hash`, and how the new kinds meet (engine-engineer, E41)
- **Context**: E36–E40 built the Stage 7/8 kinds in parallel worktrees. E41 checks them together (grey box `00_m6_mix`, cross-task tests in `crates/jnr-core/src/tests/m6_mix.rs`) and the solver's de-duplication key.
- **`world_hash` and the stage clock**: the drill frame of an active officer and every shutter's frame are entity fields and were hashed already, but an officer who is **not active yet** has no drill frame: he starts at (clock + `phase`) when the camera reaches him. Two nodes of one frame bucket can differ in the clock (one of them sat out a power-up or shrink freeze, which stops the clock but not the frame count) while every entity matches, so the solver could merge two states whose officers would later march differently. **Decision**: `Game::world_hash` adds `stage_clock` while an `Offizier` is in the entity list (the flag is gathered in the entity loop it already makes, so no extra cost; a shutter needs no clock, its frame is its hashed `count`, so shutter-only levels such as Strobl are not split needlessly); levels without officers hash exactly as before. Test `a_freeze_moves_the_drill_of_an_officer_not_yet_awake`. Every committed solver replay still matches its fresh route (`m6_shutters` and `m6_offiziere` re-solved: identical), so none was re-written.
- **One clock, one frame apart (kept)**: an officer reads the clock at the start of the frame (`UpdateCtx::stage_clock`), a shutter is placed right after the tick, so in any frame an officer's drill frame is (clock − 1 + `phase`) mod 240 while a shutter's is (clock + `phase`) mod `period`. It is a constant offset, deterministic and the same after every respawn; changing it would move every E39 drill by a frame for nothing. Level designers who line a file up with a shutter: count one frame.
- **How the kinds meet** (each pinned by a test in `tests/m6_mix.rs`): cars drive through shutters and deck chairs (they ignore platforms and enemies) and never turn a walking Kurgast; **a Resi on a deck chair in an Ampel lane is safe from the cars** (her feet 2 px above the 14 px roofs; his rest paused under her), and so is a rider on a shutter in the lane's own row; **a chair is no refuge from a bus** (28 px tall: side hurt); a closing shutter over a lane drops her onto a car's roof (a bounce without damage or points) or onto a chair below (a landing; his rest pauses again); officers turn at a lying or walking Kurgast, at a singer and at a parked bus (all „on foot"), a walking Kurgast turns at an officer, and the file stays in step; a driving Postbus and notes pass a chair; a respawn rebuilds the world exactly as at the stage start (shutters, drill, Ampel, Kurgäste, the parked Postbus, a bumped `M`-box; the clock at 0): a respawned game and a fresh one then run identically. The thrown Hackl: Ampel, shutter (open or closed), lying Kurgast, sabre zone **Pass**; car, Postbus **Bounce**; walking Kurgast (100), officer in every drill state (200) **Defeat**, in play. The lane lint checks tiles only; shutters and chairs in a lane are fine, since cars pass them.
- **Grey box `00_m6_mix`** (150 × 17, Stage 7/8 framing): a shutter over Ampel A's lane, a Kurgast in Ampel B's lane, a pen with a Kurgast and an officer file, Postbus 150 with a shelter, a pen with a singer next to a file. Solver: complete at frame 923, 7 jumps, narrowest window 6 (frame 674, x 1684: the jump over the driving Postbus), 107 199 search deaths, ~2 s; replay `m6_mix_solver`.
- **Tiles** (art-director, Strobl art round 1): `kalkfels_bg` counts a `felssteg` neighbour as the same on all four sides (the deck draws the joined cliff behind itself, like `steig_wall`); `felssteg` joins only `felssteg` (`tiles::one_way_join`, test `kalkfels_bg_joins_a_felssteg_deck_on_every_side_and_the_deck_only_itself`). No golden uses the pair yet.

## D-100 M6 review: Stage 7 as built, difficulty, time limit, HUD band and the balcony stopper (game-designer, M6)
- **Accepted, all nine deviations of the D-097 addendum "Stage 7 as built"**: the Alpenrose at 61–77 with a 9-high annex (the long running jump from the `Z`-box top onto #2 enters the staircase from above and is no bypass, since #3 is still the only step onto the roof); the 3-high stopper at 55; shutter phases #2 = 90 and #3 = 150 (open together at clock 150–239, #3 first); the Postbus parked at 119 with shelter B at 111–114; the terrace at 147–154 and „Grün blinkt" at 157; Ampel #2 at `phase` 250, `range` 15; Gelse #1 at (218, row 8); E-Biker #1 at 244 and „Radweg / war Bahn" at 239; the cliff as a hump, no walls by the junction mouths, signs at 190 and 196, and the bust without collision (a rounded figure suggests no surface, so D-038 holds; a solid bust would make a 4-high wall on the required route). game-design.md "Stage 7" describes the level as built.
- **Condition (Gelse #1)**: the solver passes the walkway before the Gelse hunts, so the walk-on answer is measured by hand (D-085 precedent): a replay `strobl_gelse_outrun` waits on deck C until it hunts, doesn't stomp it, and crosses C → D and D → bridge with it hunting; each of those presses, shifted alone, has a window ≥ 5. Fallback: narrow that gap by one column (deck D from 221, or to 227); the Gelse doesn't move.
- **Difficulty: narrowest window 8 accepted, no tightening.** It is the #2 → #3 jump, the first required use of the shutters, which should stay fair. The step above the Wolfgangsee comes from the lethal walkway and Ache gaps and the two timing systems, which the metric doesn't measure (D-073). Bad Ischl's 4–6 target (D-092) continues the ramp.
- **`time_limit` 240 stays provisional** until QA's relaxed run (D-045, ZEIT clock); likely 200–210. Set once to avoid re-cutting HUD goldens twice.
- **HUD band**: scenery may reach into the HUD band where nothing up there is in play; a surface she must land on is at screen y ≥ 24 at the camera she jumps from (the annex roof: ≥ 27 by camera maths). The art-director confirms with one shot of her on shutter #3.
- **The balcony stopper is a carved wooden corner post with a geranium pot**, not a stone post. Collision unchanged (`stone`, col 55, rows 8–10), drawn exactly over those cells (front prop or solid tile look, whichever needs no code; D-038). The left flower box at 51 gets the geranium look too.
- **Addendum (level-designer, M6 flow task): conditions met.**
  - *Gelse #1*: replay `strobl_gelse_outrun` (handwritten). As `strobl_gelse` to deck C (2370), she waits on its left part; Gelse #1 hunts from 2400; at 2410 she runs right under it while it is still above her head, jumps C → D off the deck's right end (press 2457) and D → bridge right after landing (press 2511), never stomping it (score 8500, no hurt); it is still hunting behind her when she lands on the Klausbrücke (2556). Each press shifted alone (±12, every other input at its frame, judged 2 s after landing as the solver does): **C → D −3..+7 = 11 frames** (earlier she jumps into the Gelse: death; later the unshifted D → bridge press comes too early, a route limit), **D → bridge −7..+12 = ≥ 20** (earlier: short into the gap; +12 is the shift limit). Both ≥ 5: **no gap narrowed**, the fallback was not needed.
  - *Balcony looks*: props `balkon_pfosten` (front, cell (56, 10), `dx` −16, drawn over the stone stopper cells at col 55 rows 8–10) and `balkon_blumenkasten` (front, cell (50, 10), `dx` 16, over the box at 51); collision unchanged, the solver route and every Strobl replay unchanged. Shot `strobl_tour_balkon`.
  - *HUD band*: shot `strobl_tour_shutter3` (tour frame 618, play frame 447): Resi standing on shutter #3 (69, 9), for the art-director.
  - *Sign moves (art-director golden round 1)*: „Grün blinkt / 4×. Danke!" from 157 (inside Ampel #1's lane 138–163: every car covered its face) to **141**; „Stau / seit 1957" from 141 to **109** (Dorfplatz, between the Gasthof door and shelter B: the only spot ≥ 6 columns from its neighbours that is clear of the D-088 Oblaten, the parked Postbus and every car's view). At 141 the board ends at x 2304; a car is drawn only right of the screen's left edge, which is ≥ 2288 once Ampel #1 counts (its cell fully on screen), so at most the board's right 16 px could ever meet a car at the edge; no frame of any replay does. Not taken: the terrace deck (its 4 Oblaten at 149–152 sit behind every board position whose posts stand on the deck, D-088). The Postbus still drives past „Stau" once when it leaves its halt. Signs only: the solver route and every replay are unchanged.

## D-101 M6 review: Stage 8 as built, difficulty trial, time limit, ending (game-designer, M6)
- **Accepted, all seven deviations of the D-096 addendum "Stage 8 Bad Ischl as built"**: Kurgast #1 at 38 with the Katrin station 4 columns left (canopy 30–34, `?`-box 32), since he walks from the first frame; the pigeon on the hat brim at (23, row 11) `dy` −5; the urn at 186 removed, so Kurgast #4 and file B share the pen 174–203 and the file turns at his chair; the lawn wall at 204; the wall steps 216 (2 high) / 217–218 (3 high); singer #2 at 245 with the 2-high bench 249–250 before file C; the Fiaker at 93. game-design.md "Stage 8" describes the level as built.
- **No `dx` for `taube_sitting`**: a pigeon on the Kaiser's hat brim carries the joke; the art-director may shape the hat so the seat looks deliberate.
- **The wall steps are on the required route, with one note hazard**: the Blasmusiker's notes meet her only on the 2-high step 216; one visible hazard for one tile.
- **Difficulty: one bounded trial.** Narrowest 8 (the leap off the `Z`-box on B2 over singer #2 onto the bench). Trial: singer #2 at col 246. If the re-solve's narrowest counted window is 4–7, the leap is not route-limited and every Stage 8 rule holds, it stays; otherwise back to 245 and 8 is accepted (D-073). No second trial.
- **`time_limit` 270 stays provisional** until QA's relaxed run (likely 200–210), set once as for Strobl (D-100).
- **Ending checked against D-097: the level side matches** (no Ortsende, „Café Zauner / gleich da" at 293, façade and queue as back-layer decoration). Owed by the M6 flow/tour task as a condition of acceptance: the golden of the goal sequence at the queue, and a flow replay on the real route (Bad Ischl clear → map walk to Café Zauner with the D-098 labels → „Fortsetzung folgt …" → title).
- **Addendum (level-designer, M6 flow task): the trial and the ending conditions.**
  - *Trial run, reverted*: singer #2 at col 246 (bench and file C unchanged; `levels lint` clean): the solver completes at 1863, 18 jumps, but the **narrowest counted window is 11** (the leap off the `Z`-box over the singer at frame 1535, x 3807, shifts 0..10, fail/fail; also 11 at 1614, x 4005): the singer one column further right gives the leap more room, not less. Outside 4–7, so the singer is back at **245** and **narrowest 8 is accepted** (the leap off the `Z`-box, frame 1510, −1..6 fail/fail), as decided. No dependent replay changed.
  - *Ending*: the goal sequence at the queue is `bad_ischl_tour_zauner_queue` (tour frame 2100: Resi walking past the queue in front of Café Zauner); the flow on the real route is `flow_m6_bad_ischl_teaser_title` (run started in Strobl: Strobl clear → map Wait → Bad Ischl with the run carried over → clear at 4803 → map walk to Café Zauner (shot `map_walk`) → „Fortsetzung folgt …" → title with the run reset). Note for the art review: during the 24-frame map walk Resi crosses the primary „Bad Ischl" and „Café Zauner" labels (she stands clear of both on the teaser itself, as D-098 says).
  - *Board move (art-director golden round 1)*: „Café Zauner / gleich da" from 293 (it hid 4 of the queue tourists and the café window's left columns) to **286**; its cell held the `trafik` prop, now anchored from (287, 15) with `dx` −72 (same pixels; `MAX_PROP_DX_PX` raised to 96 by the engine for this). The board (x 4544–4623) overlaps the Trafik's right 32 px, ends 21 px left of the pole's first opaque pixel (4645) and 48 px left of the queue and the façade (from 4672). Signs and props only: the solver route is unchanged (complete 1851, narrowest 8).

## D-102 M6 QA fixes: sabre zone, chair berth, Ampel light test, B2 stopper, Esplanade kerb, time limits (game-designer, M6 acceptance)
- **Sabre zone = the drawn blade** (supersedes D-096's 8×16 zone size only): `offizier_sabre = [8, 10]`. The 16 px zone hurt 6 px above anything drawn, so a still Resi on a 2-high pen wall beside a halted officer died (`qa_m6_bug_sabre_over_bench`, `qa_m6_bug_sabre_over_fountain_basin`). The zone now ends 32 px above his feet, level with a 2-high wall's top (strict overlap), so the bench 249–250, the basin 67, the Trinkhalle steps 173 and the lawn wall 204 are safe perches; dropping onto or stepping off into a raised sabre still hurts. Level rule: a file is never penned by a 1-high wall where she may stand. Fallbacks: (a) engine, the zone ignores a standing player whose feet are at or above his hitbox top; (b) level, `stone` at (250, 12), (67, 12), (173, 12), (204, 12).
- **Berth at a deck chair** (extends D-095/D-099): `kurgast_berth = 16` px. A walker that turns at enemies (Selfie-Tourist, Nordic-Walker, Pilger, Offizier, walking Kurgast) turns at a lying Kurgast once its hitbox overlaps the chair box widened by 16 px on each side (same rows). It stays ≥ 16 − speed px clear, more than the 11 px a standing rider can hang past the chair (`qa_m6_bug_officer_turns_into_chair_rider`). All other turns keep the plain overlap. Fallbacks: berth 13 px; then a contact rule (a player standing on a chair isn't hurt by a stompable enemy's body or a sabre zone).
- **Ampel on-screen test = the lamp housing** (supersedes D-093's spawner-cell test for the Ampel only): the counter runs while her centre is left of the spawner, within `range`, and a box 16 px wide in the spawner's column, from the cell's bottom − 48 to − 20 px, is fully on screen (`qa_m6_bug_ampel1_stuck_red_camera_raised`). Level rule: refuges ≤ 3 tiles above a lane. The Souvenirstand keeps its cell test. Fallback: a horizontal-only test for the Ampel.
- **B2 stopper**: `stone` at col 237, rows 8–10, replaces the 1-high flower box (`qa_m6_bug_zaunerstollen_rolls_off_b2`); the plain stone tiles read as the balcony's stone end pier. Re-solve and record the narrowest window (gate 4). Fallback: a 2-high stopper (rows 9–10).
- **Esplanade kerb**: `stone` at (151, row 14), directly left of the checkpoint at 152 (`qa_m6_bug_checkpoint_left_into_traun`); the notch 2 landing becomes 147–150. Fallback: kerb at 152, checkpoint at 153.
- **Time limits** (D-045, ZEIT clock): Strobl `time_limit = 230` (`qa_m6_strobl_relaxed` 75.05 s); Bad Ischl `time_limit = 210` (`qa_m6_bad_ischl_relaxed` 69.67 s). Replaces 240/270. Not re-measured after these fixes.
- **Determinism**: Stages 1–6 stay bit-identical. Strobl replays may change only where the camera rested below y 60 within an Ampel's range or where an `expect` depends on ZEIT/time bonus; Bad Ischl replays and its solver replay are re-cut; the `00_m6_*` test levels may change. HUD goldens of both stages are re-approved by the art-director. The five QA bug replays become regressions.
- **Addendum (game-designer, E42): B2 stopper as built.** D-102's 3-high pier left the empty col-236 shaft between the `Z`-box and the pier; the solver's hop from the flower box onto the box top (frame 1485) had a 3-frame window (one frame late she overshot into the shaft), below the gate of 4. The 2-high fallback (top y 144) does not stop the Stollen (it rolls off the y-128 box top over the pier and off the balcony). Built instead: **pier `stone` at col 237, rows 7–10 (4 high, top y 112) and a bridge `stone` at (236, row 8)**, so box and bridge form one flat top 235–236 at y 128 with the pier one tile above it. The Stollen turns at the pier's row-7 face and rolls back to her (`qa_m6_zaunerstollen_stays_on_b2` passes); the solver completes at 1851, **narrowest window 5** (frame 1448; then 7 at 1490), inside D-092's 4–6 target. The Oblate at (236, row 10) stays, under the bridge. Big Resi can still leave B2 over the pier (a 16 px hop from the box top) or by dropping to the street. Not taken: moving the `Z`-box to 234 (re-cuts the QA bump replay, Stollen untested); a step at (236, row 10) (window still 3).

## D-103 Bad Ischl: a street-level camera top clamp for the B2 leap; M6 art notes (game-designer, M6 acceptance)
- **Problem** (art-director, M6 acceptance, solver frames ~1490–1596): on the leap from B2 over singer #2 onto the bench, the street, the singer and file C are below the screen until ~12 frames before she lands; the bench and the singer's body appear only at 1584. Players will copy the move, so it is the M5 hidden-hazard pattern.
- **Diagnosis** (corrects the review's wording): Bad Ischl is a side level, and side levels have **no high-ground rest**. D-086/D-090's rest and top clamp apply only to `camera = "autoscroll"` levels (`Game::follow_camera`: side levels call the plain dead-zone `Camera::follow`). The lift comes from the side dead zone: her centre above screen y 64 (`deadzone_top`) lifts the camera at once, and it comes down only when her centre passes screen y 120. B2 is 7 rows above the street (box top and bridge y 128, pier top 112, street top 240), so the frame is too short to show both. Standing on the pier (Normal: centre 101) the dead zone allows at most camera y 37 (Zuckerschock/Hackl 33). Singer #2's hitbox (level y 218–240) is then at screen ≥ 181, fully off; the bench top (208) is at 171. On the box top it is at most 53/49, and any hop up from the flower box lifts it further. The solver's 12-frame hop at 1490 peaks with her feet near y 110 and lands on the pier at ~1512; its 20-frame leap at 1520 peaks near y 51 and takes the camera to ~0. A held hop by a human does the same. So no take-off from B2's high ground keeps the hazards in view under the plain dead zone, whatever the solver does.
- **Rule: a per-level camera top clamp for side levels.** New optional level key `[meta] camera_top = <level px>`. In a side level that sets it, the camera's y is never above it in any phase: after the dead zone every frame of play, at `Camera::new` (start, respawn, stage change, title framing) and in the goal sequence's `follow_y`. This is the side-level twin of D-090's `autoscroll_top`. Validation: only with `camera = "side"` (climb and auto-scroll levels reject it), `0 ≤ camera_top ≤ level_h − 180`. **Bad Ischl sets `camera_top = 76`**: the street's top (row 15, y 240) is at screen y ≤ 164, so at least 16 px of street always show, and every street-level enemy's hitbox and sprite (feet on y 240) is fully on screen in every frame. Where the clamp wins over the dead zone, her top may leave the screen (D-090: "a higher Resi may leave the top of the screen, as in SMB3").
- **What it changes in Bad Ischl**: only frames where the dead zone would put y < 76, i.e. her centre above level y 140 (feet above ~151 as Normal, ~155 big). These are standing on or jumping from the `Z`-box top, the bridge and the pier (B2), jumps off B1/B2 and the Katrin canopy, standing on the bandstand roof (feet 144: her top at screen 46), jumps off the Trafik roof (the Wurstkranz jump), and high stomp bounces. Nothing in play lies above level y 112: rows 0–6 are empty, and the pier top shows at screen 36. The one exception is singer #1's high C, which rises from B1 into the sky; it stays on screen from its base up to level y 76, and anything above that can only touch a Resi who is herself above the screen. The row-8 boxes and Oblaten show at ≥ 52, and a Zaunerstollen rising from the `Z`-box at ≥ 36 (D-090 box rule: 128 − 76 − 12 = 40 ≥ 22). Every surface she must land on is at screen ≥ 24 (D-100 HUD band rule: box top 52, bandstand roof 68). At the leap: at take-off from the pier, singer #2's hitbox is at screen 142–164, the bench top at 132 and file C (sprites from y 208) at ≥ 132, all on screen for the whole leap. The cost: her own sprite is above the screen top while her feet are above level y 76. On the solver's leap that is ~30–35 frames around the apex (art-director, measured on the E43 render: 1524–1556), and on the pier her head is at screen 14 (big: 6), inside the HUD band. Accepted as in D-090: her x is the camera's follow point and the landing is in view. Bad Ischl has no camera-dependent trigger (no Gelse, Schwan, Regattaboot, Ampel or Souvenirstand; activation and despawn are horizontal), so the game state changes only in the camera's y. The route, events and windows are expected to stay the same.
- **Horizontal note** (not changed): the screen shows her centre + 176 px ahead (`follow_x` 144), so at a take-off from the pier's right end (centre ≤ x 3813) the bench's left edge (x 3984) is at the right screen edge. It comes into view within the first ~6 frames of the leap, ≥ 70 frames before she lands; singer #2 (x 3916–3932) is in view at take-off. The acceptance check is therefore: **in the re-render of the Bad Ischl solver at 1500–1600 every 6 frames, singer #2's hitbox and the bench row (top at screen 132) are on screen at her take-off frame, and the bench's left edge by take-off + 6**.
- **Stages 1–7 are untouched by construction**: no stage but Bad Ischl sets the key, an absent key changes no code path's result, and the key is part of a level's static hash only when present. So every Stage 1–7 replay and golden and the testbed's pinned `FINISH_HASH` stay bit-identical. The sticky side dead zone exists there too (backlog: the M1 art note on Salzburg solver frames 1440–1500, and art note b below), but nothing there is a required leap from high ground onto street hazards. A general side-camera ground settle stays a backlog candidate for a milestone that re-cuts those stages anyway.
- **Level unchanged otherwise**: B2 keeps the E42 pier and bridge, so `qa_m6_zaunerstollen_stays_on_b2` and the gate (narrowest 5 ≥ 4) are unaffected. Re-run `solve bad_ischl`; if the output differs (the camera is in the state, not in the route), re-write the solver replay and record the narrowest window, which must stay ≥ 4.
- **Touched**: `08_bad_ischl.level` `[meta]` (`camera_top = 76`; level-designer); engine: the key, its validation and the clamp (engine-engineer). Every Bad Ischl replay must pass with identical events and completion frames; only replays that pin a state hash are re-cut, and so are the flow replays through Bad Ischl (`flow_m6_*`) if they pin one. Goldens: the Bad Ischl and Bad Ischl flow shots whose camera was above y 76 are re-rendered and re-approved by the art-director (B2 and the pier, the bandstand roof, the Wurstkranz jump, high bounces; the engine task lists them). Every other golden is unchanged.
- **Tests**: unit tests: the clamp holds after `follow`, `new` and `follow_y`; it doesn't exist without the key; validation rejects it on climb and auto-scroll levels and above the bottom clamp. Game test `bad_ischl_street_stays_on_screen`: on every frame of `bad_ischl_solver`, `bad_ischl_tour` and `qa_m6_bad_ischl_relaxed`, camera y ≥ 76; at the solver's leap take-off, singer #2's hitbox is fully on screen vertically and the bench top is at screen y ≤ 164. Then the art-director's re-render above.
- **Not taken**: (1) limiting or skipping a "high-ground rest" in side levels, because there is none. (2) A D-086-style ground settle for side levels: at best it brings y back to 53 on the box top and never past 37 on the pier, so the singer stays hidden from the pier; applied to all side levels it would change every Stage 1–3, 5 and 7 replay in which the camera was ever lifted. (3) Level fallbacks. A ceiling over the pier, e.g. `stone` at (237, row 5) with a 16 px gap, makes it unstandable, but the leap must then clear y 80, a higher apex that lifts the camera more, with a gate risk. The 2- and 3-high piers failed the Stollen or the gate (E42). A take-off from the box top still hides the singer after a held hop. (4) Bad Ischl as a climb level: the climb camera frames her feet at screen 148, so on B2 the street drops even further out of view.
- **Fallback**: if the art review finds her time above the screen top on the leap too long, use `camera_top = 60`. That is the lowest value at which a street-level enemy's hitbox and sprite are fully on screen (240 − 180); the street's own top row is then just below the screen, and she is off the top for only a few frames on the leap.
- **Art-director notes (non-blocking), decided**:
  - (a) **Deck chairs in front of boards: accepted as is.** Kurgast #2's chair can park in front of „Himmel / auf Erden" (73) or „Grundriss: E / wie Sisi" (80), and Kurgast #4's over „Solebad / macht Kaiser" (176). A chair stands only where she stomped him. Each board is read on the way in, before she reaches a Kurgast who walks at 0.375 px/frame, and it is free again after ≤ 340 frames. A spa guest parking his deck chair in front of the imperial signage is the Kurgast joke itself. Moving „Grundriss" onto the avenue 88–99 meets the parked Fiaker (93) and the row-13 Oblaten at 91–92, 97–98 and 101–104 (the D-088 board clearance): a level change and a re-solve for a cosmetic overlap.
  - (b) **Strobl, frames ~528–552, her head above the screen top off the Alpenrose roof: accepted.** The camera is at the level-top clamp, nothing in play is hidden, and D-090's "a higher Resi may leave the top of the screen" applies. Strobl gets no `camera_top`, so Stage 7 stays bit-identical.
  - (c) **Strobl Gelse #1 contrast: backlog.** The `gelse` sprite is shared with Stage 6, so a 1 px wing-root highlight re-renders Wolfgangsee goldens, which M6 keeps bit-identical. It reads (weakest, not failing: the bob and the wings carry it). Take it in the next art pass that re-approves Stage 6 goldens anyway.
  - (d) **„Zimmer frei / ab 7 Nächte" → „Zimmer frei / ab 7 Nächten"** (`stage.strobl.sign_zimmer`). After „ab" with a number standard German takes the dative, and the project writes standard German with Austrian seasoning (german-satire-writing skill). The joke is the minimum stay, not the grammar. 12 characters (the sign limit; the board is a fixed 80 px). Render only: the Strobl goldens that show the board (col 47) are re-rendered and re-approved; no replay changes.
- **As built (engine, E43)**: `LevelMeta::camera_top: Option<i32>`, validated in `Level::parse` (`validate_camera_top`: "only allowed with camera = \"side\"", "need 0 <= camera_top <= level_h - 180 = N"). `Camera::clamp_top(top, level_h)` (y ≥ top, or the bottom clamp if that is higher) is the shared body of D-090's `clamp_autoscroll_top` and the new clamp; `Game::clamp_camera_top` applies it after the side camera's `follow` every frame of play, after `Camera::new` in `place_camera` (start, respawn, stage change; the side title framing is `place_camera`'s) and after the goal sequence's `follow_y`. `level_parts` hashes `"camera_top"` and the value into the static hash only when the key is present: every replay of Stages 1–7, the testbed's `FINISH_HASH` and every Stage 1–7 golden pass unchanged. Checked before committing: on every Bad Ischl replay (the flow replays through it included) the events, her hitbox and the camera's x are identical with and without the key on every frame; only the camera's y differs (0–1447 frames per replay). `solve bad_ischl` gives the same route (complete at 1851, 17 jumps, narrowest window 5 at frame 1448), so `bad_ischl_solver` and the replays that copy it are unchanged and nothing was re-cut. The solver's leap leaves the pier at frame 1520 (last grounded 1519) with the camera at (3663, 76): singer #2's hitbox (x 3922–3934, y 218–240) at screen (259–271, 142–164), the bench top at screen 132; the bench's left edge (x 3984) is in view from 1521 (camera x 3666). Tests: `level::camera_top_is_side_only_and_at_most_the_bottom_clamp`, `camera::side_camera_top_clamp_holds_after_new_follow_and_follow_y`, `tests::bad_ischl::camera_top_clamps_the_start_and_every_frame_and_nothing_else` (a small side level with and without the key: identical player, camera y = plain y clamped, the start clamped) and `tests::bad_ischl::bad_ischl_street_stays_on_screen` (camera y ≥ 76 on every frame of `bad_ischl_solver`, `bad_ischl_tour`, `qa_m6_bad_ischl_relaxed` and, for a respawn, `qa_m6_offiziere_drill_after_respawn`; the take-off checks above). Goldens: only `bad_ischl_tour_praesentiert` (frame 1817, her jump over the blocks by „Zauner: anstellen!“: the old frame had the camera above 76 and the street at the screen's bottom edge) and `strobl_tour_balkon` (the „Zimmer frei / ab 7 Nächten" board, note d) change; no other Bad Ischl or flow shot had the camera above 76.

## D-104 M7 scope: Stage 9 Café Zauner, a short room with a boss, and the ending (game-designer, M7 planning)
- M7 is **one short stage, one boss and the ending**: `09_cafe_zauner` (140 × 17, about half a normal stage), the Oberkellner (D-106), the finale and the ending (D-107). New kinds are few and each extends a system that has been through QA. The **Kellner** (D-105) is a Nordic-Walker with the Reisebus's contact rule. The **Wartende** are the still Selfie-Tourist (D-026) under a real-level name. The **Oberkellner** is a state machine built from the Fiaker's telegraph and charge, the Reisebus's exact stop, the officer's state-dependent contact (D-096) and the Steinschlag's falling hazard (his bills). The `goal_stollen` is a goal kind like `goal_maibaum`. Everything else is reuse (Kurgast, Selfie-Tourist, boxes, checkpoint, one-way tiles, signs).
- **Shape: a side level inside the café with a fixed vertical camera.** The journey table's "short final stage inside the café" is taken as written: the café is one long room (Pfarrgasse door → cloakroom → salon → the queue → the counter), so the level is a side level with `camera_top = 92` (D-103's key) equal to the bottom clamp of a 17-row level. The camera never moves vertically, the ceiling (rows 0–6, solid) shows only in the HUD band, and the floor sits at the usual street framing (screen 148). Reasons: a room has a ceiling, so a fixed frame is the natural SMB castle look; the boss arena must show everything at once; and the D-103 lesson (a lifted side camera hides the floor) cannot happen. Not taken: a climb or auto-scroll café (no reason in the setting); 12 rows (every side-level convention, the HUD band rule and the goal framing assume 17); the café's two floors (a vertical café needs stairs, the Zauner salon is one floor).
- **Difficulty: a celebration with a climax.** No pits and no lethal ground (a café floor doesn't kill); `min_jump_window = 5`, one step easier than Bad Ischl's 4, design target narrowest 6–9 in sections 1–4; one moving hazard per required jump, two per pen over solid ground; an upper route (chandeliers) beside every enemy group. The climax is the boss: fair, telegraphed, one screen, beatable as Normal without a hurt, with each required press measured by hand ≥ 8 (D-106). The full table is in game-design.md "Difficulty and reach rules (stage 9)".
- **Numbers**: `time_limit` per D-045 by the ZEIT clock, provisional 200 (relaxed ~55–65 s with the fight). Oblaten 50 ± 10. `M`-boxes at 14 and 117 (5 columns after the checkpoint at 112, right before the arena). **No `Z`-box**: the only Zaunerstollen in the finale is the one Resi earns. **No `H`-box** (the D-081/D-097 pattern: the legend puts the Hackl at the Falkenstein and St. Wolfgang); a Hackl carried in keeps working (D-105/D-106 classify every new kind).
- **Running gags, last round**: the Bus 150 chain ends with Resi no longer caring („Bus 150? / Wurscht!" at the door) and pays off in the ending scene (Postbus 150 drives past the café window, full: „Voll. Ist Resi wurscht."). The checkpoint joke is a reserved card, „Reserviert / seit 1832". The queue foreshadowed in Bad Ischl (D-097) becomes the stage's set piece and the boss's motive: Resi jumps the queue, and the Oberkellner is the man who says „Hinten anstellen!".
- **Satire limits**: the targets are queue culture at famous cafés, cake photos for social media, the waiter clichés („Komm' gleich!", the dignified Herr Ober, the notepad, „Zahlen, bitte!"), reserved tables, the coffee-house afternoon over one Melange, k.u.k. nostalgia, and Resi herself as the queue-jumper. Café Zauner is honoured, never mocked: its real history is the stage's material (1832, the court confectioner, Nickerl's broken Oblaten, the recipe in the safe). No claim about its prices, service or Wi-Fi; no living person (owners, staff) by name or likeness. The Oberkellner is an anonymous archetype and wins the last word with grace („Bitte sehr!"). The bills he throws are a slapstick on „Zahlen, bitte!", not on the café's prices.
- **Not in M7** (backlog): riding a waiter's tray as a moving platform (an enemy-as-platform that moves needs `carry`, D-095 addendum); the Café Esplanade Zauner on the Traun (1927) as a terrace bonus; a second boss form (the queue itself moving forward, „die Schlange rückt vor"); a Melange or Gugelhupf power-up; the emperor's Gugelhupf and Katharina Schratt; save games and a stage select after the first clear; a hard mode; music for the ending (MA).
- **Tooling taken on** (backlog "Full contact sheet too tall", due since M5): `cargo xtask sprites sheet` pages the full contact sheet (E44), because M7's acceptance needs the art-director's full-sheet review and the sheet grows again.
- **Fallback for the scope** (the D-066/D-092 pattern): if the milestone runs long, cut in this order: the ending's Map phase and the credits' sprites (D-107), the boss's volley (D-106: dash and breather only), the Kellner's popup (D-105). The stage, the boss's dash/breather fight, the `goal_stollen`, the Bill, the text credits and the title mark ship in every case.
- **Addendum (engine, M7 task E48): as built.** Tiles `marmor`, `marmor_punkt`, `stuck`, `vitrine`, `theke` (solid) and `luster` (one-way) in `TileKind::from_tile_name`; until the art lands they draw as grey boxes by kind (test `cafe_tiles_draw_as_grey_boxes_by_kind_until_the_art_lands`). The four props of the catalog's "Props (group `props`, M7)" table in `PROP_SPRITES` (the catalog test reads that table too). `stollen_glocke` is **not** a prop: the renderer draws the bell from the `goal_stollen`'s state (E47), so it stays out of `PROP_SPRITES`. Background: an opaque full-height first layer already replaced the flat sky at any scroll factor; E48 pins it for factor 16 (`bg_zauner_salon`, tests `an_opaque_full_height_layer_at_factor_16_covers_every_column` and `an_opaque_salon_wall_at_factor_16_replaces_the_flat_sky_in_a_cafe_level`) and tightens `background::covers_screen` to layers fixed to the screen bottom (`y` 0 and `factor_y` 0), since a raised or sinking layer leaves a strip uncovered. Every Stage 1–8 first layer is `bg_sky_salzburg` at `y` 0, `factor_y` 0, so nothing there changes. The `options::LEVELS` line for `09_cafe_zauner` comes with the level file (level-designer).

- **Addendum (art-director, M7 café art, round 1–2): exceptions.** (1) The `stuck` ceiling tile is solid but uses a cream `c` fill, which art-style §8 otherwise keeps for collectibles. Accepted as an exception: it is the out-of-reach ceiling band behind the HUD, no Oblate is ever placed in rows 0–7 of the café, and it matches `bg_zauner_salon`'s ceiling. (2) `bg_zauner_salon`'s chandelier chains are fixed to the planned chandelier columns (x mod 640 = 8, 56, 168, 280, 584), so the level keeps L1–L6 on those columns or the background follows. (3) The floor dots join the marble floor through the renderer alias `marmor_punkt` → `marmor` (E49).

- **As built (E49)**: the alias is in `tiles::autotile_family` (test `marmor_punkt_joins_the_marmor_floor`); no golden used the dots yet. **Design gap (props on the counter)**: a prop's legend entry may carry a `tile` only if it does not collide (`level.rs` `entity_tile`), so the catalog's "`kuchentheke` … place at col 138, row 14" (a cell of the 3-high counter) does not parse. Place it on the floor cell left of the counter with a `dx` instead (Stage 9: col 136, row 14, `dx = 32` centres it on 138; the grey box `00_m7_cafe` does this); the drawn result is the same (bottom centre on the floor line, column 138).

- **Addendum (level-designer, M7 level task): Stage 9 Café Zauner as built, difficulty and named timings** (D-073 style; `content/levels/09_cafe_zauner.level`, its header has the column list). Sections, the arena, the enemy set (Kellner 2, Wartende 9, Kurgast 1, Selfie-Tourist 1, Oberkellner 1), the chandeliers L1–L6 (on the background's chain columns), the signs and the `options::LEVELS` line are as specified; `levels lint` clean (arena lint, D-088, signs ≥ 6 apart, rows ≥ 8, the box rule).
  - *Deviations, for the game-designer*: (1) **`M`-box (115, row 11)** instead of 117 (E46's finding: at 117 a big Resi had a 2 px band onto the step; at 116 the Mozartkugel fell off the box onto the step and turned at the arena's vitrine; at 115 it lands at 117 and turns at the step's face, replay `cafe_zauner_mozartkugel_step`). After a respawn at 112 it is 3 columns away. (2) **Boxes clear of the back wall's mirrors and window** (art-director note: keep Oblatenkisten off the mirror frame's bottom bar; the frame's bar is at screen 99–101, a row-11 box's bottom at 100): `M`-box 12 and `?`-box 17 (spec 14 / 16, both in front of the first mirror, bg x 215–264; 12 and 17 keep 7 px clear of its frame), `?`-box 52 (spec 53, second mirror's frame), `?`-box 108 (spec 105, in front of the second window's sill). (3) The entrance's **5 Oblaten on row 12 at 7–11** (spec row 13 at 6–10: they sat on the bus board and on „Sie werden / platziert", D-088). (4) **„k.u.k. Hof- / zuckerbäcker" at 21** (spec 24: the `garderobe` prop takes that cell; the board stands beside the coat stand). (5) The `kuchentheke` prop is anchored at cell (136, 14) with `dx` 32 (its catalog cell (138, 14) is the solid counter); `luster_deko` at row 8 on the free chain columns 10, 36, 50, 123, 130 (`dx` 8). (6) **Oblaten 46 placed + 3 in `?`-boxes = 49** (target 50 ± 10). `time_limit` 200 stays provisional (D-045).
  - *Solver (E46 ranking, with the fight)*: complete **2283**, 15 jumps, **narrowest counted window 18** (frame 288, x 719: from pen B over Vitrine B onto the `?`-box top at 52; and 392, x 979: from pen C over Vitrine C onto Vitrine D); every other counted window 25 or route-bounded. `min_jump_window = 5` holds. The 6–9 design target for sections 1–4 is **not reached by the counted windows** (they are 18 and up): the solver's tight moments are bounces (no `Jumped` event), so the metric does not count them (D-073: the list belongs to the route). Measured by hand (D-100 shift, every other input at its frame), the queue is where the stage is tight:
  - *Named timings*: **Kellner #1 at the vitrine drop**: he walks from the first frame (active from the start), reaches Vitrine 18–19's face (x 320) at frame ~128 and turns; the solver drops off the vitrine onto his tray at 129 („Komm' gleich!"); a walker arriving later finds him pacing the 12-tile pen (period ~360 frames) with the hat shelf above. **The queue hops**: (a) the solver bounces over six heads at run speed (73, 76, 79, 85, 91, 97; stomps 466–622), the release windows of its held JUMP 7 / 12 / 13 / 21; (b) the full chain at walk speed (`cafe_zauner_queue_chain`, 3 columns apart: 100 … 8000 and „+1 Leben" at 773) has release windows 7 / 7 / 6 / 6 / 4 / 21 / 21 / 21 (±20) and the jump onto the first head 7: a skill line for a 1-up, not required; (c) hopping each Wartender on foot from a standstill lands in the 32 px gaps with the walk slide (windows 1–4 by the press shift, i.e. a matter of hold and run-up, not of timing: precision work); (d) **the chandelier route** (Vitrine D → L3 → L4 → L5 → L6, 4-tile gaps with the ceiling 3 tiles above the bands) needs a run across the 3-wide chandelier and a jump at its edge (inside the coyote frames): **3–4 frames** per hop (L3 from Vitrine D 4). The chandeliers are optional by the rules, but the spec offers them as the bypass of the whole queue; with the chain columns fixed by the background (D-104 art exception 2) the gaps can't shrink without the art.
